# Lead Management Development Progress

This document is the authoritative handoff state for ongoing Lead Management enhancement work in this repository. It exists so a new Claude CLI session (or a new engineer) can pick up the work tomorrow without needing this conversation's history.

## 1. Development Workflow / Rules

Every Lead Management enhancement — no exceptions — follows this process:

1. **Inspect** — read the actually-relevant models, migrations, controllers, services, routes, policies/permissions, jobs/events/listeners, notifications, frontend components, existing tests, and DB relationships for the feature at hand.
2. **Impact Analysis** — explicitly reason about what existing behavior the change could touch.
3. **Architecture Proposal** — explain the planned approach (data model, triggers, UI, permissions, risks) before writing any code.
4. **Wait for User Approval** — do not proceed past the proposal until the user explicitly approves it. Open decision points get asked as explicit questions, not silently assumed.
5. **Implementation**
6. **Testing** — comprehensive tests for the new feature, covering success paths, edge cases, permission boundaries, and tenant isolation.
7. **Regression Verification** — Pint, PHPStan, the new feature's own tests, the full `tests/Feature/Leads/` suite, and the full application suite. Confirm no pre-existing behavior changed.

**Never skip directly to implementation for a new feature.**

Core principles:

- Preserve all existing functionality. Additive-only wherever possible.
- Implement one feature at a time — do not bundle multiple roadmap items into one change.
- Avoid scope creep. Build exactly what was approved, nothing more.
- Do not modify unrelated code, routes, permissions, or UI.
- Proactively identify risks and architectural conflicts before they become bugs — surface them in the proposal, not after implementation.
- Do not reopen a completed/approved feature unless a real blocker or regression is discovered by a later feature. A completed feature's own backlog items (see §7) are deferred, not reopened, until the user asks for them.

## 2. Current Lead Management Baseline

The Lead Management module (pre-existing, before Features #1/#2 in this document) is a **Blade/Livewire** module — no React, no separate frontend framework — built on this app's Tenant/TenantUser/Role/Module/Permission stack, ported from a sibling project (`OpticoreLeadManagementHub`).

Implemented baseline capabilities:

- **Multi-channel lead capture**: Facebook Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp, Facebook Lead Ads, Website Form, Email (IMAP polling via `leads:fetch-emails`), LinkedIn Lead Gen (Phase 1, manual token entry). All via `App\Http\Controllers\Api\LeadWebhookController::createLead()` (5 call sites) or `App\Jobs\ProcessEmailLeadSource`. Each fires `App\Events\LeadReceived` exactly once, on creation.
- **Manual lead creation**: `App\Actions\Tenant\CreateManualLead` — lazily provisions a per-tenant "Custom" `LeadSource`. Does **not** fire `LeadReceived` (a pre-existing, known gap — see §5's manual-lead scoring workaround for how Feature #2 handled this without fixing it).
- **Status pipeline**: `App\Models\LeadStatus` — Platform-Admin-managed shared catalog, attached per-tenant via a pivot with per-tenant `position` ordering; has an `is_closed` flag. Full history in `LeadStatusLog`.
- **Kanban**: `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Index` renders either the table (Client Admin) or the Kanban board (Client User) from the same `filteredQuery()`/`FiltersLeadsQuery` concern.
- **Lead assignment/distribution**: `App\Services\LeadAssignmentService` — manual assign/unassign, plus automatic round-robin / least-loaded / random distribution, driven by `Tenant::lead_distribution_mode`/`auto_distribution_method` (configured Platform-Admin-side). Full history in `LeadAssignment`.
- **Notes**: `App\Models\LeadNote`, freeform, tied to a `TenantUser`.
- **Attachments**: `App\Models\LeadAttachment`, downloaded async via `App\Jobs\DownloadLeadAttachment`, stored on the private `local` disk.
- **Activity timeline**: `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Show::activity()` aggregates `LeadStatusLog`, `LeadAssignment`, `LeadNote`, `LeadDetailUpdateLog` (+ now `LeadDuplicateLink` merge events, +Feature #1) into one chronological feed.
- **Meetings/follow-ups**: `meeting_date`/`meeting_time` (time is record-only, never drives the alert), `next_follow_up_at`, live non-persisted Kanban-card shimmer alerts (`meetingAlertActive()`/`followUpAlertActive()`), tenant-configurable enable + days-before threshold, daily `leads:send-alert-notifications` sweep persists bell notifications.
- **Notifications**: `NewLeadNotification`, `LeadAssignedNotification`, `MeetingUpdatedNotification`, `MeetingCancelledNotification`, `MeetingAlertNotification`, `FollowUpUpdatedNotification`, `FollowUpCancelledNotification`, `FollowUpAlertNotification` — all real-time via Reverb through `App\Listeners\BroadcastUserNotification` on Laravel's internal `NotificationSent` event.
- **Multi-currency budget/deal fields**: `estimated_budget`/`estimated_budget_currency_id`, `deal_value`/`deal_value_currency_id`, `win_probability` — `App\Models\Currency` is a Platform-Admin-managed shared catalog, tenants opt in via `tenant_currencies`.
- **Excel export**: `App\Exports\FacebookLeadAdsExport` (`maatwebsite/excel`) — currently the only export, mirrors the Facebook Lead Ads spreadsheet view's dynamic per-form-field columns.
- **Permissions**: `App\Policies\LeadPolicy` (`viewAny`/`create`/`view`/`update`/`delete`/`assign`, +`merge` from Feature #1). `PermissionSlug` is a **fixed, global 4-value enum** (`View`/`Create`/`Update`/`Delete`) — not extensible per module without a much larger cross-cutting change. Every new capability added by Features #1/#2 reuses the existing `lead-management` module's `update` permission + `isClientAdmin()`, never a new slug.
- **Tenant isolation**: `App\Concerns\BelongsToTenant` adds a global scope, but it **only activates when `app()->bound('currentTenant')`** — which is **not** the case in public webhook/job/command contexts. Every new query in Features #1/#2 filters `tenant_id` explicitly rather than relying on the trait alone. This is the single most important correctness rule for any future Lead Management feature touching cross-lead queries.
- **Architecture discovered during inspection** (load-bearing, apply to any future feature):
  - `Lead::identifier` is monotonic and never reclaimed (`{tenant.lead_id_prefix}-LEAD-{n}`, generated in `Lead::booted()`'s `creating` hook) — never mass-assignable, never reused even after deletion/merge.
  - A background/event-driven write to a `Lead` column must use a **raw query builder update** (`DB::table('leads')->where(...)->update(...)`), not `$lead->save()`, when it must not touch `updated_at` — otherwise it spuriously trips `Lead::hasUnseenUpdate()`'s "recently updated" highlight for every viewer. Established by `Lead::markUpdateSeen()`, reused by Feature #1's merge (loser update) and Feature #2's score writes.
  - `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Index` excludes Facebook Lead Ads leads from the Client Admin's table (aggregated into `SourceLeads` instead) but shows them as normal Kanban cards for Client Users.
  - No reporting/dashboard exists anywhere for leads — `App\Livewire\Tenant\Dashboard` shows only tenant/module/user/role counts, zero lead data. The only existing aggregation is `Lead::countsByStatus()` for Kanban column headers.
  - No `EventServiceProvider` exists — Laravel 11+ auto-discovers listeners from their `handle()` method's type-hinted event parameter.
  - `Auth::user()` is typed `PlatformUser|TenantUser|null` by Larastan throughout the whole app (a pre-existing, accepted, unfixed limitation — confirmed identical in pre-existing files like `Roles/Index.php`) — don't try to "fix" this pattern in new code; it's baseline noise, not a real bug.

## 4. Feature #1 — Duplicate Detection & Merge

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- Cross-channel duplicate detection by normalized email/phone match, same-tenant only — `App\Services\LeadDuplicateDetectionService`. Writes/reuses `App\Models\LeadDuplicateLink` rows (`pending`/`dismissed`/`merged`), canonically ordered by ULID pair (`LeadDuplicateLink::pairKeys()`) so a pair is never re-flagged after a dismissal.
- **Merge behavior**: `App\Services\LeadMergeService::merge()` reparents the loser's child records (notes, attachments, status logs, assignments, relevant `LeadDetailUpdateLog` entries) onto the survivor, then marks the loser `merged_into_lead_id` — **no hard delete, no identifier reuse**. The loser stays fully queryable but is excluded from normal list views (`Index::filteredQuery()`, `SourceLeads::baseQuery()`); opening the loser's own detail page redirects to the survivor.
- The whole merge runs inside **one `DB::transaction()`** — verified with a real mid-transaction FK-failure test proving full rollback (no partial state).
- **Conflict values require explicit selection** — `LeadMergeService::conflictingFields()` only flags fields where the loser has a real, differing, non-empty value; the default resolution is always "keep survivor" unless the admin explicitly picks "use loser's value." Fields not in the mergeable allow-list (pipeline/relationship columns: `status_id`, `assigned_to`, `source_id`, `priority`, dates, etc.) are **never** touched by a merge.
- Audit trail: `LeadDuplicateLink` stores `resolved_by`, `resolved_at`, `status = merged`, and `field_resolutions` (a snapshot of every conflicting field's chosen side plus both leads' values at merge time).
- **Duplicate badge is detail-page only** — never on the leads table or Kanban card (a deliberate scope decision, not a limitation to "fix").
- Permissions: `LeadPolicy::merge()` reuses `update` + `isClientAdmin()` — no new permission slug. This became the template every later capability (including Feature #2) copies.

**Explicit backlog, not a current blocker** (see also `.claude`/memory: `project_lead_merge_backlog.md`):
- Duplicate-detection query performance — `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor()` scans all of a tenant's contactable leads in PHP; confirmed via `EXPLAIN` against real Postgres that the `(tenant_id, email)`/`(tenant_id, phone)` indexes aren't effectively used for this. Future fix: Postgres functional/expression indexes + SQL-level normalized matching.
- Merge budget/currency pairing — `estimated_budget`/`estimated_budget_currency_id` (and the `deal_value` pair) are resolved as fully independent choices in the merge UI, allowing a mismatched amount+currency. Future fix: pair them into one selection.
- **Intentional, documented UX trade-off (not a bug)**: when the survivor's field is empty and the loser has a value, the conflict still defaults to "keep survivor" (stays empty) rather than auto-filling — uniform, non-destructive-by-default behavior, at the cost of requiring the admin to notice and act.

**Do not modify Feature #1 unless a future feature causes a real regression.**

## 5. Feature #2 — Lead Scoring Phase 2a

Final approved implementation:

- Score is a **nullable integer, 0–100**. No active rules for the tenant → `lead_score = NULL` ("Not scored"). At least one active rule → always a real, deterministic 0–100 integer (including a legitimate computed 0).
- Rules carry **signed integer points** (no per-rule min/max) — the final total is hard-clamped to [0, 100], not scaled/normalized (scaling would make unrelated leads' scores drift whenever a rule is added elsewhere, which breaks explainability).
- **Raw total (pre-clamp) is preserved** in `LeadScoreLog.raw_total`, alongside the clamped `score`.
- **Every recomputation is from scratch**: current Lead state + current tenant's active rules + current time — `LeadScoringService::computeFor()` never increments/decrements the previously stored score. Verified deterministic (same inputs → same output across repeated calls) and non-accumulating.
- A `LeadScoreLog` row is written **only when `(score, breakdown)` actually differs** from the lead's last log — trigger reason is metadata, not part of that comparison. This keeps the log table's growth proportional to real change, not event volume.
- `breakdown` is a **point-in-time snapshot** (rule name/field/operator/value/matched value/points for every matched rule) — not a live join, so history stays readable after a rule is edited or deleted.
- Malformed/unsupported rules (bad field, bad operator, invalid field/operator pairing) are **safely skipped and logged server-side** (`Log::warning`) — never break lead creation, editing, merge, or recalculation.
- `lead_score_computed_at` is maintained on every compute run (even a no-op one). The score write is a **raw query builder update**, never touching `updated_at` (see §2's architecture note).

**Scoring fields** (extensible registry, `App\Enums\LeadScoringField`): `priority`, `source_type`, `status`, `estimated_budget`, `deal_value`, `win_probability`, `has_email`, `has_phone`, `days_since_created`, `days_since_last_status_change`.

**Supported operators** (`App\Enums\LeadScoringOperator`): `>=`, `<=`, `>`, `<`, `=`, `!=`, `in`, `not_in` — each field's kind (numeric/enum/boolean) constrains which operators are valid, enforced both in the UI and server-side in `save()`.

**Trigger points** (`App\Services\LeadScoringService::SCORING_RELEVANT_DETAIL_FIELDS` is the single source of truth for which detail fields count):
- `LeadReceived` (new listener `RecomputeLeadScoreOnReceipt`)
- `CreateManualLead::handle()` (manual leads never fire `LeadReceived` — additive workaround, not a fix to that gap)
- `Show::updateStatus()` — status and/or priority changed
- `Show::applyDetailChanges()` — only when changed fields intersect `estimated_budget`, `deal_value`, `win_probability`, `email`, `phone`
- `LeadMergeService::merge()` — only when the merge's applied field resolutions intersect the same scoring-relevant set
- Daily `leads:recalculate-scores` sweep (scheduled, excludes merged-away leads) — the only thing that re-evaluates time-derived fields

**Deliberately does NOT trigger scoring**: assignment/unassignment, notes, attachments, generic `LeadUpdated` broadcasts, company-only edits, message-timeline appends, meeting/follow-up schedule/cancel/done actions.

**UI**:
- Score + per-rule breakdown shown on the Lead detail page and the Kanban flyout (`score-breakdown.blade.php`), read-only on view (never triggers a recompute itself).
- Manual "Recalculate" action, gated to Client Admin (`Show::canManageScoring`).
- Tenant-side Scoring Rules management page at `/leads/scoring-rules` (`App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadScoring\Index`), full CRUD, Client-Admin-only, with a soft warning callout when active rules could total over 100.
- The pre-existing hero stat box mislabeled "Lead Score" (it was actually `win_probability`) was corrected to **"Win Probability"** — `win_probability`'s own meaning/behavior/validation is completely unchanged, display label only.
- Priority remains a manual field; Score is a computed field — kept visually/conceptually distinct in the UI copy.
- **No manual score override** — out of scope for Phase 2a by explicit decision.

**Permissions**: no new permission slug — `App\Policies\LeadScoringRulePolicy` and `Show::canManageScoring` both reuse the existing `lead-management` `update` permission + `isClientAdmin()`. Tenant isolation enforced explicitly in every query (not relying on the `BelongsToTenant` trait alone, per §2).

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #2** (Phase 2b or later, not started): score badge in list/Kanban, score filtering, score sorting, dashboard/reporting, score-aware assignment (`LeadAssignmentService` was **not** made score-aware), ML/predictive scoring, workflow/nurturing integration, manual override, In-App Communication.

## 6. Feature #2 Verification

- 77 new scoring tests, all passing:
  - `LeadScoringServiceTest`: 27/27
  - `LeadScoringTriggersTest`: 12/12
  - `LeadScoringRuleManagementTest`: 6/6
  - `LeadScoringDisplayTest`: 5/5
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: 449 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures.
- Full application suite: 604 passed, 2 failures, 1 skip.
- The 2 failures are pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` failures (expects `Index::getListeners()` to map to `'$refresh'`, but the actual code already maps to `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) — reproduced identically on a clean `git stash` of `main` with none of this session's changes applied. Confirmed pre-existing, not caused by this work.
- Pint: clean.
- PHPStan: baseline was 258 errors; current full-project output is 269. Every additional error was individually traced to either the pre-existing `Auth::user()` union-type limitation (confirmed byte-for-byte identical in kind to pre-existing `Roles/Index.php`) or pre-existing `Lead.php` return-type gaps in code this work never touched — not scoring logic.
- Two real implementation bugs were found (by test failures, not phpstan) and fixed during this pass:
  1. `updateStatusAndAssignment()`'s internal `$this->updateStatus()` call broke once `updateStatus()` gained a method-injected `LeadScoringService` parameter (method injection only works for Livewire's own request-level action dispatch, not a plain internal PHP call) — fixed by resolving the service via `app()` inside `updateStatus()` instead.
  2. `LeadScoringService` used `loadMissing(['source', 'status'])`, which silently skipped reloading an already-cached, now-stale relation right after `Show::updateStatus()` changed it — fixed by switching to `load()` (force reload).
- Migrations and the `leads:recalculate-scores` command were both run successfully against the real Postgres dev database.
- Existing lead creation (all 3 paths), per-channel dedupe, merge, assignment/distribution, status pipeline, and notification behavior all remain passing, unmodified.

**Post-approval browser QA (2026-09-09) found two real bugs — Feature #2 was held open, not closed prematurely, until both were fixed and re-verified:**

1. **Priority/SourceType value orientation bug**: `LeadScoringField::valueOptions()` built the Priority/SourceType dropdown as `[label() => value]` (e.g. `'High' => 'high'`), but `x-dropdown-select` treats the array key as the option's stored value and the array value as its display label — the opposite convention. Picking "High" from the dropdown therefore bound the rule's `value` to the string `"High"` (the label) instead of `"high"` (the enum value), so an Equals/NotEquals rule built through the UI could never match against a Lead's real (lowercase) enum value — reproduced exactly via browser QA on a real "Priority is High" rule that silently failed to match. `HasEmail`/`HasPhone` had already been fixed from this identical defect once before (see the original `LeadScoringRuleManagementTest` regression test); this generalizes that fix to Priority/SourceType. **Fix**: inverted the pair to `[value => label]` in `LeadScoringField::valueOptions()`.
2. **Numeric Equals/NotEquals used lexicographic (string) comparison, not numeric comparison** — found while auditing bug #1's blast radius. `LeadScoringOperator::evaluate()`'s `=`/`!=` cases now branch to true numeric comparison when both operands are numeric, matching the four inequality operators' existing behavior; `normalize()` also gained a defensive array guard. No demonstrated live failure — a precautionary correctness fix, not a reactive one.
3. **Alpine/Livewire stale dropdown**: `x-dropdown-select` bakes its `options` array into Alpine's `x-data` once, at the DOM node's initial mount, and never re-evaluates it on a later Livewire morph of the same node. The Scoring Rules form's Value dropdown had no `wire:key`, so reopening the modal (even to edit the same rule again) could keep showing a stale options array baked in from an earlier mount in the same browser tab — reproduced by browser QA even after bug #1's server-side fix was already deployed and independently proven correct at every layer (DB round-trip, Eloquent cast, field resolution, operator evaluation, the actual `recalculateScore()` action). **Fix**: `App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadScoring\Index` gained a `formInstance` counter, incremented on every `create()`/`edit()` call and combined with `$field` into the Value dropdown's `wire:key` — forcing a fresh Alpine mount on every form open and on every live field switch inside an already-open form.

**Final verification after both fixes** (the authoritative closing state, superseding the initial counts above): 13 additional regression tests added across `LeadScoringRuleManagementTest`/`LeadScoringServiceTest` — an HTML-level reproduction of the dropdown-binding bug (parses the actual rendered Alpine `options:` JSON rather than assuming a value), the exact end-to-end browser scenario (Has Email +10 / Priority High +20 → 30, both rules in the breakdown), `wire:key` remount proofs, Status/SourceType/numeric-Equals coverage, and `days_since_last_status_change` fallback/log-lookup coverage that had no prior test at all. Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: 610/611 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures. Full application suite: 774/777 passed — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatch documented above (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`), confirmed unrelated to Lead Scoring. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 310 project-wide, every diagnostic traced to the same accepted baseline categories already documented throughout this module.

**Manually browser-QA'd and confirmed by the user** (as Client Admin, not a Client User): rule CRUD; Has Email +10; Priority High +20; combined score correctly 30/100 with both rules shown in the breakdown; the Value dropdown correctly retained "High" after switching the Field away from and back to Priority without a page reload; re-saving the rule and recalculating still produced 30/100 on a repeat pass.

**Feature #2 — Lead Scoring Phase 2a is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 7. Feature #3 — Lead Tagging

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Tenant-owned catalog, not Platform-Admin-managed**: `App\Models\LeadTag` (table `lead_tags`) carries `tenant_id` directly, like `LeadScoringRule` — deliberately NOT shaped like `LeadStatus`/`Currency`'s shared-catalog-plus-tenant-pivot pattern, since a tag is a tenant's own business-specific label with no cross-tenant consistency need. The same name/slug (e.g. "VIP") can exist independently in different tenants; `unique(tenant_id, slug)` is the only uniqueness constraint. No `BelongsToTenant` trait — every query filters `tenant_id` explicitly instead, same discipline as `LeadScoringRule`.
- **Attachment**: `Lead::tags()` is a `BelongsToMany` through the Laravel-default pivot table `lead_lead_tag` (no explicit table name needed), `withPivot('tagged_by')->withTimestamps()->orderBy('lead_tags.name')` — ordered by name for a stable chip-rendering order. `unique(lead_id, lead_tag_id)` on the pivot backs up the idempotent `syncWithoutDetaching()` attach path against duplicate rows.
- **Permissions — two-tier bar, no new `PermissionSlug`**: catalog CRUD (`App\Policies\LeadTagPolicy`) is Client-Admin-only (`isClientAdmin()` + `lead-management` `update`), the same bar as `LeadScoringRulePolicy`. Attach/detach on a specific lead reuses `LeadPolicy::update()` as-is (admin, or the lead's assigned Client User) — the same lightweight bar as adding a `LeadNote`, since tagging a lead you can already update is a day-to-day action, not catalog management.
- **Catalog management UI**: `/leads/tags` (`App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadTags\Index`), full CRUD, Client-Admin-only, mirrors the Scoring Rules page's shape exactly (create/edit modal, `x-confirm-modal` delete confirmation, `slugTaken` pre-check + `UniqueConstraintViolationException` catch for the race).
- **Attach/detach UI**: `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Show::attachTag()`/`detachTag()`, a shared `partials/lead-tags-manager.blade.php` (tag chips + remove buttons + an "Add tag" picker limited to the tenant's existing catalog — **no inline tag creation** from the lead page by design) included from both the full-page `lead-hero.blade.php` and the Kanban flyout's `header.blade.php`. `attachTag()` re-verifies the selected tag's `tenant_id` server-side, never trusting the dropdown's rendered options alone.
- **Table/Kanban display**: a shared `partials/tag-chips.blade.php` renders up to 2 tag chips + a "+N" badge, included from both the Leads table (new 8th "Tags" column — `<colgroup>`/header/all row-shapes/skeleton updated in lockstep, percentages recomputed) and the Kanban card (`lead-card.blade.php`, additive block under `service_interest`, no flex/layout changes to existing elements). The Tags filter dropdown uses `icon="hashtag"`, deliberately not `icon="tag"` (already used, coincidentally, by the unrelated Source filter).
- **Filtering**: single-select "has this tag" added to `FiltersLeadsQuery` (`$tag` property, `tagOptions()`, one more `applyDropdownFilters()` branch using `whereHas('tags', ...)`) — shared by `Index` (both table and Client User Kanban board) the same way `$status`/`$priority` already are. Multi-tag AND/OR filtering is explicitly deferred, not built.
- **Activity logging**: `tag_added`/`tag_removed` reuse `LeadDetailUpdateLog`'s existing extensible `action` string mechanism (the same one `meeting_cancelled`/`followup_done`/`unassigned` already use) — `changed_fields => ['tag' => $tag->name]`, no new table. `Show::activity()` now includes `'fields' => $log->changed_fields` on every action-type event (harmless no-op for the pre-existing action types, which don't read it); `activity-card.blade.php` gained two matching `@elseif` branches.
- **Merge integration (Feature #1)**: `LeadMergeService::unionTags()` runs inside the merge's existing single transaction (no nested transaction) — every unique tag from both leads ends up on the survivor exactly once; a tag both leads already carried is never duplicated; a tag carried over from the loser keeps its original `tagged_by` attribution. Reads the loser's pivot rows via a plain `DB::table('lead_lead_tag')` select (not `$loser->tags`'s Eloquent pivot magic property) to keep this fully statically typed. Not added to `MERGEABLE_FIELDS` (not a scalar Lead column) or `CHILD_MODELS` (a tag definition isn't owned by either lead, only the attachment is) — it's its own explicit step.
- **Tag deletion**: deleting a tenant's tag (`LeadTags\Index::delete()`) detaches it from every lead that carried it via the pivot's `cascadeOnDelete` on `lead_tag_id` — no delete-when-in-use block, unlike `LeadStatus`/`Currency`, since this catalog is single-tenant with no cross-tenant referential risk to protect against. The delete confirmation modal shows the exact usage count first (`confirmingDeleteUsageCount`).

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #3** (not started): multi-tag AND/OR filtering, tag-based scoring (`LeadScoringField` untouched), tag analytics/reporting, bulk tag actions, CSV import/export for tags, inline tag creation from the Lead detail page.

**Verification**: 31 new tests, all passing (`LeadTagCatalogTest`, `LeadTagAttachDetachTest`, `LeadTagFilterAndDisplayTest`, `LeadMergeTagUnionTest`). Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: 480 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures. Full application suite: 635 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (`tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php`, the same `$refresh` vs `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated` mismatch already documented as pre-existing in §6), 1 pre-existing skip. Pint: clean. PHPStan: baseline 269 (unchanged from Feature #2's end state) → 283 with this feature; all 14 additional errors individually traced to the same accepted `Auth::user()` union-type / generic-`Model` baseline patterns already present throughout this module, applied to new files — not new categories of error.

**Do not modify Feature #3 unless a future feature causes a real regression.**

## 8. Feature #4 — Deal / Opportunity (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Many-to-one with Lead, not a Lead-field mirror**: `App\Models\Deal` (table `deals`) carries `tenant_id`/`lead_id` directly — a Lead may have any number of Deals over its lifetime (repeat business, an upsell, a lost deal followed by a fresh one). `Lead::deals(): HasMany`. No uniqueness constraint caps a Lead to one Deal.
- **Deal status is a plain 3-value PHP enum, deliberately not a catalog**: `App\Enums\DealStatus` (`Open`/`Won`/`Lost`) — NOT a tenant-configurable catalog like `LeadStatus`, and NOT reusing `LeadStatus` itself. Lead's own pipeline already models proposal_sent/negotiation/won/lost at the Lead level; Deal's status only ever answers "does this specific opportunity still need work" and is completely independent — a Deal status change never touches `Lead::status_id`, and vice versa. `DealStatus::canTransitionTo()` allows only `Open⇄Won` and `Open⇄Lost` — a direct `Won→Lost` or `Lost→Won` flip is blocked; reopening back to `Open` first is required, and every real transition (including reopen) writes a `DealStatusLog` row via `Show::changeDealStatus()`, which is the single entry point for every Deal status button (Mark Won/Mark Lost/Reopen all call it with a different target).
- **`DealStatusLog` is keyed by `deal_id` only** — never `lead_id`/`tenant_id`. This is why `LeadMergeService::CHILD_MODELS` gains `Deal::class` only, never `DealStatusLog::class`: a merge updates a Deal's `lead_id` but never its `id`, so every `DealStatusLog` row stays correctly attached to its Deal automatically, with zero reparenting code of its own. Do not add `DealStatusLog::class` to `CHILD_MODELS` — it would fail outright (that table has no `lead_id` column).
- **`Lead::currentDeal()` is a derived, non-persisted UI convenience only** — no `primary_deal_id`/`is_primary` column exists anywhere. It returns the most recently created **open** Deal if any exist, otherwise the most recently created Deal overall (a display fallback so the card isn't empty for a Lead whose only Deal already closed), with a deterministic `created_at` then `id` tiebreak. It never hides, deactivates, or subordinates any other Deal — every Deal stays fully visible/editable/reopenable in the Deal list regardless of what this returns.
- **Deal ownership is independent of Lead assignment**: `Deal.owner_id` defaults to `Lead.assigned_to` at creation time (a one-time copy, not a live sync) and is independently editable thereafter. A later Lead reassignment (`LeadAssignmentService::assign()`) never overwrites an existing Deal's `owner_id` — `LeadAssignmentService` itself was not touched by this feature at all.
- **Permissions — no new `PermissionSlug`, no new policy class**: every Deal action authorizes directly against the parent Lead via the existing `LeadPolicy`. Create/edit/close(Won)/close(Lost)/reopen all reuse `Gate::allows('update', $lead)` (admin, or the lead's assigned rep) — the same lightweight bar as adding a `LeadNote` or a Feature #3 tag. **Delete is the one exception**, gated `Gate::allows('delete', $lead)` (Client-Admin-only), matching the bar for deleting the whole Lead — removing a revenue record outright is treated as consequential.
- **Currency**: `Deal.currency_id` reuses the existing `Currency`/`tenant_currencies` catalog exactly as `Lead.deal_value_currency_id`/`estimated_budget_currency_id` already do (a currency without an amount means nothing — only persisted alongside a real `value`, same rule `Show::updateDeal()` already enforces). `Platform\LeadManagement\Settings::deleteCurrency()`'s in-use check was extended to also check `deals.currency_id` — this was a required correctness fix (not optional scope), since without it a currency referenced only by a Deal could be deleted out from under it.
- **Existing Lead commercial fields are completely untouched**: `deal_value`, `deal_value_currency_id`, `win_probability`, `estimated_budget`, `estimated_budget_currency_id` keep their exact current schema, validation, `LeadScoringField` behavior, `LeadMergeService::MERGEABLE_FIELDS` conflict-resolution, and UI — unchanged, for backward compatibility and Feature #2 (Lead Scoring) compatibility. No backfill, no mirroring, no deprecation in this phase. Any future reconciliation is its own separate, explicitly-approved decision.
- **UI is detail-page-first, Phase 1 only**: one new card (`partials/deals-card.blade.php`) on both the full Lead detail page (`show.blade.php`) and the Kanban flyout (`show-panel.blade.php`) — listing every Deal (open first, then closed, newest first within each group), with create/edit/status-change/delete actions inline. No new route, no Deal Kanban, no Deal table/list view, no Kanban-card/Leads-table surfacing of Deal data — all explicitly deferred (see below).
- **Activity logging**: Deal status transitions surface in `Show::activity()` as a new `deal_status_change` event type, queried directly against `DealStatusLog` (via `whereHas('deal', ...)`, one query regardless of how many Deals a Lead has) rather than looping each Deal's own `statusLogs()` — same "no N+1" discipline as every other `activity()` source. `activity-card.blade.php` gained one matching `@elseif` branch.
- **Merge integration (Feature #1)**: `Deal::class` added to `LeadMergeService::CHILD_MODELS` — the *only* merge-side change this feature needed, since Deal is a plain one-to-many child exactly like `LeadNote`/`LeadAttachment`/etc. No bespoke union logic was needed (unlike Feature #3's tags, which are many-to-many and needed real de-duplication) — two Deals from two merging leads simply both end up on the survivor, which is already a valid state (a Lead can have multiple Deals).
- **Scoring (Feature #2) and Tags (Feature #3) are both completely frozen by this feature**: `LeadScoringField` was not touched, no Deal-based scoring exists, a Deal change never triggers a recompute. Deals use the Lead's existing tags (no Deal-specific tag catalog was built).

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #4 Phase 1** (not started): Deal Kanban, a global Deal table/list route, reporting/forecasting/weighted pipeline, bulk Deal actions, automation/workflows, Deal-specific tags, Deal-specific notes-thread/attachments (beyond the single `notes` text column already on Deal), advanced Deal filters, commissions, products/line items, quotations/invoices/payments, CRM integrations, configurable Deal sub-stages beyond the 3-state enum, and any backfill of existing `deal_value`/`win_probability` data into historical Deal rows.

**Verification**: 40 new tests, all passing (`DealManagementTest`, `DealLifecycleTest`, `LeadCurrentDealTest`, `DealMergeTest`). Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: 520 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures. Full application suite: 675 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (the same `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatch documented as pre-existing since Feature #2), 1 pre-existing skip. Pint: clean. PHPStan: baseline 283 (Feature #3's end state) → 285; both additional errors traced to the same accepted "magic property access to a `#[Computed]` method" baseline pattern already pervasive throughout this file (two new call sites: `$this->tenantCurrencies` and `$this->assignableUsers`, both newly consumed from `Show`'s own PHP code by this feature) — not a new category of error. One genuinely new PHPStan issue found during implementation (a `collect($this->assignableUsers)` call whose argument type PHPStan couldn't resolve) was fixed at the root by rewriting `dealOwnerOptions()` as a plain `foreach` rather than suppressed.

**Do not modify Feature #4 unless a future feature causes a real regression.**

## 9. Feature #5 — General Tasks / Reminders (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Many-to-one with Lead, not a Lead-field mirror**: `App\Models\Task` (table `tasks`) carries `tenant_id`/`lead_id` directly, exactly the same shape as Deal — a Lead may have any number of Tasks. `Lead::tasks(): HasMany`. `next_follow_up_at`/`meeting_date`/`meeting_time` and their existing alert/completion/cancellation behavior are **completely untouched** — Task is purely additive, coexisting with them, never migrating, mirroring, or deprecating them.
- **`due_date` (date) + `due_time` (time, record-only)** — deliberately mirrors `meeting_date`/`meeting_time`'s split rather than a single `due_at` datetime column. `Task::alertActive()` is date-only, same as `meetingAlertActive()`.
- **One shared `closed_at` timestamp** for both Completed and Cancelled outcomes — mirrors `Deal.actual_close_date` exactly (one column covering both terminal states). Never cleared/set except by `Show::changeTaskStatus()`; `due_date` itself is **never** touched by a status change (verified against `Show::recordDateAlertAction()`'s different, single-slot-field behavior — that pattern does NOT apply here, since Task has a real `status` column).
- **`priority` reuses `App\Enums\LeadPriority`** directly — no parallel `TaskPriority` enum.
- **`TaskStatus`**: `Pending`/`Completed`/`Cancelled`, structurally identical to `DealStatus` — `Pending⇄Completed`, `Pending⇄Cancelled` allowed; `Completed⇄Cancelled` directly blocked by `TaskStatus::canTransitionTo()`, must reopen through `Pending` first. Every real transition (including reopen) writes a `TaskStatusLog` row via `Show::changeTaskStatus()`, the single entry point every status button calls with a different target.
- **`TaskStatusLog` is keyed by `task_id` only** — never `lead_id`/`tenant_id`, same reasoning as `DealStatusLog`. `LeadMergeService::CHILD_MODELS` gains `Task::class` only; `TaskStatusLog::class` is deliberately never added (would fail outright — no `lead_id` column on that table) and needs no bespoke merge logic — a Task's `id` never changes, so its log history stays attached automatically. A Task's `deal_id` also survives a merge untouched and remains valid, since both Task and its linked Deal get reparented to the same survivor by the same `CHILD_MODELS` loop.
- **Task ownership is independent of Lead assignment**: `Task.assigned_to` defaults from `Lead.assigned_to` at creation time only (a one-time copy, never a live sync), independently editable thereafter, never overwritten by a later Lead reassignment. `LeadAssignmentService` was not touched.
- **Deal linkage**: `Task.deal_id` is nullable; when set, it's re-validated server-side (`Rule::exists('deals', 'id')->where('tenant_id', ...)->where('lead_id', ...)`) to belong to the *same* Lead and tenant as the Task — never trusted from the dropdown alone. Most Tasks have no Deal link.
- **Permissions — no new `PermissionSlug`, no dedicated policy class**: create/edit/status-change all reuse `Gate::allows('update', $lead)`, same bar as every other lightweight Lead-child action. **Deletion is deliberately NOT the same single Client-Admin-only bar Deal uses** — the approved model is: a Client Admin may delete any Task within an authorized Lead, gated by `Gate::allows('delete', $lead)` (`LeadPolicy::delete()` — the module's independently-assignable "delete" permission, not just "update"); a non-admin may only delete a Task actually assigned to *them* (never another user's, including an unassigned one), gated by the lighter `Gate::allows('update', $lead)` bar. Implemented as `Show::canDeleteTask(Task $task)`, reused by both the UI gate and `deleteTask()`'s own guard so the rule lives in exactly one place. No stronger pre-existing Task/Todo convention was found anywhere in the repository to defer to (confirmed by search — none existed before this feature). See "Final Verification Pass" below — the admin branch's "delete" permission check was a real bug found and fixed after the initial implementation.
- **Notifications reuse the existing pipeline entirely** — `TaskAssignedNotification` (fires on creation-with-assignee or genuine reassignment only, never when the assignee is unchanged) and `TaskAlertNotification` (daily sweep counterpart to `Task::alertActive()`), both `database`-channel, both get real-time bell delivery for free via the existing `NotificationSent`→`BroadcastUserNotification` listener — zero new broadcasting code. Both include `lead_id` in their payload so `NotificationUrlResolver` resolves click-through for free. `NotificationBell::present()` gained two new `match()` cases; `TaskAlertNotification` deliberately stores its data under the same `name`/`due_date` keys `MeetingAlertNotification` uses, so it reuses `dateReminderMessage()` unmodified.
- **`tasks:send-alert-notifications`** is a **new, dedicated command** (not folded into `SendLeadAlertNotifications`) — copy-adapted from that command's exact sweep/dedup/idempotency shape, but with its own `recipients()` keyed off the *Task's* own assignee (not the Lead's), since the two are independent. Scheduled `dailyAt('08:00')` in `routes/console.php`, same cadence as its sibling. `SendLeadAlertNotifications` itself was not modified.
- **New tenant settings** `task_alert_enabled`/`task_alert_days_before` — a third, independent alert pair (not reusing meeting/follow-up's settings), same column shape, same toggle-then-confirm UI pattern added to `Index.php`/`leads.blade.php` (a third switch+modal alongside the existing two) — required, functioning infrastructure for the approved database fields, not scope creep.
- **Activity timeline**: only `task_status_change` is surfaced (mirrors `deal_status_change` exactly, single query via `whereHas('task', ...)` with eager loading, no N+1). Task creation and reassignment are explicitly **not** logged to the activity feed — reassignment is a notification concern only (see above).
- **UI**: one new "Tasks" card (`partials/tasks-card.blade.php`) on both the full Lead detail page and the Kanban flyout, mirroring `deals-card.blade.php`'s shape — list (pending first, then closed, newest first), create/edit modal, inline Complete/Cancel/Reopen, delete-with-confirmation, an overdue red badge (`status === pending && due_date->isPast()`, a simpler check than the tenant alert-window calculation). No global Task dashboard, no Task Kanban, no new route — Phase 1 stays entirely Lead-detail-embedded.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #5 Phase 1** (not started): recurring tasks, subtasks/dependencies, task comments/attachments (beyond the single `description` field), Task-specific tags, a global Task dashboard/list route, Task Kanban, bulk Task actions, email/SMS reminder channels, per-tenant timezone handling, Task-based scoring, Task reporting/analytics, any migration of `next_follow_up_at`/`meeting_date` into Task.

**Initial verification**: 52 new tests, all passing (`TaskManagementTest`, `TaskLifecycleTest`, `TaskAssignmentNotificationTest`, `TaskMergeTest`, `SendTaskAlertNotificationsTest`). Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` + `tests/Feature/Notifications/`: 602 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures. Full application suite: 728 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (the same `LeadsIndexListenerTest` mismatch documented as pre-existing since Feature #2), 1 pre-existing skip. (One full-suite run also showed a third, one-off failure in `KanbanBoardTest`'s search-scope test — confirmed via three isolated re-runs to be pre-existing Faker-data flakiness (a randomly generated phone number coincidentally containing the digits being searched for), not a Feature #5 regression; it did not reproduce on a clean re-run of the full suite.) Pint: clean. PHPStan: baseline 285 (Feature #4's end state) → 298; all 13 additional errors individually traced, file-by-file and message-by-message (not just by raw count, to correctly discount line-number drift from ~250 inserted lines), to the same three already-accepted baseline pattern categories used throughout this module (`#[Computed]` magic property access, `Auth::user()`'s `PlatformUser|TenantUser` union passed where a concrete `TenantUser` is expected, and `Tenant`'s own relation methods lacking generic type parameters) — not a new category of error.

**Final Verification Pass** (a dedicated, targeted review against the approved architecture, run after the initial implementation above — see `Show::canDeleteTask()`'s own docblock for the resulting code):

- **Authorization bug found and fixed**: `Show::canDeleteTask()`'s Client-Admin branch originally only checked `Gate::allows('update', $lead)` + `isClientAdmin()` — it never checked the module's independently-assignable "delete" `PermissionSlug` at all. A Client Admin role deliberately scoped without Delete rights on `lead-management` (a real, supported configuration — the same boundary Deal's own admin-only deletion already honors) could still delete any Task. **Fixed**: the admin branch now requires `Gate::allows('delete', $lead)` (`LeadPolicy::delete()`); the non-admin branch is unchanged (own-assigned-task only, gated by the lighter `update` bar, per the original approved model).
- **Additional regression coverage added** (all in `tests/Feature/Leads/TaskManagementTest.php`, which grew from 19 to 23 tests):
  - A Client Admin whose role lacks the "delete" permission cannot delete a Task (proves the fix above), while still being able to create/edit one (proves the denial is specifically about "delete", not a broader authorization failure).
  - Deleting a Deal through `Show::deleteDeal()` nulls a linked Task's `deal_id` while the Task itself survives untouched — previously only implied by the migration's `nullOnDelete()`, never exercised end-to-end.
  - A query-count regression test proving the Tasks card's `assignee`/`deal` relations are eager-loaded (no N+1) — confirmed already correct from the initial implementation, locked in for the future.
  - Deleting the assigned/creating `TenantUser` nulls `Task.assigned_to`/`created_by` via the FK's `nullOnDelete()`, without deleting the Task itself.
- **Everything else re-verified against the approved architecture with no further code changes needed**: tenant/Lead isolation, Deal linkage validation, the full Task lifecycle (including that a rejected transition touches neither `status` nor `closed_at`), assignment-notification de-duplication, the `tasks:send-alert-notifications` command (recipients, dedup, overdue behavior, `leads:send-alert-notifications` confirmed byte-for-byte untouched), the activity timeline's single `task_status_change` event, `LeadMergeService::CHILD_MODELS` (`Task::class` present, `TaskStatusLog::class` deliberately absent), and every database FK/index.
- **Final counts**: `TaskManagementTest.php` 23/23 passed. Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` + `tests/Feature/Notifications/`: 606/607 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures. Full application suite: 732/735 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (the same documented `LeadsIndexListenerTest` mismatch, verified against history — unchanged since Feature #2), 1 pre-existing skip. Pint: clean (full repo). PHPStan: 298 → 298, zero diff — the fix introduced no new diagnostics.

**Do not modify Feature #5 unless a future feature causes a real regression.**

## 10. Feature #6 — Bulk Actions (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Scope**: 7 bulk actions only — Status Change, Priority Change, Assignment, Unassignment, Tag Attach, Tag Remove, Delete. No background jobs, no bulk dashboard, no global bulk Deal/Task actions, no aggregate bulk activity record, no bulk search/filter redesign, no new permission slug, no new infrastructure.
- **Selection re-derivation, never trusted from the client**: `Index::eligibleSelectedLeads()` is the single choke point every bulk action calls through — it re-derives the real, authorized Lead set from this tenant's own `filteredQuery()` (tenant-scoped, `whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')` so a merged-away lead can never be actioned) + `applyAssignmentScope()` (a non-admin's selection is silently narrowed to their own assigned leads), capped at `MAX_BULK_SELECTION = 100`. A submitted id outside this set (cross-tenant, out-of-scope, or merged-away) is silently dropped, never trusted. Deliberately does **not** re-apply the dropdown filters (`applyDropdownFilters()`) — only the tenant/assignment/merge scope, per the approved spec's exact wording.
- **Selection state**: `$selectedLeadIds` (capped array of ids), cleared on any filter/search change (`updating()`), any pagination change (`updatingPage()`), and per-page size change (`setPerPage()`) — never carried across a query-changing action. `toggleSelectAllOnPage()`/`toggleSelectAllOnBoard()` are additive/idempotent toggles (select all not-yet-selected visible rows, or clear all visible rows if already fully selected), capped the same way.
- **Every action replicates the single-Lead path's exact business logic as parallel code in `Index.php`, never a raw mass update and never a literal call into `Show.php`** (explicit instruction: don't refactor `Show` to be callable from here): Bulk Status/Priority mirror `Show::updateStatus()`'s status log / `LeadStatusChanged` event / scoring recompute / `first_contacted_at`/`closed_at` bookkeeping; Bulk Assign/Unassign call `LeadAssignmentService::assign()`/`unassign()` directly (**never** `autoAssign()` — no round-robin/least-loaded distribution is invoked) and replicate the exact no-op guard (`assigned_to` unchanged → skip) and the manual `LeadDetailUpdateLog`('unassigned') + `LeadUpdated` event `unassign()` itself doesn't write; Bulk Tag Attach/Remove reuse `Show::attachTag()`/`detachTag()`'s idempotent `syncWithoutDetaching()`/`detach()` pattern, logging only on an actual state change. Each Lead is processed inside its own `DB::transaction()` — one failure never rolls back everything else already applied, and a no-op (already at target state) counts as a success, not a skip.
- **Bulk Delete** adds one new rule beyond the single-Lead delete path: a server-side source-type restriction (`email`/`website_form`/`manual` only), mirroring the per-row Delete button's existing UI-only restriction in `leads.blade.php` but actually enforced here (the single-Lead path's own UI-only gap was explicitly left alone — out of this feature's scope to fix). Reuses the existing password-confirmation modal/trait (`ConfirmsLeadDeletionPassword`) via a `$bulkDeleteMode` flag on `Index` rather than a second modal instance — a second instance would have put two `wire:model="deletePassword"` inputs (same DOM id) on the page at once. `LeadPolicy::delete()` (Client-Admin-only) is still checked per Lead. No aggregate activity entry; each Lead's own cascade-deleted child history is unaffected.
- **Partial-success reporting**: `Index::reportBulkResult()` — "X of Y Leads {verb}. Z skipped." toast, where Y is the original (capped) selection size, not just however many `eligibleSelectedLeads()` returned — a stale/dropped id counts as skipped too, same as a per-Lead authorization failure.
- **UI**: desktop table gained a 9th checkbox column (colgroup/header/rows/skeleton kept in lockstep, same discipline as Feature #3's Tags column); Kanban board cards gained an inline checkbox (click-stop wrapped, same pattern as the existing "Move to…" menu) plus a "Select all visible" control in the filter row; a shared `partials/bulk-actions-toolbar.blade.php` (sticky bottom toolbar + the 4 non-delete modals) is `@include`d from both `leads.blade.php` and `leads-kanban.blade.php`. **Mobile card stack intentionally has no checkbox** — explicitly out of Phase 1 scope, not a gap.
- **A real regression was found and fixed during implementation, before this feature was ever reported complete**: the bulk-actions toolbar's modals were initially rendered unconditionally (independent of whether anything was selected), which permanently double-embedded the Tags filter's option catalog into the page (Alpine `x-data` baked at render time — see Feature #2's identical class of bug) and broke `LeadTagFilterAndDisplayTest`'s "a tag name appears exactly once" assertion. **Fixed**: the entire toolbar partial (not just the visible strip) is now gated behind `$this->selectedCount > 0`, so none of its modals render into the DOM at all until there's a selection to act on.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #6 Phase 1** (not started): background/queued bulk processing, a bulk-actions dashboard, bulk actions on Deals/Tasks, an aggregate bulk activity record, bulk search/filter redesign, "select all matching filtered results" (only currently-visible page/board), mobile card-stack bulk selection.

**Verification**: 20 new tests, all passing (`BulkActionsTest`). Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` + `tests/Feature/Notifications/`: 648/649 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures. Full application suite: 774/777 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (the same `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` `'$refresh'` vs `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated` mismatch documented as pre-existing since Feature #2), 1 pre-existing skip. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 310 project-wide — unchanged from the count already established at Feature #2's closure; zero new diagnostics from this feature's files. Explicit regression re-runs of every completed feature's own test files (Feature #1's `LeadMergeTest`/`LeadMergeTagUnionTest`/`DealMergeTest`/`TaskMergeTest`, Feature #2's 4 scoring files, Feature #3's 3 tag files, Feature #4's `DealManagementTest`/`DealLifecycleTest`/`LeadCurrentDealTest`, Feature #5's `TaskManagementTest`/`TaskLifecycleTest`/`TaskAssignmentNotificationTest`/`SendTaskAlertNotificationsTest`) all passed with zero regressions. Code review confirmed: no `Deal`/`Task` references anywhere in the bulk-action methods, no raw mass update against the `leads` table, no new migration/schema change, no new `PermissionSlug`/policy, and only the 4 intended files (`Index.php`, `leads.blade.php`, `leads-kanban.blade.php`, `lead-card.blade.php`) plus the 2 new files (`bulk-actions-toolbar.blade.php`, `BulkActionsTest.php`) were touched — no other completed feature's source was modified. Manually browser-QA'd and confirmed by the user (Client Admin) as complete and passing.

**Feature #6 — Bulk Actions Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 11. Feature #7 — CSV Import (+ Reverb Integration)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker). Full field-level detail (exact headers, size/row limits, duplicate-detection ordering, currency-code resolution, idempotency strategy) lives in `docs/lead-management/FEATURE-07-CSV-IMPORT-HANDOFF.md` — this section is the authoritative closure summary; that document is the detailed implementation reference.

**Core import architecture:**
- Max file size **10 MB**, max **10,000** data rows (header excluded), both enforced before anything is queued — the whole upload is rejected outright, never truncated.
- Exact allowed headers, case-insensitive/whitespace-trimmed: required `name`; optional `email`, `phone`, `company`, `industry`, `location`, `service_interest`, `budget`, `budget_currency`, `deal_value`, `deal_value_currency`, `status`, `priority`, `assignee_email`, `tags`. A missing `name` or any unknown column rejects the entire upload before a `LeadImport` row ever exists.
- Row-level failures are **partial-success** — one bad row fails on its own, every other valid row still imports.
- Duplicate detection: **existing-Lead** (normalized email OR phone, conflicting matches rejected) and **in-file** (first valid row wins, checked *before* the existing-Lead check so an in-file duplicate gets correct, distinct attribution) — never merges, never overwrites.
- Status/priority/assignee/tags/currency resolved **only** against the tenant's own catalogs — never auto-created; an unresolvable value fails only that row.
- Assignment goes through the real `LeadAssignmentService::assign()` (real notification); `autoAssign()`/round-robin is never invoked. Scoring is explicit via `LeadScoringService::computeFor($lead, 'lead_received')`, synchronous per row.
- `LeadReceived`/`LeadUpdated` **never** fire for a CSV-imported row (avoids per-row notification/auto-assignment fan-out at import scale) — this is why the import's own live-status mechanism (below) had to be built independently rather than riding on those events.
- Processing is queued and chunked via Laravel Excel's native `ShouldQueue` + `WithChunkReading` (chunk size 200) — real Laravel job **chaining**, never `Bus::batch()`. Chunks run strictly sequentially, guaranteeing deterministic row-order with no lock.
- Uploaded CSVs stored on **private** local storage, private path never rendered in either Blade view.
- `App\Models\LeadImport`/`LeadImportRow` persist accurate per-row outcomes and a terminal status (`Completed`/`CompletedWithErrors`/`Failed`).
- New lazily-provisioned `LeadSourceType::CsvImport` (structurally identical to `Manual`), required a companion Postgres CHECK-constraint migration (`lead_sources.type`) — the same established pattern every prior `LeadSourceType` addition has needed.
- No scope creep: no field-mapping UI, no new permission slug (reuses `Gate::authorize('create', Lead::class)` — the same bar as manual Lead creation), no Deal/Task creation from import.
- Navigation: an **"Import Leads" button was added to `/leads`** (Client Admin table view only, next to "Export CSV", gated by `canCreateLead`) at the user's explicit request — the Scoring Rules/Tags-style "URL-only, no nav entry" precedent noted during initial build was superseded for this feature specifically; the Kanban (Client User) view was deliberately left unchanged.

**Real-time status architecture (Reverb primary + one-time reconciliation, added during browser QA remediation — see bugs below):**
- `App\Events\LeadImportStatusChanged` — `ShouldBroadcast` + `ShouldDispatchAfterCommit` (defensive; verified against actual Laravel Excel source that neither firing point currently sits inside a wrapping transaction, so this is a no-op today and protects only against a future refactor), deliberately never `ShouldBroadcastNow` (finalization must never block on Reverb being reachable).
- Fires at **exactly the two existing terminal finalization points** in `App\Imports\LeadCsvImport` — the `AfterImport` listener (normal completion) and `failed(Throwable $e)` (chain-aborting infrastructure fault) — never per-row, never on the Pending→Processing transition.
- `PrivateChannel('lead-import.{id}')`, authorized in `routes/channels.php` with the same bar as `Show::mount()`: `$user->tenant_id === $leadImport->tenant_id && Gate::forUser($user)->allows('create', Lead::class)`.
- `broadcastWith()` carries **only** `['id' => $leadImport->id]` — `App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadImports\Show::getListeners()` (`echo-private:lead-import.{id},.lead-import.status-changed` → `refreshStatus()`) always re-queries authoritative DB state rather than trusting the payload, matching every other Echo listener in this app (`NotificationBell`, `Leads\Index`).
- **No recurring polling of any kind.** A single **one-time reconciliation** — `x-data` + `x-init="setTimeout(() => $wire.refreshStatus(), 5000)"` on the Show page's root element, inside the same non-terminal `@if` guard the old poll used — fires exactly once, 5s after mount, to close two narrow races Reverb alone can't: the terminal broadcast landing before this page's private-channel subscription finishes, or a brief Reverb disconnect exactly during finalization. It never re-arms; after it fires, Reverb is the sole remaining mechanism for that import. This is a plain Alpine `setTimeout`, not Livewire's `wire:poll` machinery, so it is not subject to that directive's background-tab throttle (see bugs below).
- Reverb/Echo wiring (`resources/js/echo.js`, `routes/channels.php`'s existing channel-auth pattern, `window.Echo` loaded app-wide via `resources/js/app.js`) was verified end-to-end in the real browser: WebSocket 101 upgrade, `connection.state === 'connected'`, a live `socket_id`, and a successful `/broadcasting/auth` for the `lead-import.{id}` channel — confirmed via actual pusher-js source that a successful `/broadcasting/auth` call is only reachable once the socket is genuinely connected, not merely configured.

**Bugs found and fixed during browser QA (in the order encountered):**
1. **Upload button stuck loading indefinitely, every attempt.** Root cause: the Livewire component's own action method was named `upload()`, colliding with Livewire's **reserved client-side `$wire.upload()` magic API** (used internally for `wire:model` file-input uploads). `wire:submit="upload"` resolved to Livewire's built-in uploader instead of the server action, which then crashed client-side (`TypeError: can't access property "name", file is undefined`) *before* any request for the real action was ever sent — invisible to every PHP-level `Livewire::test()->call('upload')` test, since those bypass the browser JS layer entirely. Fixed by renaming the method (and `wire:submit`/`wire:target`/all test call sites) to `import()`.
2. **Status badge stuck on "Processing" until a manual refresh**, even though the backend finalized correctly in ~2s every time. Root cause, confirmed by reading Livewire's actual compiled JS (`wire-poll.js`): `wire:poll` has a built-in throttle that skips ~95% of ticks while `document.hidden` is true, and the original `wire:poll.3s="refreshStatus"` lacked the `.keep-alive` modifier needed to opt out. The MCP/CDP-automated browser QA session effectively backgrounded the tab for most of the wait, so polling was throttled to roughly one tick per ~60s rather than genuinely broken. **`wire:navigate` was explicitly investigated and ruled out** (verified in source: `nowInitializeAlpineOnTheNewPage()` correctly re-scans directives after every navigate swap). This finding is what drove the deliberate architecture decision to move to Reverb-primary + one-time reconciliation (above) rather than just adding `.keep-alive` to a recurring poll.
3. Two smaller bugs from the pre-Reverb final verification pass, both fixed and re-verified: a missing Postgres CHECK-constraint migration for `csv_import` (production-blocking on Postgres, invisible on SQLite's test driver), and test-only `Storage::fake('local')` omissions that had been leaking real files into `storage/app/private/lead-imports`.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #7** (not started): field-mapping UI, background import dashboard, CSV export/import for other entities, Deal/Task creation from import rows, Kanban-side import navigation entry.

**Final verification**: Feature #7 focused suite (`LeadImportUploadTest`, `CreateLeadFromImportTest`, `LeadCsvImportEngineTest`, `LeadImportBroadcastTest`, `ChannelAuthorizationTest`): **54/54 passed**. Full application suite: **819/823 passed**, 1 pre-existing skip; the 4 non-passing results are the 2 already-documented pre-existing `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures (`$refresh` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`, unchanged since Feature #2) plus 1 `KanbanBoardTest` failure independently re-run 3/3 passing in isolation — the same pre-existing Faker-data flakiness already documented in Feature #5's own verification, not a Feature #7 regression. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 317, identical to the baseline established at Feature #6's closure — zero new diagnostics in any file this feature touched.

**Feature #7 — CSV Import + Reverb Integration is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 12. Feature #8 — Reporting & Analytics (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Scope — 4 metrics only, one shared date range**: Lead volume (total + a daily breakdown table), current status/funnel distribution, source performance, and assignment/team distribution. Explicitly deferred: score, Deal, Task, duplicate-trend, and import-trend reporting — none of these read from `LeadScoreLog`, `Deal`/`DealStatusLog`, `Task`/`TaskStatusLog`, `LeadDuplicateLink`, or `LeadImport`/`LeadImportRow` at all.
- **Status/funnel and assignment/team are both current-state snapshots, not historical analysis** — "of the leads created in this range, what status/assignee do they have *right now*." Neither reads `lead_status_logs`' stage-transition history nor `lead_assignments`' reassignment history; explicitly not a conversion-rate or per-rep-outcome calculation. This scoping was raised and explicitly confirmed before implementation, not assumed.
- **`App\Services\LeadReportingService`** — one method per metric (`volumeCount`, `dailyVolumeBreakdown`, `statusBreakdown`, `sourceBreakdown`, `assignmentBreakdown`), each building its own query from scratch: explicit `tenant_id` filter (never relying on `BelongsToTenant`'s global scope alone, same discipline as every other cross-lead query in this module) and `whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')` (the same exclusion `Leads\Index::filteredQuery()` already applies) on every single query, no exceptions. `statusBreakdown()` delegates to the pre-existing `Lead::countsByStatus()` rather than reimplementing status-grouping — zero-filled against the tenant's actual configured pipeline (`Tenant::leadStatuses()`), same as the Kanban board's column headers.
- **Date range**: reuses `FiltersLeadsQuery`'s exact boundary semantics (`created_at >= startOfDay(fromDate)`, `created_at < startOfDay(toDate + 1 day)`). New to this feature (no prior precedent to inherit): defaults to the last 30 days (including today) on first load, and rejects — never silently truncates — any selection wider than 92 calendar days via the existing `$this->addError()`/`@error()` validation convention.
- **Daily lead-volume breakdown**: one row per calendar day in the selected range, zero-filled for days with no leads (the query returns only days with ≥1 lead; every day in the range is then filled in against the known full set — the same zero-fill technique `Lead::countsByStatus()` already uses, just in PHP instead of relying on a partial DB result). Paginated at 50 rows/page (`WithPagination`, matching `LeadImports\Show::rows()`'s exact convention) — the only one of the four metrics whose row count isn't naturally bounded by small cardinality.
- **Source performance never filters out inactive sources** — a source's historical lead volume must not disappear from the report just because it was later deactivated (`LeadSource::active()`'s scope is never applied here). The UI shows a cosmetic "Inactive" badge next to a deactivated source's name; the count itself is unaffected.
- **Assignment/team includes an explicit "Unassigned" bucket** rather than dropping `assigned_to IS NULL` leads from the grouping — queried as a separate `whereNull('assigned_to')->count()` rather than folded into the grouped query, since SQL `GROUP BY`/`pluck()` would otherwise coerce the NULL key to an empty-string array key, making "unassigned" indistinguishable from a real (if malformed) user id.
- **No monetary aggregation** — `estimated_budget`, `deal_value`, and `Deal.value` are never summed/averaged; no currency conversion exists anywhere in this codebase to make such a sum meaningful. Deal reporting itself is deferred (see above).
- **Authorization — no new `PermissionSlug`, no dedicated policy class**: `Show::mount()`-equivalent inline guard — `Gate::authorize('viewAny', Lead::class)` (the existing, unmodified `LeadPolicy::viewAny()`) followed by `abort_unless(Auth::user()->isClientAdmin(), 403)`. Client-Admin-only, tenant-wide (no separate Client User view exists for this page, same precedent as `LeadScoring\Index`/`LeadTags\Index`).
- **No caching, no scheduled/precomputed infrastructure** — every metric is a live query on every page load, by explicit Phase 1 decision. `CACHE_STORE=database`/`QUEUE_CONNECTION=database` were confirmed not actively used for anything in this module before this feature, and remain untouched by it.
- **No chart library** — stat cards (`flux:card`) and `flux:table`s only, matching the existing `Tenant\Dashboard`'s and Import-detail-page's stat-tile conventions exactly. No new frontend dependency was added.
- **No export** — out of scope for Phase 1; `app/Exports/` untouched.
- **New index**: `leads(tenant_id, created_at)` (migration `2026_09_13_060000_add_composite_index_to_leads_for_reporting.php`) — every one of this feature's 5 queries filters by this composite, and no prior index served it. Shaped identically to the precedent `2026_09_05_083210_add_composite_index_to_leads_for_assignment_scope.php`.
- **Route & navigation**: `leads/reports` (`tenant-leads.reports`), inside the existing `module:lead-management` route group, positioned before the `leads/{lead}` catch-all alongside `scoring-rules`/`tags`/`import`. A dedicated sidebar item ("Reports & Analytics", `chart-bar` icon) was added to `resources/views/layouts/app/sidebar.blade.php`, nested inside the same `isClientAdmin()` → `hasModule('lead-management')` conditional block as the existing "Lead Sources" item — the closest and only real precedent for a standalone, Client-Admin-only Lead Management sub-page in this app's nav (CSV Import's earlier in-page button was considered and not used here, since a report isn't a leads-list-contextual action the way importing/exporting is).
- **Two implementation-time technical necessities, neither a scope or behavior deviation**: (1) the test suite runs on SQLite (`phpunit.xml`), which has no `date_trunc()` — the daily-breakdown query is driver-aware (`config('database.default') === 'pgsql'` → `date_trunc`, else SQLite's `date()`); production/Postgres still gets exactly the `date_trunc` approach that was approved. (2) this app globally overrides `now()` to return `CarbonImmutable` (`AppServiceProvider::boot()`'s `Date::use(CarbonImmutable::class)`), while `FiltersLeadsQuery`'s own convention (`Carbon::parse(...)`) returns mutable `Carbon` — `LeadReportingService`'s date parameters are typed against the shared `Carbon\CarbonInterface` rather than a concrete class so both flavors work correctly.

**Explicitly deferred from Feature #8 Phase 1** (not started): lead score performance reporting, Deal/opportunity reporting, Task reporting, duplicate-trend reporting, CSV-import-trend reporting, any monetary aggregate, charts/visualizations, export, caching/precomputation, Client User visibility, weekly/monthly/custom time-bucketing beyond the single daily-volume table.

**Verification**: 17 new tests, all passing (`LeadReportingTest`) — volume/status/source/assignment correctness, zero-fill (status and daily), merged-lead exclusion, tenant isolation, Client User/no-permission 403, date-boundary correctness matching `FiltersLeadsQuery` exactly, 92-day rejection and exact-92-day acceptance, from-after-to rejection, daily-table pagination, inactive-source inclusion + UI badge, the unassigned bucket, a bounded query-count assertion (N+1 guard), and sidebar visibility for admin vs. non-admin. Full application suite: 837/840 passed, 2 pre-existing failures (the same `LeadsIndexListenerTest` mismatch documented as pre-existing since Feature #2), 1 pre-existing skip. Pint: clean. PHPStan: 337 vs. the 317 baseline established at Feature #7's closure — the +20 traced individually to two already-accepted categories used throughout this module (`Auth::user()` union type, magic property access to a `#[Computed]` method) plus one already-pre-existing `Collection` generic-covariance limitation (confirmed identical in kind to `Leads\Show.php:607`, not a new category) — no suppressions, no `@phpstan-ignore` comments, no type casts added purely to silence a finding; every genuinely fixable issue (a `ConnectionInterface` method call, a `collect()` template-resolution gap, missing `Collection<TKey,TValue>` generics, an undefined-property access on a synthetic aggregate row) was fixed at the root before accepting the rest as baseline.

**Feature #8 — Reporting & Analytics Phase 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future feature causes a real regression.

## 13. Feature #9 — Workflow / Nurturing (Phase 1)

Final approved architecture and decisions (do not re-litigate without a real blocker):

- **Scope — lead-inactivity nurturing only**: one condition (a lead inactive ≥ a tenant-configured threshold), two independently-toggleable actions (send a notification, create a follow-up task). Explicitly no generic action dispatcher, no rules-management CRUD, no visual workflow builder, no multi-step sequence/per-lead workflow-state table, no execution-log table, no event-driven triggering — scheduled-only, single-step, by deliberate decision.
- **`App\Console\Commands\NurtureInactiveLeads`** (`leads:nurture-inactive-leads`, scheduled `dailyAt('08:00')->withoutOverlapping()` in `routes/console.php`, alongside the three existing daily alert commands) — same structural shape as `SendLeadAlertNotifications`/`SendTaskAlertNotifications`: iterate active tenants explicitly (`Tenant::query()->where('status', Active)->where(...notify_enabled...OR...task_enabled...)`), every lead query filtered by that tenant's `tenant_id` explicitly (never `BelongsToTenant`'s global scope, which doesn't activate in this context).
- **Condition — reuses the existing Lead Scoring evaluation logic exactly, without touching `LeadScoringRule`**: `App\Enums\LeadScoringField::DaysSinceLastStatusChange->resolve($lead)` (unmodified — same fallback to `Lead.created_at` when no `LeadStatusLog` exists) compared via `App\Enums\LeadScoringOperator::GreaterThanOrEqual->evaluate($daysInactive, $tenant->lead_nurturing_inactive_days)` — `>=` semantics, confirmed in code. `LeadScoringField`/`LeadScoringOperator` themselves were not modified by this feature (any uncommitted diff on those two files predates Feature #9 — see the verification note below).
- **Tenant settings** (`tenants` table, migration `2026_09_14_060000_...`): `lead_nurturing_notify_enabled`, `lead_nurturing_task_enabled` (both boolean, default false, independently toggleable — deliberately not a single shared flag) + `lead_nurturing_inactive_days` (unsigned smallint, default 0, shared threshold). Same column shape as the three existing alert-settings pairs (`meeting_alert_*`/`followup_alert_*`/`task_alert_*`).
- **Lead eligibility**: `whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')` (the same exclusion `Leads\Index::filteredQuery()` already applies) and `whereNotIn('status_id', $closedStatusIds)` (`$closedStatusIds` from `$tenant->leadStatuses()->where('is_closed', true)`) — a closed lead is never eligible, a merged-away lead is never eligible, regardless of how inactive either appears.
- **Notification action**: `App\Notifications\LeadInactivityAlertNotification` (`database` channel), recipients are every active Client Admin plus the lead's own active assignee if not already in that set — the identical recipient rule `SendLeadAlertNotifications::recipients()` already uses, with the same no-duplicate guard when the admin and assignee are the same person. Same-day idempotency per recipient via an `alreadyNotifiedToday()`-style existence check against the `notifications` table.
- **Task action**: reuses the existing `Task::create()` path exactly (`tenant_id`, `lead_id`, `status = Pending`, `priority = Medium`, `assigned_to = $lead->assigned_to`, `created_by = null`, `due_date = today()`, `title = "Follow up — inactive for :days days"`). An assigned task still fires the existing `TaskAssignedNotification` unmodified. Same-day idempotency via a new `leads.nurturing_task_created_at` nullable timestamp (migration `2026_09_14_060001_...`), checked before creating and written via a **raw query builder update** (`DB::table('leads')->where('id', ...)->update(...)`), never `$lead->update()` — deliberately excluded from `Lead`'s `#[Fillable]` (same treatment as `lead_score`/`lead_score_computed_at`) so it can never spuriously touch `updated_at` and trip `Lead::hasUnseenUpdate()`'s highlight for every viewer.
- **Failure isolation**: the notification and task actions each run inside their own `try`/`catch` inside `processLead()` — one failing is logged and does not prevent the other from running. Per-lead and per-tenant iteration are each wrapped the same way, so one lead's or one tenant's failure never stops the rest of the sweep.
- **Idempotency/concurrency note**: the check-then-write on `nurturing_task_created_at` is safe without a row lock specifically because the single scheduled command instance (guarded by `withoutOverlapping()`) processes every tenant's leads strictly sequentially within one PHP process — there is no concurrent writer that could race between the check and the update. This would need revisiting only if the command were ever made to run concurrently (e.g. queued per-tenant), which it isn't.
- **Authorization/configuration**: no new `PermissionSlug`, no dedicated policy class. The 4 new Livewire methods (`toggleNurturingNotify`/`toggleNurturingTask`/`openNurturingDaysEditor`/`saveNurturingDays` on `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Index`) are each gated `isClientAdmin()`-only, identical to the three existing alert-settings toggles. UI: two independent `flux:switch` controls sharing one threshold-editor modal (`min:0`/`max:365` validated), added to the same settings row as the meeting/follow-up/task alerts on `/leads` — no new page.

**A genuine, pre-existing PostgreSQL bug was found (not introduced) during this feature's implementation and verification, confirmed live against the real database, not assumed:**

- `App\Console\Commands\SendLeadAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` and `App\Console\Commands\SendTaskAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` both query `->where('data->lead_id', ...)` / `->where('data->task_id', ...)` — a JSON-path operator that requires a `json`/`jsonb` column. The real `notifications.data` column (Laravel's own default notifications migration) is a plain `text` column. Re-run standalone against the actual Postgres database, both queries throw `SQLSTATE[42883]: operator does not exist: text ->> unknown` every time — meaning each existing command's own duplicate-notification guard is currently broken in real production usage (SQLite, the test driver, doesn't enforce this the same way, which is why the existing test suite never caught it).
- **This is pre-existing, outside Feature #9's scope, and was explicitly NOT fixed or refactored** — per direct instruction, `SendLeadAlertNotifications`/`SendTaskAlertNotifications` were left completely untouched. `App\Console\Commands\NurtureInactiveLeads::alreadyNotifiedToday()` deliberately does **not** copy this pattern: it uses a `LIKE` match against the JSON-encoded text (`where('data', 'like', '%"lead_id":"'.$lead->id.'"%')`), verified working against the real Postgres database with no exception.
- **Recommended as a separate, future, explicitly-scoped follow-up fix** — not bundled into Feature #9's closure.

**Failure-isolation verification method — disclosed, not concealed**: the "notification failure doesn't block the task action, and vice versa" and "one lead/tenant failure doesn't stop the rest of the sweep" guarantees are verified by direct code-structure inspection (the independent `try`/`catch` blocks described above) plus organic manual evidence from real-Postgres smoke-testing (the pre-fix JSON bug above genuinely threw inside the notification action during testing, was logged, and the task action for that same lead still completed in the same run — real, reproduced evidence, not fabricated). No dedicated automated exception-injection test exists for these two guarantees specifically — constructing one cleanly would have required adding test-only seams to production code, which was deliberately avoided rather than done silently.
