- Focused `PlatformLeadSourceDeletionTest.php` (new file): **11/11 passed** — empty-source success (with exact success-toast assertion), each of the five dependencies individually blocked (with exact danger-toast assertions), messages-survive-a-blocked-attempt, mixed-dependencies-blocked-by-the-first-checked-one, authorization-still-enforced, cross-tenant-isolation, and a `lockForUpdate()`-compiles-into-the-query test mirroring `LeadSourceCostCatalogTest.php`'s own established technique.
- `LeadPipelinePickerTest.php` (contains the one pre-existing Platform-deletion test, left untouched): **10/10 passed**.
- Tenant-side deletion + Currency-guard regression (`FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest`, `LeadSourceCostCatalogTest`, `LeadManagementSettingsTest`): **59/59 passed**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **899/900 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite: **1065/1068 passed, 2 failures, 1 skip** — the 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2. Zero new failures — independently re-run and reproduced during a separate final-verification pass before closure.
- Pint: clean.
- PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly; explicitly confirmed the errors still listed against `LeadSources.php` are the same six pre-existing methods this fix never touched, at shifted line numbers only; zero errors on `delete()` itself or in the new test file.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: authorization and tenant-scoped resolution unchanged in behavior; no model, migration, policy, or service file was touched (`app/Livewire/Platform/Clients/LeadSources.php` was the only production file changed); the tenant-side `deleteSource()` path was not modified; no force-delete capability was introduced; no Conversation/Message lifecycle, archive, or backfill change was made.

**C1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. **DE1 remains open/deferred** — not resolved by C1, not scheduled, pending an explicit product decision.

### C2 — LeadMergeService does not reparent Conversation/Message records — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `LeadMergeService::CHILD_MODELS` omitted `Conversation`/`Message` (both added in Feature #15, after `CHILD_MODELS` was last extended for Feature #14's Consent work). After a merge, the loser's normalized conversation/message history stayed pointed at the loser (`lead_id = loser`), invisible on the survivor's timeline and composer, since every consumer (`Lead::messageTimeline()`, `Show::activeConversation()`) queries via the survivor's own `hasMany` relations.

**Fix**: Added `Conversation::class` and `Message::class` to `LeadMergeService::CHILD_MODELS`, reparented by the exact same pre-existing generic `WHERE lead_id = loser UPDATE lead_id = survivor` loop every other entry (`Task`, `Deal`, `LeadConsentEvent`, etc.) already uses — no bespoke merge method, no new abstraction. Schema safety was explicitly verified before implementation: `conversations_identity_unique` (`tenant_id, lead_source_id, channel, external_thread_id`) and `messages_external_id_unique` (`tenant_id, channel, external_message_id`) neither include `lead_id`, so a blind reparent can never collide with either constraint — `RecordInboundMessage::handle()`'s own `firstOrCreate()` already guarantees at most one `Conversation` row exists per identity tuple tenant-wide regardless of which lead currently owns it, so moving `lead_id` can't create a duplicate of an already-unique row. `Message.lead_id` is a separate, denormalized column (not derived from `conversation_id`), so both classes are reparented independently in the same loop to keep a reparented `Conversation` and its `Message`s consistent with each other.

**Explicitly deferred, investigated but NOT implemented as part of C2** (each requires its own separate, future decision):
- **Webhook lead-resolution continuity** — the Messenger/Instagram/WhatsApp inbound "find existing lead" lookups (`source_data->facebook_user_id`/`source_data->instagram_user_id`/`source_id`+`phone`) already carry a `whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')` guard (**correction, made at a later closure**: this bullet originally and incorrectly stated the guard was absent — re-verified against the current codebase and found present on all three lookups). The actual gap is a different, adjacent one: once that guard correctly excludes a merged-away loser, none of the three lookups has any way to find the *survivor* instead (since `source_data`/`phone` are deliberately never merged onto it — see `LeadMergeService::MERGEABLE_FIELDS`), so a *new* inbound message after a merge spawned a spurious brand-new Lead rather than continuing onto the survivor. Discovered during C2's inspection; `LeadWebhookController.php` was deliberately **not modified** at the time — closed later as "RecordInboundMessage Conversation/Lead Identity Mismatch / Webhook Lead-Resolution Continuity" (see §23 below).
- **`messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case** — after reparenting, a survivor's own older legacy `source_data` entries could in a narrow scenario (duplicate leads across two different `lead_source_id`s of the same channel, one with pre-Feature-#15 legacy history, the other with earlier normalized history) be hidden by the loser's now-reparented, earlier normalized message pulling the survivor's `$earliestDualWriteAt` cutover backward. **Accepted as documented technical debt, not fixed** — `app/Models/Lead.php`/`messageTimeline()` were deliberately **not modified**.
- **Historical backfill** — C2 is forward-only; leads merged before this fix shipped keep their orphaned Conversation/Message rows on the loser unless a separate, explicitly-approved backfill effort addresses them later. No backfill script was written.
- **M8 (no `lockForUpdate()` on the survivor/loser pair before the already-merged check)** — pre-existing, unrelated to C2's own two new `CHILD_MODELS` entries (which inherit the same transaction every other entry already has), left untouched.

**Verification**:
- `LeadMergeTest.php` (extended, not a new file — the natural, already-established home): **12/12 passed** — 4 existing tests extended with `Conversation`/`Message` fixtures and assertions (primary reparenting test, cross-tenant rejection, rollback/atomicity), plus 2 new tests (multiple loser conversations across different `lead_source_id`s reparent without deduplication; `messageTimeline()` surfaces the loser's reparented message while the survivor's own legacy `source_data` entry stays untouched).
- Sibling merge tests (`DealMergeTest`, `TaskMergeTest`, `LeadCustomFieldMergeTest`, `LeadMergeTagUnionTest`, `LeadStatusAssignmentMergedFormTest`): **34/34 passed**, unaffected.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **901/902 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures** (902 = the 900-test baseline + 2 new tests).
- Full application suite, run twice at final verification: **1067/1070 passed, 2 failures, 1 skip**, both times — the 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2. A one-off, non-reproducing `KanbanBoardTest.php` failure (a Faker-generated-value/search-term collision, unrelated to Lead merging, Conversation, or Message) appeared on exactly one of five total full-suite runs across implementation and verification and did not recur on the other four — investigated and confirmed a pre-existing test-ordering fragility, not a C2 regression; left untouched.
- Pint: clean.
- PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly; zero errors in either changed file (`app/Services/LeadMergeService.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadMergeTest.php`).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `Conversation`/`Message` reparenting uses only the pre-existing generic `CHILD_MODELS` loop, no bespoke logic; the existing transaction/rollback guarantee covers both new entries without any additional code; tenant/self/already-merged guards are unchanged; merge authorization (`LeadPolicy::merge()`) is unchanged; no migration, model, controller, or webhook file was touched — `app/Services/LeadMergeService.php` was the only production file changed.

**C2 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The webhook lead-resolution gap, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, historical backfill, and M8 all remain separately deferred/open — none are resolved by C2's closure.

### C3 — SendLeadAlertNotifications' PostgreSQL-incompatible notifications.data JSON-path query — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `SendLeadAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` queried `->where('data->lead_id', $lead->id)` against `notifications.data`, a plain `text` column (Laravel's stock notifications migration), not `json`/`jsonb`. Laravel's `->` syntax compiles to a Postgres JSON operator, which Postgres rejects against a `text` column with `SQLSTATE[42883]: operator does not exist: text -> unknown` — confirmed to throw on every run against the real production database. SQLite (the test driver) enforces no such type check, which is why this was never caught by the existing test suite.

**Fix**: Replaced the JSON-path condition with the exact portable pattern already established and proven in `NurtureInactiveLeads::alreadyNotifiedToday()`: `->where('data', 'like', '%"lead_id":"'.$lead->id.'"%')`. Verified directly against this app's real PostgreSQL connection (read-only `->toSql()` inspection, not just inferred) that the new query compiles to a valid `"data"::text like ?` expression. `notifications.data`'s column type was not changed — no migration, no schema change. Tenant and recipient scoping (`$recipient->notifications()`) is unchanged; only the match clause changed.

### C4 — SendLeadAlertNotifications does not exclude merged-away leads — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `sweep()`'s query (`$tenant->leads()->whereNotNull($dateColumn)`) had no `merged_into_lead_id` exclusion, unlike every sibling sweep in this module (`NurtureInactiveLeads`, `DetectDuplicateLeads`, `RecalculateLeadScores`, `LeadAssignmentService`). A merged-away (loser) lead that still carried a `meeting_date`/`next_follow_up_at` kept generating daily bell notifications pointing at a lead whose own detail page immediately redirects to its survivor.

**Fix**: Added `->whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')` to the same query, matching the convention already used everywhere else in this module. Verified directly against the real PostgreSQL connection that the clause compiles correctly. Eligible survivor leads remain fully unaffected; tenant scoping (`$tenant->leads()`) is unchanged.

**Implemented and closed together as one remediation unit** (both changes live in the same method chain of the same 127-line file, with no interaction risk between them), per the approved architecture/decision brief.

**Explicitly out of scope, discovered but NOT fixed as part of C3/C4**: `SendTaskAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` (line 104) has the exact same JSON-path bug (`->where('data->task_id', $task->id)`) against the same `text` column — confirmed present, deliberately left untouched, tracked as a separate, unapproved finding for a future session.

**Verification**:
- `SendLeadAlertNotificationsTest.php`: **8/8 passed, 13 assertions** — the existing same-day-deduplication test continues to pass unmodified against the new C3 query; one new test proves a merged-away lead produces zero notifications while an eligible survivor still produces its expected one.
- `SendTaskAlertNotificationsTest.php` (untouched file): **9/9 passed**.
- Relevant Lead alert tests (`LeadAlertTest`, `LeadAlertSettingsTest`, `LeadAlertShimmerRenderTest`, `LeadMeetingFollowUpAlertActionsTest`): **38/38 passed**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **901/902 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite: **1068/1071 passed, 2 failures, 1 skip** — the 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2; reproduced identically across two full-suite runs at final verification, zero new failures either time.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero attributable to this fix. `git diff --check`: no whitespace/check issues.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `notifications.data` remains a `text` column, no migration or schema change was made; tenant and recipient scoping unchanged; existing deduplication behavior intact; `SendTaskAlertNotifications.php`, `NurtureInactiveLeads.php`, `Lead.php`, `LeadMergeService.php`, and every other audit finding (C1, C2, DE1, M1–M10, L1–L4) were not touched.

**C3 and C4 are CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `SendTaskAlertNotifications.php`'s identical JSON-path bug remained open/deferred at this closure, not resolved by it — it was later resolved as its own separate, standalone fix; see "SendTaskAlertNotifications PostgreSQL JSON-Path Compatibility Fix" below.

### C6 — Facebook Lead Ads XLSX Export Formula Injection — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `App\Exports\FacebookLeadAdsExport` wrote attacker-controlled Facebook Instant Form values (the lead's name, and every dynamic Instant Form field value) directly into exported XLSX cells with no sanitization or explicit typing. Confirmed by reading the installed `phpoffice/phpspreadsheet` 5.9.0 source directly: its `DefaultValueBinder` classifies any string beginning with `=` that successfully parses as a formula as `DataType::TYPE_FORMULA`, and `maatwebsite/excel`'s own `DefaultValueBinder` (used automatically since this export didn't implement `WithCustomValueBinder`) inherits that behavior unchanged — meaning a submitted value like `=HYPERLINK("http://evil.example/","Click here")` would be written as a real, executable formula cell, not just text that merely resembles one.

**Fix**: `FacebookLeadAdsExport` now implements `Maatwebsite\Excel\Concerns\WithCustomValueBinder`, composing (not inheriting) an internally-held `PhpOffice\PhpSpreadsheet\Cell\StringValueBinder` instance and delegating `bindValue()` directly to it, used with its default, unconfigured settings. This forces every cell in the export to `DataType::TYPE_STRING` unconditionally, regardless of a leading `=`, `+`, `-`, or `@`, with the exact original text preserved — no manual escaping, no regex, no new dependency (both classes are already vendored via the existing `maatwebsite/excel`/`phpoffice/phpspreadsheet` requirements). Protection is applied entirely at the export/rendering boundary — original lead data, the webhook ingestion path, and every model are completely unchanged.

**Verification**: proved at the actual generated-XLSX cell level, not just `map()`'s return array — the new tests render a real XLSX via `Excel::raw()`, read it back with `PhpOffice\PhpSpreadsheet\IOFactory::load()`, and assert the real `getDataType()`/`getValue()` of the resulting cells.
- `FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest.php`: **35/35 passed, 94 assertions** — 4 new tests (formula-like name, formula-like dynamic field, `+`/`-`/`@`-prefixed values, normal values all unchanged) alongside the 31 pre-existing tests, all passing unmodified.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **905/906 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite, reproduced identically across two separate runs: **1072/1075 passed, 2 failures, 1 skip** — the 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2, confirmed again unrelated to C6.
- Pint: clean (verified in both auto-fixing and non-modifying check modes). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero attributable to this fix. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: original lead data is unchanged — no webhook, model, or data-ingestion file was touched (`app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php`, `app/Models/Lead.php` both confirmed untouched); no migration or new dependency was added (`composer.json`/`composer.lock` confirmed unmodified); existing authorization (`Gate::authorize('viewAny', Lead::class)`) and tenant-scoped querying in `SourceLeads::downloadXlsx()` remain unchanged; `app/Exports/` contains only this one export, confirming no other export was affected.

**C6 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. C3, C4, C1, C2, and C5 all remain closed as previously recorded. `SendTaskAlertNotifications.php`'s separate, pre-existing C3-shaped JSON-path issue was explicitly out of scope and separately tracked at this closure — not resolved by C6's closure; it was later resolved as its own separate, standalone fix, see "SendTaskAlertNotifications PostgreSQL JSON-Path Compatibility Fix" below.

### C2 FOLLOW-UP — Merged Lead Webhook Continuity — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: a follow-up to C2, discovered but deliberately left open at that closure — the inbound Messenger, Instagram, and WhatsApp webhook "find existing lead" lookups in `LeadWebhookController` had no `merged_into_lead_id` exclusion. A merged-away (loser) lead's `source_data`/`phone` values are never modified by a merge, so these lookups kept matching the loser indefinitely — a customer's follow-up message after a merge would silently land back on the retired loser lead (mutating its legacy `message`/`source_data` columns) instead of the survivor, re-fragmenting exactly the history C2's own `Conversation`/`Message` reparenting fix was built to consolidate.

**Fix**: added `->whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')` to all three existing lookups — `facebookMessenger()`, `instagram()`, and `whatsapp()` — the same one-clause pattern already used throughout this module (C4, `Index::filteredQuery()`, `LeadAssignmentService`, `LeadReportingService`, `DetectDuplicateLeads`, `NurtureInactiveLeads`, `CreateLeadFromImport`). **Approved behavior**: a merged-away lead is now excluded from matching; when no unmerged lead matches the external identity, the existing webhook flow creates a **new** lead, exactly as if the contact had never been seen before. **No merge-chain or current-survivor resolution was introduced** — a follow-up message after a merge does not get routed onto the survivor; it starts a fresh lead. This was an explicit, deliberate design decision for this remediation, not an oversight.

**Verification**:
- `WebhookTest.php`: **27/27 passed, 94 assertions** — 3 new tests (one per channel), each using the real, unmodified `LeadMergeService::merge()` call to merge a lead away, then posting a follow-up webhook from the same external identity and asserting: a brand-new third lead is created; the loser's own `message`/`source_data` are byte-for-byte unchanged from their post-merge state; the tenant's lead count is exactly survivor + loser + new = 3. The three pre-existing "existing lead found, message appended" tests (unmerged case) continue to pass unmodified, proving normal follow-up behavior is untouched.
- Relevant merge/webhook regression (`LeadMergeTest`, `WebhookAttachmentTest`, `InboundMessageDualWriteTest`, `WebhookSignatureVerificationTest`, `WebhookProfileLookupTest`, `WebhookFacebookLeadAdsTest`): **46/46 passed**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **908/909 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite, reproduced identically across two separate runs: **1075/1078 passed, 2 failures, 1 skip** — the 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero attributable to this fix. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `createLead()`, `RecordInboundMessage`, `LeadMergeService`, and tenant/source resolution were all confirmed untouched; Facebook Lead Ads handling (a separate code path) was not affected.

**A separate, distinct future finding was discovered during this work and is explicitly NOT resolved by this closure**: `RecordInboundMessage::handle()`'s `Conversation` lookup is keyed only by `(tenant_id, lead_source_id, channel, external_thread_id)`, never by `lead_id`. When a follow-up webhook creates a new lead for a previously-merged contact, an already-existing `Conversation` row for that same channel identity (reparented onto the survivor by C2's own fix) is reused as-is by `firstOrCreate()`, while the new inbound `Message` row is written against the new lead — leaving that `Conversation` and its newest `Message` disagreeing about which lead owns them. This is a pre-existing characteristic of `RecordInboundMessage`'s design (the identical inconsistency already existed before this fix, just attributed to the old loser lead instead of the new one) — **not introduced or worsened by this remediation, and not addressed by it**. No code was changed for this. It remains open, tracked as its own separate, not-yet-approved future finding.

**C2 FOLLOW-UP is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The `RecordInboundMessage` Conversation/Message identity-mismatch finding above remains open and unresolved — do not treat it as fixed by this closure.

### M8 — Concurrent Lead-Merge Race (No Row Lock Before Already-Merged Guard) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `LeadMergeService::merge()`'s already-merged guard (`$survivor->merged_into_lead_id !== null || $loser->merged_into_lead_id !== null`) ran on already-loaded, in-memory Lead objects **before** `DB::transaction()` opened, with no row lock. Two concurrent merge requests for the identical survivor/loser pair (a double-click, a network retry, or two admins independently confirming the same duplicate-link banner) could both pass this check before either transaction committed. Reparenting itself was naturally idempotent on a second concurrent pass, but `mergeConsents()`'s unconditional `LeadConsentEvent::create()` was not — a race could produce a duplicated synthetic "merged" consent-audit entry.

**Root cause**: the already-merged guard was a plain PHP conditional on already-loaded attributes, not a database-enforced check — nothing forced a second concurrent call to see the first call's write before making its own decision.

**Remediation**: the guard was moved inside the existing `DB::transaction()`, behind a `lockForUpdate()` acquired on both the survivor and loser Lead rows together, in one query, ordered deterministically by `id` (`Lead::query()->whereIn('id', [$survivor->id, $loser->id])->orderBy('id')->lockForUpdate()->get()`). Since Lead IDs are ULIDs (lexicographically sortable), this produces a fixed, repeatable lock order for any given pair regardless of which is named survivor vs. loser, or which of two concurrent callers references them in which role — closing the deadlock risk a naive two-separate-lock-statements approach would have carried. The guard then re-checks `merged_into_lead_id` against the freshly-locked rows, not the original in-memory objects. No write occurs before the lock and guard. Everything after the guard (the `CHILD_MODELS` reparent loop, `unionTags()`, `mergeConsents()`, field-resolution, the loser's raw `merged_into_lead_id` update, `LeadDuplicateLink` handling) is completely unchanged — it continues to use the original `$survivor`/`$loser` objects' `id`/`tenant_id`, both immutable post-creation, so no object-state inconsistency was introduced.

**Verification**:
- `LeadMergeTest.php`: **13/13 passed, 55 assertions** — 12 pre-existing tests (already-merged rejection, cross-tenant rejection, rollback/atomicity, and the primary reparenting test covering C2's `Conversation`/`Message` handling) all re-verified passing unmodified, plus one new test proving `lockForUpdate()` with deterministic `ORDER BY "id" ASC` ordering is genuinely compiled into the query the real `LeadMergeService::merge()` call executes — verified directly against this app's real PostgreSQL connection, not merely inferred.
- Relevant merge/webhook regression (`DealMergeTest`, `TaskMergeTest`, `LeadCustomFieldMergeTest`, `LeadMergeTagUnionTest`, `LeadStatusAssignmentMergedFormTest`, `WebhookTest`): **61/61 passed**, confirming no regression to child reparenting, tag union, custom field merge, or C2 FOLLOW-UP's webhook merge-continuity behavior.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero attributable to either changed file.

**Known, honestly-documented limitation**: true concurrent-transaction blocking cannot be observed under SQLite (the test driver) — no row-level locking to exercise, and Laravel's SQLite grammar drops `FOR UPDATE` entirely. The new test proves the correct query shape is compiled (the same guarantee two prior locks in this codebase — `LeadSourceCosts\Index::save()` and C1's `LeadSources::delete()` — already rely on); it does not and cannot execute a genuine two-connection race end-to-end. The actual concurrency guarantee comes from `lockForUpdate()` compiling to a real `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the production PostgreSQL database — standard RDBMS behavior, not something this test suite can prove beyond the query shape itself.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: exactly two files changed (`app/Services/LeadMergeService.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadMergeTest.php`); `Show::confirmMerge()`, `LeadDuplicateLink`, `mergeConsents()`'s own logic, and `CHILD_MODELS` are all unchanged; tenant isolation and merge authorization (`LeadPolicy::merge()`) are unaffected; C2's `Conversation`/`Message` reparenting and C2 FOLLOW-UP's webhook continuity behavior both remain fully intact.

**Implementation commit**: recorded below, alongside this closure entry (implementation and closure committed together).

**M8 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The separately-tracked `RecordInboundMessage` Conversation/Message identity-mismatch finding remains open and unresolved — not addressed by this closure.

### M1 — LeadAssignmentService::assign() Non-Atomic Persistence Writes — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED (atomicity sub-problem only)**

**Finding**: `LeadAssignmentService::assign()` performed three separate, unguarded writes — `$lead->update(['assigned_to' => ..., 'assigned_at' => ...])`, `LeadAssignment::create(...)`, and `$assignee->update(['last_assigned_at' => now()])` — with no transaction around them. A failure partway through (e.g. the `LeadAssignment` insert failing on a constraint) could leave the lead marked assigned with no matching audit-history row, or leave `last_assigned_at` stale relative to the lead's actual assignment state. M1 also identified a second, distinct sub-problem — a fairness race in the round-robin/least-loaded auto-assignment picker queries, where the row to protect isn't known in advance since it's the outcome of the racing query itself — which was explicitly **not** approved for this cycle and remains open (see below).

**Fix (atomicity sub-problem only)**: the three writes in `assign()` are now wrapped in a single `DB::transaction()` closure, so a failure partway through rolls back everything already written in that same call. `event(new LeadAssigned(...))` remains outside the transaction, firing only after it commits — this preserves the exact behavioral guarantee the previous (transaction-less) code already provided (every write already completed before the event fired), and avoids a queued listener (`SendLeadAssignedNotification implements ShouldQueue`) ever observing pre-commit data. No assignee-selection, round-robin, least-loaded, or random-distribution logic was touched.

**Verification**:
- `LeadAssignmentTest.php`: **14/14 passed, 28 assertions** — 13 pre-existing tests (manual assignment, unassignment, board unassign, activity logging, broadcast on assign/unassign, real-time notification, resubmission dedup, merged-form no-renotify, auto-assign manual/round-robin/least-loaded/inactive-exclusion) all re-verified passing unmodified, plus one new test proving transaction atomicity: a never-persisted `TenantUser` passed as `$assignedBy` causes a real foreign-key constraint failure on `LeadAssignment.assigned_by` during the second write, strictly after the first write (`$lead->update()`) already ran in the same transaction — mirroring the established rollback-test pattern already used in `LeadMergeTest.php`. Confirmed the failure is a genuine DB constraint violation (SQLite foreign-key enforcement is on by default in this test suite) and that the test would fail without the transaction (the first write would otherwise autocommit before the second write's failure).
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **910/911 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- `tests/Feature/Notifications/`: **39/39 passed**.
- Pint: clean (verified in both auto-fixing and non-modifying check modes). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly; the 7 pre-existing errors already present in `LeadAssignmentService.php` (lines 77–120, inside the untouched `autoAssign()`/`pickRoundRobinUser()`/`pickLeastLoadedUser()`/`pickRandomUser()`) are unrelated to and not introduced by this change, since none fall inside the modified `assign()` method. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `unassign()`, `autoAssign()`, `pickRoundRobinUser()`, `pickLeastLoadedUser()`, `pickRandomUser()`, and `eligibleUsers()` are all byte-for-byte unchanged; no `lockForUpdate()`, `SKIP LOCKED`, or `Cache::lock()` was introduced anywhere in this file; tenant isolation and existing `LeadAssigned` event/notification/broadcast behavior are unaffected.

**M1's fairness race (round-robin/least-loaded selection under concurrent auto-assignment) is explicitly NOT resolved by this closure and must not be treated as fixed.** It remains a separate, open, not-yet-approved sub-problem — three candidate approaches (`lockForUpdate()` on the SELECT, `SKIP LOCKED`, or an app-level `Cache::lock()` mutex) were identified but no architecture decision has been made or approved.

**M1 (atomicity sub-problem) is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The fairness-race sub-problem remained open and unresolved at this closure — it was later resolved separately; see "M1 — Fairness Race" below.

### M2 — Status-Change + Status-Log Writes Not Atomic (3 Locations) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: three locations in `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Show.php` each performed a primary-record status write and its paired audit-log write as two separate, unguarded statements with no transaction: `updateStatus()`/`LeadStatusLog`, `changeDealStatus()`/`DealStatusLog`, and `changeTaskStatus()`/`TaskStatusLog`. A crash between the two statements (e.g. the log insert failing on a constraint) would leave the status change committed with no matching audit-log row, silently breaking the audit trail. MEDIUM severity, MEDIUM confidence per the original audit; fix risk assessed as NONE.

**Fix**: each of the three methods now wraps its primary write and paired audit-log write in one `DB::transaction()`. `updateStatus()`'s transaction additionally includes its existing status-change side-effect chain — the `LeadStatusLog`/`LeadDetailUpdateLog` audit rows, the `LeadScoringService::computeFor()` recompute, and the `LeadStatusChanged`/`LeadUpdated` events — mirroring the identical `DB::transaction()` boundary and event placement already established and shipped in `Index::applyBulkStatusChange()` for this exact same side-effect chain (that method's own docblock explicitly says it "Replicates `Show::updateStatus()`'s status-only side effects... in its own `DB::transaction()`"), so this fix brings `updateStatus()` in line with a pattern this codebase's own maintainers had already chosen and proven, not a new design. `changeDealStatus()`/`changeTaskStatus()` wrap only their existing `$deal->update()`/`$task->update()` + `DealStatusLog::create()`/`TaskStatusLog::create()` pair, since neither has any additional side effects to preserve. No assignee-selection, transition-rule, authorization, validation, or scoring logic was touched.

**Verification**:
- `LeadStatusTest.php` + `DealLifecycleTest.php` + `TaskLifecycleTest.php`: **35/35 passed, 100 assertions** — all pre-existing tests re-verified passing unmodified, plus one new rollback test per method. Each forces a genuine SQLite foreign-key constraint violation on the log table's `changed_by` column, by authenticating as a `TenantUser` instance carrying the same `tenant_id`/`role_id`/`user_type` as a real, already-authorized Client Admin but never actually persisted (`LeadPolicy::update()` passes since it only reads in-memory attributes and the `role` relation, never re-queries the acting user's own row) — mirroring the established never-persisted-FK-reference technique already used in `LeadMergeTest.php`/`LeadAssignmentTest.php`, adapted here via the authenticated user since these three methods source `changed_by` from `Auth::id()` rather than a parameter. Each test proves the primary write (already executed earlier in the same transaction) is rolled back and no audit-log row exists; `updateStatus()`'s test additionally proves no `LeadScoreLog` row exists either, since scoring runs later in the same transaction.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **913/914 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**, confirmed across two independent runs during final verification. A `KanbanBoardTest.php` failure observed once during implementation did not reproduce across 3 subsequent attempts (2 full-suite runs + 1 isolated run) and has zero code-path overlap with any method this fix touched — treated as pre-existing, unrelated test flakiness, not a regression from this change.
- `LeadScoringTriggersTest.php` + `LeadDetailPanelTest.php`: **17/17 passed, 40 assertions**.
- Pint: clean (verified in both auto-fixing and non-modifying check modes). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero new errors. One pre-existing error sits inside the new `updateStatus()` transaction closure (`Auth::user()`'s `PlatformUser|TenantUser|null` return type vs. `LeadStatusChanged`'s `TenantUser`-typed constructor parameter) — confirmed via diff that this exact statement is byte-for-byte unchanged from before the fix (only its indentation/nesting changed), and the identical `Auth::user()`-typing pattern recurs unchanged at roughly a dozen other untouched lines throughout this same file, confirming it is systemic and pre-existing, not introduced by this fix. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: authorization, tenant scoping, validation, transition rules, no-op/rejected-transition behavior, `first_contacted_at`/`closed_at`/`actual_close_date` bookkeeping, activity-feed behavior, and existing event/broadcast behavior are all unchanged beyond the approved transaction boundary; no `lockForUpdate()`, `SKIP LOCKED`, `Cache::lock()`, or retry/idempotency logic was introduced.

**A separate, distinct issue was discovered during this work and is explicitly NOT resolved by this closure**: `Index::moveLead()` (the Kanban board's drag-drop handler) has the identical non-atomic `LeadStatusLog::create()`-then-`$lead->update()` pattern — a fourth location with the same bug class, not named in M2's original finding text (which lists exactly the three `Show.php` locations above). It was **not modified**, is **not fixed**, and remained an open, separate, not-yet-approved observation at this closure — it was later resolved as its own separate pass; see "M2 — Index::moveLead() Status + LeadStatusLog Atomicity" below. This closure resolves M2 **only** for the three originally-named `Show.php` locations — it does not claim the application-wide status/log atomicity problem is fully resolved.

**M2 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `Index::moveLead()`'s equivalent gap remained open and unresolved at this closure — it was later resolved separately, see below; do not treat it as fixed by *this* closure. M1's fairness-race sub-problem also remained separately open at this point, untouched by this closure (later closed on its own — see the M1 fairness race entry above).

### M3 — LeadScoringService::computeFor() Score Write + LeadScoreLog Write Not Atomic Outside Merge — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `LeadScoringService::computeFor()`'s score write (`DB::table('leads')->update(['lead_score' => ..., 'lead_score_computed_at' => ...])`) and its conditional `LeadScoreLog` write (via `logIfChanged()`) were two separate, unguarded statements with no transaction of their own inside `computeFor()`. Atomicity was previously an accident of the caller: safe only when `computeFor()` happened to be invoked from inside an already-open transaction (e.g. `LeadMergeService::merge()`), and genuinely non-atomic for every standalone caller. MEDIUM severity, MEDIUM confidence per the original audit; fix risk assessed as NONE.

**Fix**: the score write and the `logIfChanged()` call are now wrapped in one `DB::transaction()`, centralized **inside `computeFor()` itself** rather than repeated at each call site — a different shape from M1/M2's per-call-site fixes, chosen because `computeFor()` is documented as "the single place that... writes `leads.lead_score`/... and `lead_score_logs`," making its own internal atomicity that method's own responsibility. Laravel's `DB::transaction()` safely nests: the 6 call sites that already run inside an outer transaction (`LeadMergeService::merge()`, `CreateManualLead`, `CreateLeadFromImport`, `Show::updateStatus()`, `Index::applyBulkStatusChange()`, `Index::applyBulkPriorityChange()`) simply have this participate in that same outer transaction with no behavior change; the standalone callers (`Show::recalculateScore()`, the queued `RecomputeLeadScoreOnReceipt` listener, and the `leads:recalculate-scores` scheduled sweep) now each get their own small, atomic transaction scoped to that one lead's recompute. No scoring algorithm, rule evaluation, clamping, deduplication logic, public method signature, or return-value behavior was touched.

**Verification**:
- `LeadScoringServiceTest.php`: **35/35 passed, 55 assertions** — 34 pre-existing tests re-verified passing unmodified, plus one new rollback test. It passes a never-persisted `TenantUser` directly as `computeFor()`'s own `$triggeredBy` parameter (no `Auth::actingAs()` needed, since this method takes it as an explicit argument rather than reading `Auth::id()`) — `lead_score_logs.triggered_by`'s real foreign key to `tenant_users` then fails on the `LeadScoreLog` insert, strictly after the score `UPDATE` already ran inside the same transaction. Proves the score write and `lead_score_computed_at` are both rolled back to `null` and no `LeadScoreLog` row exists; confirmed this would fail without the transaction, since an unwrapped `DB::table()->update()` autocommits immediately, before the log insert's FK violation ever throws.
- `LeadScoringTriggersTest.php`: **12/12 passed, 20 assertions**. `LeadMergeTest.php`: **13/13 passed, 55 assertions** — confirms no behavior change for the nested-transaction case inside `merge()`.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **914/915 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**, confirmed across two independent runs.
- Pint: clean (check-only mode). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero new errors; `LeadScoringService.php` carries zero PHPStan errors both before and after this change. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: exactly two files changed (`app/Services/LeadScoringService.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadScoringServiceTest.php`); the "no active rules → null score" early-return branch (a single, unpaired write) was correctly left untouched, since it has no atomicity gap to close.

**This closure resolves M3 only for `LeadScoringService::computeFor()`'s own internal score-write + score-log-write atomicity — it does not claim application-wide lead-scoring atomicity is solved.** A separate, distinct issue discovered during this work and explicitly **NOT** resolved by this closure: `Show::applyDetailChanges()` (shared by `updateContact()`/`updateCompany()`/`updateDeal()`) has its own separate, unrelated non-atomicity gap between `$this->lead->save()` and `LeadDetailUpdateLog::create()` — structurally similar in shape to M2's original findings but never named in M3's finding text (which names only `LeadScoringService.php:104-118`). It was **not modified**, is **not fixed**, and remained a distinct, not-yet-approved observation at this closure — it was later resolved as its own separate pass; see "Show::applyDetailChanges() — Lead + LeadDetailUpdateLog Atomicity" below.

**M3 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `Show::applyDetailChanges()`'s separate gap remained open and unresolved at this closure — it was later resolved separately, see below; do not treat it as fixed by *this* closure. `Index::moveLead()`'s M2-adjacent gap, M1's fairness-race sub-problem, and all C1–C6/M2/M8 closed work remain exactly as previously recorded, untouched by this closure.

### M4 — Duplicate Detection Query Optimization (LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor() Full-Tenant Scan) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor()` fetched every one of a tenant's non-merged, email-or-phone-populated leads unconditionally and compared each candidate's normalized email/phone against the target lead in PHP — an unbounded, unindexed scan triggered live on every single lead detail page view (`Show::possibleDuplicates()`), and once per lead in `DetectDuplicateLeads`'s sweep, effectively O(N²) per tenant if that command is ever run. The existing `(tenant_id, email)`/`(tenant_id, phone)` indexes were never usable by this query shape, since it filtered by existence only, never by the actual normalized value. CONFIRMED per the original audit; fix risk assessed LOW-MEDIUM depending on approach.

**Fix — Option C, application-computed normalized columns** (selected over SQL expression indexes and database-generated/stored columns after an architecture decision spike): added two ordinary, nullable columns, `leads.email_normalized`/`leads.phone_normalized`, kept in sync by a new `App\Observers\LeadObserver::saving()` hook that recomputes each one from the existing `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::normalizeEmail()`/`normalizePhone()` static methods whenever `email`/`phone` is dirty — the same, single normalization implementation used everywhere, never reimplemented in SQL. Indexed with `(tenant_id, email_normalized)`/`(tenant_id, phone_normalized)`. `detectFor()`'s candidate query now filters directly against these indexed columns instead of fetching the full tenant set. Deliberately **not** database-generated/`storedAs()` columns or raw SQL expression indexes: SQLite (this app's test driver) has no regex-replace equivalent for `normalizePhone()`'s digit-stripping, and a database's own `LOWER()` can disagree with `normalizeEmail()`'s multibyte-aware `Str::lower()` on Unicode edge cases — computing normalization once in PHP and storing the result guarantees the indexed lookup and the PHP matching logic can never diverge.

**Existing-data backfill**: migration `2026_09_20_060000_add_normalized_email_phone_to_leads_table` adds the two columns, then backfills every existing lead with a non-null email or phone (chunked, `chunkById(200)`) using the exact same `normalizeEmail()`/`normalizePhone()` static methods via direct attribute assignment (not a raw SQL `UPDATE`, and not `#[Fillable]`-mediated mass assignment — these two columns are deliberately excluded from `Lead`'s `#[Fillable]` list, the same established pattern as `identifier`/`lead_score`/`merged_into_lead_id`), then adds the two composite indexes only after the backfill completes. `down()` drops the indexes before the columns, with no raw SQL in either direction — independently verified reversible via a full `migrate:fresh` → `migrate:rollback` → `migrate` cycle outside the normal test suite.

**Verification**:
- `LeadDuplicateDetectionTest.php`: **18/18 passed, 43 assertions** — all 7 pre-existing correctness tests (cross-channel email/phone matching, tenant isolation, self-exclusion, no-email/no-phone exclusion, pair-dedup, dismissed-link non-reflagging, merged-lead exclusion) re-verified passing unmodified, plus 11 new tests covering normalization-compatibility edge cases (mixed case, whitespace, phone formatting, null/empty), `LeadObserver` synchronization on create/update, backfill-computation correctness, and a query-shape regression test proving the candidate query filters by `email_normalized`/`phone_normalized` rather than fetching the full tenant set.
- `LeadMergeTest.php`: **13/13 passed, 55 assertions** — confirms the survivor's field-resolution save (which can include `email`/`phone` merged from the loser) still correctly keeps the normalized columns in sync via the observer.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **925/926 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**, confirmed across multiple independent runs during implementation and final verification.
- Pint: clean (check-only mode). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero new errors; `LeadDuplicateDetectionService.php`, `LeadObserver.php`, and the new migration all carry zero PHPStan errors. `git diff --check`: clean.
- **Independent EXPLAIN evidence** (final verification pass): running the exact composed candidate-query shape against an isolated SQLite database produced `SEARCH leads USING INDEX leads_tenant_id_phone_normalized_index (tenant_id=?)` — direct confirmation the query planner uses the new composite index rather than a full table scan.

**Write-path audit** (required before implementation, per the approved plan): every location in the codebase that writes `leads.email`/`leads.phone` was audited — `Lead::create()` (CreateManualLead, CreateLeadFromImport, ProcessEmailLeadSource, and all four `LeadWebhookController` capture channels via its shared `createLead()` helper), `$lead->save()` (`Show::applyDetailChanges()`, `LeadMergeService`'s survivor field-resolution), and every raw `DB::table('leads')->update()` call site (`LeadScoringService.php` ×2, `LeadMergeService.php`, `NurtureInactiveLeads.php`, `Lead::markUpdateSeen()`) — confirmed none of the raw-update sites touch `email`/`phone`, and every genuine email/phone write goes through Eloquent, so `LeadObserver::saving()` catches all of them. No raw-update bypass was found; no additional fix beyond the observer hook was required.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `normalizeEmail()`/`normalizePhone()` themselves are unchanged; `pendingLinksFor()`, `recordPendingLink()`, and `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`'s public API are unchanged; `matchedFields()` now compares the candidate's pre-normalized columns directly rather than re-normalizing raw email/phone per candidate, which is behaviorally identical since the observer guarantees those columns always equal what re-normalizing would produce.

**Two separate, distinct observations were discovered during this work and are explicitly NOT resolved by this closure**: `CreateLeadFromImport::checkExistingLeadDuplicate()` uses its own narrower, raw-column equality duplicate check (not normalized-aware) that can miss a case/whitespace-variant match `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor()` would catch — a pre-existing inconsistency, not introduced or worsened by this closure. `DetectDuplicateLeads` (`leads:detect-duplicates`) was confirmed **not actually scheduled** in `routes/console.php` despite its own docblock's "nightly sweep" framing — this closure did not schedule it, and its own query now benefits from the same indexed lookup with zero changes needed on its side. Neither observation was acted on.

**M4 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `CreateLeadFromImport.php`'s narrower matching was later resolved as its own separate, standalone closure — see "CreateLeadFromImport::checkExistingLeadDuplicate()" below. `DetectDuplicateLeads`'s unscheduled status was later resolved as its own separate, standalone closure — see "DetectDuplicateLeads (`leads:detect-duplicates`) Scheduling Gap" below. `Show::applyDetailChanges()`'s M3-adjacent atomicity gap, `Index::moveLead()`'s M2-adjacent gap, M1's fairness-race sub-problem, and all C1–C6/M2/M3/M8 closed work remain exactly as previously recorded, untouched by this closure.

### M5 — Leads\Index::export() Unbounded/Unpaginated CSV Export — **COMPLETE / VERIFIED / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Finding**: `Leads\Index::export()` (`app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php:1236-1242`) called `->get()` over the full filtered lead set and materialized it in memory before streaming, giving the CSV export an unbounded PHP memory footprint — same category as the already-known, separately-scoped `FacebookLeadAdsExport` limitation. CONFIRMED. Fix risk assessed LOW (chunked/cursor fix) to MEDIUM (async/queued download, out of scope — see below).

**Fix**: `export()` no longer calls `->get()`. The filtered `Builder` (identical `filteredQuery()` → `applyAssignmentScope()` → `applyDropdownFilters()` chain, identical `->with(['source', 'assignedUser', 'status', 'estimatedBudgetCurrency', 'dealValueCurrency'])` eager loads) is captured unexecuted and passed into `streamDownload()`'s closure, where row-writing is now driven by `$query->chunkById(200, ...)` — the same chunking pattern already proven in production against this model by `RecalculateLeadScores`/`DetectDuplicateLeads`. Memory is now bounded to one 200-row page at a time regardless of tenant size. `Gate::authorize()`, every existing filter (status/priority/tag/assignedTo/date-range/source-type/search), tenant isolation, the `merged_into_lead_id` exclusion, the initial-load gate, non-admin assignment scope, all 5 eager-loaded relationships, the CSV header row/column set/per-row values, the `leads.csv` filename, the `text/csv` Content-Type, and the synchronous browser-download UX are all unchanged — the only production file touched was `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php`, scoped entirely to `export()`'s body.

**Known non-blocking behavior change, reviewed and accepted**: `chunkById(200)` imposes a deterministic ascending ULID/`id` order on CSV rows, whereas the previous un-ordered `->get()` result had implementation-defined ordering (whatever the database happened to return, with no `orderBy` anywhere in the query). Nothing in the codebase or test suite depended on the old ordering. Reviewed during M5 final verification and accepted as non-breaking — not a regression.

**Verification**:
- `LeadsExportTest.php` (new file, 12 tests covering successful export, exact headers, exact row contents with relationship-derived columns verified via `Model::preventLazyLoading()`, the "Unassigned" fallback, status/search/source-type filters, non-admin assignment scope, tenant isolation, merged-lead exclusion, a 250-row fixture spanning two `chunkById(200)` pages with exact-count/no-duplicate/no-gap assertions, and a query-log-based behavioral proof of exactly 2 bounded `limit 200` queries against `leads` rather than one unbounded `get()`): **12/12 passed, 20 assertions**.
- `LeadMergeTest.php` (unaffected by this change, re-run to confirm): **13/13 passed, 55 assertions**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **937/938 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures** (925-baseline from M4 + 12 new M5 tests + 1 = 938; consistent across the implementation and final-verification passes).
- Full application suite: **1104/1107 passed, 2 pre-existing failures, 1 skip** — the 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2, confirmed byte-for-byte identical (same test names, same lines, same message) during final verification.
- Pint: clean (`--test`, check-only mode). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, independently confirmed via a raw non-truncated run with zero errors in either changed file (`Index.php`, `LeadsExportTest.php`). `git diff --check`: clean.
- No blocker identified at implementation or final verification.

**Explicitly out of scope, not touched by this closure**: `SourceLeads::downloadXlsx()` / `App\Exports\FacebookLeadAdsExport` (the already-known sibling limitation the finding text itself distinguishes as a separate, intentionally-unpaginated design decision), async/queued export, export UI redesign, any other Lead Management file.

**M5 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression.

### SendTaskAlertNotifications PostgreSQL JSON-Path Compatibility Fix — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

**Not an independently-numbered forensic finding** — first discovered and documented as an explicitly out-of-scope sibling observation during C3/C4's closure (`SendLeadAlertNotifications`'s own, separately-numbered C3 finding), then re-confirmed as still open during C6's and M4's closures. Tracked and closed here as its own standalone item rather than folded into C3 (already closed, describing a different file) or given a new, unassigned C/M number.

**Finding**: `SendTaskAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` (`app/Console/Commands/SendTaskAlertNotifications.php:104`) queried `->where('data->task_id', $task->id)` against `notifications.data` — a plain `text` column (`database/migrations/2026_08_26_125420_create_notifications_table.php:18`, Laravel's stock notifications migration, unmodified), not `json`/`jsonb`. Laravel's `->` syntax compiles to Postgres's native JSON path operator (`->>`), which Postgres rejects outright against a `text` column. Re-verified directly against this app's real configured PostgreSQL connection (`optifin_erp`) during inspection: the query throws `SQLSTATE[42883]: operator does not exist: text ->> unknown` on every execution. SQLite (the test driver) enforces no such type check, which is why `tests/Feature/Notifications/SendTaskAlertNotificationsTest.php`'s existing 9 tests passed throughout, never catching it.

**Production impact confirmed during inspection**: `tasks:send-alert-notifications` is actively scheduled (`routes/console.php:15`, `dailyAt('08:00')`) — not dormant. `alreadyNotifiedToday()` is called before `notify()` for every recipient of every alert-active task, and the command has no `try`/`catch` anywhere in its call chain (`handle()` → `Tenant::query()->each()` → `sweep()` → `Task::query()->each()`), so the first alert-active task/recipient pair encountered in a run throws uncaught, aborting the entire `Tenant::query()->each()` loop — meaning every tenant processed after that point in the same run also received zero task alert notifications that day, not just the triggering tenant.

**Fix**: replaced the JSON-path condition with the exact portable pattern already proven twice elsewhere in this module (`NurtureInactiveLeads::alreadyNotifiedToday()`, and `SendLeadAlertNotifications::alreadyNotifiedToday()` since its own C3 closure): `->where('data', 'like', '%"task_id":"'.$task->id.'"%')`. `TaskAlertNotification::toArray()`'s `'task_id' => $this->task->id` confirms the stored JSON key; `Task` uses `HasUlids` (same fixed-length string ID shape as `Lead`), so the quoted-string LIKE match is structurally exact, with no possibility of a partial/false match between distinct task IDs.

**Verified directly against this app's real PostgreSQL connection** (read-only `->toSql()`/`exists()` check, no data written): the new query compiles to `select * from "notifications" where "data"::text like ?` and executes successfully with no exception.

**Verification**:
- `SendTaskAlertNotificationsTest.php` (existing file, unmodified): **9/9 passed, 13 assertions** — including the existing same-day-deduplication test, unchanged, now passing against the corrected query.
- No new test added — the existing same-task dedup test already covers the behavior this fix touches, matching the precedent already accepted when `SendLeadAlertNotifications`'s identical fix closed under C3 (which also added no dedicated "different entity ID" test); additionally structurally guaranteed safe by `Task`'s fixed-length ULID IDs.
- Full `tests/Feature/Notifications/`: **39/39 passed, 91 assertions**.
- All Task-related tests outside the Notifications directory (`TaskLifecycleTest`, `TaskAssignmentNotificationTest`, `TaskMergeTest`, `TaskManagementTest`): **48/48 passed, 122 assertions**.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly; the one error attributed to this file (line 62, an `each()`-callback parameter-type mismatch) is confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to the changed line 104. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `notifications.data` remains a `text` column — no migration, no schema change, no data transformation of any existing notification rows. `notifications.data`'s stored payload shape, tenant/recipient scoping, task alert business logic (`sweep()`, `recipients()`, alert-window calculation), scheduling, and queue configuration are all unchanged — the only production file touched was `app/Console/Commands/SendTaskAlertNotifications.php`, one line (104). No error-isolation mechanism was added — the fix resolves the query itself, which is what was causing the abort; broader per-recipient error isolation remains a separate, unapproved, unimplemented idea, not part of this fix. C1–C6, C2 FOLLOW-UP, M8, M1–M5, and every other audit finding were not touched.

**Risk level**: LOW.

**This fix is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. C5, C3, C4, and every other previously-closed finding remain exactly as previously recorded — this closure does not alter, reopen, or reinterpret any of them.

### M1 — Fairness Race (Round-Robin/Least-Loaded Auto-Assignment Selection Under Concurrency) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

The second of M1's two sub-problems (see M1's atomicity closure above, §23) — explicitly left open at that closure, pending an architecture decision. Closed here as its own dedicated pass, following the same full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval workflow as every other finding in this section.

**Problem**: `LeadAssignmentService::autoAssign()`'s candidate-selection queries (`pickRoundRobinUser()`/`pickLeastLoadedUser()`) ran unguarded, outside any transaction, before `assign()` opened its own separate one. Two near-simultaneous auto-assignments (e.g. two webhook-created leads processed by two concurrent queue workers) could both select the same "least recently assigned"/"least loaded" eligible user before either committed, sending both leads to the same person and skipping whoever should genuinely have been next. Confirmed via a real, isolated two-session PostgreSQL experiment during the architecture-brief phase, and via re-confirming the actual implemented queries produce byte-identical SQL to what was tested.

**Solution**: `autoAssign()` now wraps candidate selection and the assignment write in one outer `DB::transaction($closure, attempts: 3)`. `pickRoundRobinUser()`/`pickLeastLoadedUser()` now acquire `lockForUpdate()` on their winning candidate row and — critically — run their identical ranking query a **second** time before using the result: a single `lockForUpdate()` + `ORDER BY ... LIMIT 1` was empirically proven, against a real PostgreSQL connection, to return the same stale-ranked row after unblocking rather than automatically advancing to the next-best candidate; a second, fresh execution correctly reflects whatever committed while the first was blocked. `assign()`'s own transaction nests as a savepoint inside this new outer transaction — the same nesting `Index::applyBulkAssign()` already relied on in production, requiring no change to `assign()`'s core write logic. `attempts: 3` lets Laravel automatically retry the whole closure on a genuine PostgreSQL deadlock (a narrow but real possibility: this transaction locks a `TenantUser` row then a `Lead` row, while manual assignment locks a `Lead` row then a `TenantUser` row) — verified directly against Laravel's transaction-manager source that only concurrency-class errors (matching PostgreSQL's literal `deadlock detected` message) trigger a retry, never a genuine application failure.

**Round-robin and least-loaded are both protected** by the identical locking + mandatory-re-select mechanism — confirmed structurally identical in locking target despite least-loaded's ranking depending on a live `withCount()` over `leads` rather than a column on the locked row itself, since the correctness requirement (read fresh after any block) is satisfied the same way regardless of what feeds the ordering. **Random distribution intentionally remains without fairness locking** — `pickRandomUser()` is untouched, still a single unlocked query, since random distribution has no ordering guarantee to protect; two concurrent random picks landing on the same user is an acceptable, unremarkable outcome, not a fairness bug.

**`LeadAssigned` now dispatches via `DB::afterCommit()`** instead of a direct `event()` call immediately after `assign()`'s own transaction — the one necessary touch to `assign()` itself, required so a queued listener (`SendLeadAssignedNotification`) can never observe not-yet-durable data once `assign()` is called from inside `autoAssign()`'s new outer transaction. `DB::afterCommit()` defers the callback until the outermost transaction genuinely commits, regardless of how many savepoints are nested inside it, and fires immediately when there is no surrounding transaction — identical timing to the previous direct call for every manual-assignment caller (`Show::assign()`). As a verified, zero-risk side effect, this also correctly closes the identical pre-existing latent gap in the three other callers that already nest `assign()` inside their own outer transaction (`Index::applyBulkAssign()`, `CreateLeadFromImport`, `CreateManualLead`) — none of which were otherwise touched.

**No schema migration was required or made** — no new column, index, or constraint; `tenant_users.last_assigned_at` and the existing `leads`/`lead_assignments` schema are used exactly as before.

**Verification**:
- `LeadAssignmentTest.php` (14 pre-existing + 4 new — proving round-robin's and least-loaded's candidate query runs twice per call, random's runs only once, and `LeadAssigned` is not dispatched if the transaction `assign()` ran inside is rolled back): **18/18 passed, 36 assertions**.
- Assignment-adjacent regression suite (`DealManagementTest`, `BulkActionsTest`, `TaskManagementTest`, `CreateLeadFromImportTest`, `LeadScoringTriggersTest`, `WebhookTest`, `WebsiteFormSubmissionTest`, `LeadEventsTest`): **141/141 passed, 388 assertions** at implementation; the three files covering the other nested `assign()` callers re-verified independently at final verification: **55/55 passed, 126 assertions**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/` + `tests/Feature/Notifications/` + `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/`: **996/999 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 new failures**, reproduced identically across implementation and final-verification passes.
- The 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated since Feature #2 — verified against the known baseline at multiple independent points across this session, not merely labeled.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, independently re-confirmed via a raw non-truncated run with zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.
- **Real PostgreSQL verification**: the two-session concurrency mechanism itself (a blocked-then-unblocked `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE ... LIMIT 1` not auto-advancing to the next candidate, and a second fresh execution correctly doing so) was empirically validated during the architecture-brief phase against an isolated scratch table; the actual implemented `pickRoundRobinUser()`/`pickLeastLoadedUser()` queries were separately re-confirmed to produce byte-identical SQL (including `for update`) against the real `pgsql` connection. A full end-to-end run against real tenant/lead/user data (wrapped in a transaction explicitly rolled back, zero residual rows independently confirmed afterward) verified round-robin and least-loaded both pick correctly, `LeadAssignment` history writes correctly, and `DB::afterCommit()`'s rollback-prevents-the-event behavior holds against the real driver, not just SQLite.

**Two low-severity characteristics accepted at closure, not unresolved defects**:
1. The bounded (single) re-select pattern was validated for the realistic contention scenario tested (two near-simultaneous auto-assignments) — it does not guarantee correctness under three-or-more-way simultaneous contention on the same tenant, a scope boundary accepted when the architecture was approved, not a discovered gap.
2. The rare cross-path deadlock window (auto-assignment locks `TenantUser`-then-`Lead`; manual assignment locks `Lead`-then-`TenantUser`) is mitigated via Laravel's automatic transaction retry rather than eliminated by a global lock-order redesign — a deliberate, approved minimal-mitigation choice, not a gap left open.

**M1 is now fully CLOSED — both the atomicity sub-problem and the fairness-race sub-problem.** Do not reopen either unless a future change causes a real regression. `Index::moveLead()`'s separate, unrelated non-atomic write pattern (noted during M2's work) remained open at this closure and was not resolved by it — it was later resolved as its own separate pass; see "M2 — Index::moveLead() Status + LeadStatusLog Atomicity" below.

### M2 — Index::moveLead() Status + LeadStatusLog Atomicity — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

A separate, distinct finding from the original, already-closed M2 (the three `Show.php` locations above) — discovered during that closure's own work and explicitly left unfixed there. Closed here as its own dedicated pass, following the same full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval workflow as every other finding in this section.

**Problem**: `Index::moveLead()` (the Kanban board's drag-and-drop status handler, and the mobile "move to"/"advance" menu) performed its `LeadStatusLog::create()` write and its `$lead->update($updates)` write as two separate, unguarded statements with no transaction. A failure partway through could leave the two disagreeing — since this method writes the log **first** and the lead **second** (the reverse order from `Show::updateStatus()`'s save-then-log), the specific symptom here would be a phantom `LeadStatusLog` row recording a transition the lead's own `status_id` never actually reflects, the mirror image of the original three locations' failure mode but the same underlying bug class.

**Fix**: the `LeadStatusLog` write, the `Lead` status write, and the existing `LeadStatusChanged`/`LeadUpdated` events are now wrapped in one `DB::transaction()` — mirroring the identical boundary already shipped for this exact same status-change side-effect chain in `Show::updateStatus()` and, in this same file, `applyBulkStatusChange()`. **Write order was preserved exactly** (`LeadStatusLog` first, `Lead` update second) — no reordering was needed for atomicity. Both events fire from inside the transaction, not deferred via `DB::afterCommit()`: verified that a queued job row inserted on the same connection while a transaction is open is invisible to any other connection until that transaction actually commits (standard PostgreSQL MVCC), so a queued worker can never observe pre-commit data here — the same reasoning `Show::updateStatus()`'s own M2 fix already relies on. No callers wrap `moveLead()` in their own transaction (it's only ever invoked from the Blade layer), so no nested-transaction/savepoint concern applies, unlike `LeadAssignmentService`. No scoring recompute was added — `moveLead()`'s pre-existing lack of a `LeadScoringService::computeFor()` call (unlike `Show::updateStatus()`) is a separate, unrelated, not-yet-approved observation, explicitly out of scope and untouched.

**Deterministic rollback test — including a self-caught test-design correction**: the established "never-persisted `TenantUser`" FK-violation technique (used by the original M2/M3/M8 rollback tests) was tried first, but verified empirically to be invalid here: because `moveLead()` writes the log **first**, that technique forces the FK violation on the *first* write, before anything has been persisted — a test built that way was confirmed, by temporarily reverting the production fix and re-running it, to pass identically against the **unfixed** code, proving it was tautological. The test was redesigned: a real (non-queued) `Event::listen()` handler on `LeadStatusChanged` — fired inside the transaction, strictly after both writes have already executed — deliberately throws, forcing the failure to occur *after* both persistence operations. This version was verified both directions: it **fails** against the unfixed code (the status change persisted despite the later exception) and **passes** against the fixed code — a genuine, deterministic atomicity proof, not a tautology.

**Verification**:
- `KanbanBoardTest.php` (23 tests: 22 pre-existing + 1 new rollback test): **23/23 passed**.
- `LeadPipelineAdvanceTest.php`: **8/8 passed**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: run three times — **0 failures on two of the three runs**; the first run showed one non-reproducing failure in an unrelated test (`'a client user's board search does not match location, win probability, or deal value...'`, `KanbanBoardTest.php` line 75, `FiltersLeadsQuery::applySearchFilter()`'s search matching) with zero code-path overlap with `moveLead()`, matching — by file, test shape, and prior description — the exact pre-existing Faker-data-collision flakiness pattern **this same file's original M2 closure already documented**. **Recorded here as pre-existing, unrelated test flakiness, not an M2 defect** — not fixed, per instruction.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: tenant scoping, authorization, the invalid-status-ignored behavior, the same-status no-op guard, `first_contacted_at`/`closed_at` bookkeeping, the existing write order, the existing events, and existing UI/toast behavior are all unchanged beyond the approved transaction boundary. No schema/migration change, no new dependency, no public method signature change, no product/business decision was required. `Show::applyDetailChanges()`, `CreateLeadFromImport`, `DetectDuplicateLeads`, `RecordInboundMessage`, `messageTimeline()`, `LeadScoringService`, and every other audit finding were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The original, already-closed M2 (`Show.php`'s three locations) is unaffected and remains exactly as previously recorded — this closure does not alter, reopen, or reinterpret it.

### Show::applyDetailChanges() — Lead + LeadDetailUpdateLog Atomicity — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not itself an independently-numbered M3 — a separate, distinct finding discovered during the original M3 closure's own work (`LeadScoringService::computeFor()` score/score-log atomicity) and explicitly left unfixed there, tracked and closed here under its own descriptive name, following the same full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval workflow as every other finding in this section.

**Problem**: `Show::applyDetailChanges()` (shared by `updateContact()`/`updateCompany()`/`updateDeal()`) performed `$this->lead->save()` and `LeadDetailUpdateLog::create()` as two separate, unguarded statements with no transaction. A failure between them (e.g. the log insert failing on a constraint) could leave a lead's contact/company/deal-field change persisted with no matching audit-log row — structurally the same bug class as the original M2 (`Show.php`'s three locations) and `Index::moveLead()`, but for a fourth/fifth code path never named in either of those findings' own text.

**Fix**: the lead save, its `LeadDetailUpdateLog` write, the conditional `LeadScoringService::computeFor()` recompute (+ `refresh()`/`unset($this->latestScoreLog)`), and `event(new LeadUpdated(...))` are now wrapped in one `DB::transaction()` — mirroring the identical boundary already shipped for this same shape in `Show::updateStatus()`. `LeadScoringService::computeFor()` nests safely inside the new outer transaction with zero changes to that service: its own docblock (from its M3 fix) already explicitly documents `Show::updateStatus()` as an existing caller doing exactly this same nesting. `LeadUpdated` fires from inside the transaction, not deferred — verified that a queued job row inserted on the same connection while a transaction is open is invisible to any other connection until that transaction actually commits, the same reasoning already relied on by `Show::updateStatus()`'s and `Index::moveLead()`'s own fixes. `updateContact()`, `updateCompany()`, `updateDeal()`, `notifyIfDateScheduled()`, and `LeadScoringService` itself were not modified.

**Deterministic rollback test, verified in both directions**: `applyDetailChanges()`'s write order (save-then-log) matches `Show::updateStatus()`'s own already-fixed order exactly, so the established never-persisted-`TenantUser` FK-violation technique (from `LeadStatusTest.php`/`LeadMergeTest.php`/`LeadAssignmentTest.php`) applied directly with no adaptation needed — the FK violation lands on `LeadDetailUpdateLog.changed_by`, the second write, strictly after `$this->lead->save()` has already run. Verified empirically both ways, not assumed: run against the unfixed code, the test failed (`'Original Name'` vs actual `'New Name'` — the contact-detail change leaked through despite the later exception); run against the fixed code, the test passed (5/5 assertions) — a genuine, deterministic atomicity proof.

**Verification**:
- `LeadDetailEditingTest.php` (26 tests: 25 pre-existing + 1 new rollback test): **26/26 passed, 91 assertions**.
- `LeadScoringTriggersTest.php`: **12/12 passed**. `LeadCustomFieldEditTest.php`: **9/9 passed**. `LeadMeetingFollowUpAlertActionsTest.php`: **18/18 passed**. `LeadDetailPanelTest.php`: **5/5 passed**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **943/944 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**, reproduced cleanly across multiple runs during implementation and final verification.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, independently confirmed zero errors in either changed file via a raw non-truncated run. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `fill()`/dirty-field detection, the no-change early return, the existing `LeadDetailUpdateLog` data/`changed_by` behavior, the scoring trigger condition and `computeFor()` reason string, the `LeadUpdated` event, `updateContact()`/`updateCompany()`/`updateDeal()`/`notifyIfDateScheduled()` behavior, validation/authorization, and existing UI behavior are all unchanged beyond the approved transaction boundary. No schema/migration change, no new dependency, no public method signature change, no product/business decision was required. `LeadScoringService`, `CreateLeadFromImport`, `DetectDuplicateLeads`, `RecordInboundMessage`, `messageTimeline()`, and every other audit finding were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The original M2 (`Show.php`'s three locations), `Index::moveLead()`'s separately-closed gap, and M3 (`LeadScoringService::computeFor()`) are all unaffected and remain exactly as previously recorded — this closure does not alter, reopen, or reinterpret any of them.

### CreateLeadFromImport::checkExistingLeadDuplicate() — Normalized-Value Comparison Against Non-Normalized Lead Columns — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — a separate, distinct observation discovered during M4's own closure work (`LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor()` query optimization) and explicitly left unresolved there (see M4's closure text above). Selected and closed via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval pass, independent of any other finding.

**Problem**: `checkExistingLeadDuplicate()` correctly normalized the CSV row's incoming email/phone via `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::normalizeEmail()`/`normalizePhone()`, but then compared those normalized values against existing leads' **raw** `email`/`phone` columns (`->where('email', $normalizedEmail)` / `->where('phone', $normalizedPhone)`) instead of the tenant-indexed `email_normalized`/`phone_normalized` columns `LeadObserver`/`LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor()` already treat as authoritative. Any existing lead whose stored email/phone differed only in case or formatting from an imported CSV row (e.g. `Dup@Example.COM` vs. `dup@example.com`, or `(555) 123-4567` vs. `555-123-4567`) was silently missed — the CSV row was imported as a brand-new Lead instead of being recorded as a `Duplicate`, defeating the purpose of the check for any real-world CSV whose formatting doesn't already exactly match what's on file.

**Root cause**: the two lookup queries were written before `email_normalized`/`phone_normalized` existed as the established comparison target (they predate the M4 migration) and were never updated to use them once M4 introduced that pattern elsewhere in the module.

**Fix**: changed exactly the two lookup queries in `checkExistingLeadDuplicate()` — `->where('email', $normalizedEmail)` → `->where('email_normalized', $normalizedEmail)` and `->where('phone', $normalizedPhone)` → `->where('phone_normalized', $normalizedPhone)` — in `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateLeadFromImport.php`. Nothing else in the method changed: `tenant_id` filtering, the `merged_into_lead_id` exclusion, both null guards, the email-then-phone match order, the email-matches-A/phone-matches-B conflict rejection, `duplicate_of_lead_id` assignment, and `LeadImportRow`/import-counter persistence are all untouched. Confirmed a strict improvement, not just a correctness fix: the raw `email`/`phone` columns carry no index at all, while `email_normalized`/`phone_normalized` already carry the `(tenant_id, email_normalized)`/`(tenant_id, phone_normalized)` composite indexes added by M4's migration.
