No migration, schema change, or backfill was needed — `LeadObserver::saving()` (verified still the sole write path for `email`/`phone` across every Lead-mutating call site, per M4's own write-path audit) guarantees every current and historical lead's normalized columns are already correct.

**Regression tests, verified in both directions**: two new tests added to `CreateLeadFromImportTest.php` — an existing lead with a differently-**cased** email (`Dup@Example.COM`) matched by an imported row's differently-cased email (`dup@example.com`), and an existing lead with a differently-**formatted** phone (`(555) 123-4567`) matched by an imported row's differently-formatted phone (`555-123-4567`). Both were confirmed to **fail** against the unfixed code (`Failed asserting that 2 is identical to 1` — a second, duplicate Lead was actually created in both cases) and to **pass** against the fixed code. The existing duplicate-detection tests in the same file were confirmed to use fixture data that is coincidentally already normalized-equal on both sides (e.g. identical-case emails, already-digits-only phone numbers) — which is exactly why the defect shipped unnoticed; none of them exercised the actual case/formatting-mismatch scenario, and none needed modification for this fix.

**Verification**:
- `CreateLeadFromImportTest.php` (27 tests: 25 pre-existing + 2 new): **25/25 passed** pre-existing, **27/27 passed** total, 80 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **945/946 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**, reproduced across multiple runs during implementation and final verification.
- Full application suite: **1112/1115 passed** — the only 2 stably-reproducing failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs. actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated throughout this document; confirmed identical and unchanged across 3 independent full-suite runs at final verification. One additional, **non-reproducing** failure (`InboundMessageDualWriteTest::Lead::messageTimeline()...`) appeared on 1 of those runs, passed cleanly in isolation, and did not recur on 2 subsequent full-suite reruns — recorded here as the same class of pre-existing test-ordering/Faker-collision flakiness already documented for `KanbanBoardTest.php` under `Index::moveLead()`'s closure above, not a regression from this fix (this change touches only CSV-import duplicate detection; `Lead::messageTimeline()`, `Message`, and `Conversation` were not modified).
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: tenant scoping, the `merged_into_lead_id` exclusion, null handling, match order, conflict-rejection behavior, `duplicate_of_lead_id` assignment, `LeadImportRow` persistence, and import counters are all unchanged beyond the two approved column-name substitutions. No schema/migration change, no new dependency, no public method signature change, no product/business decision was required. `checkInFileDuplicate()`, `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`, `LeadObserver`, CSV validation/column mapping, and every other audit finding were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `DetectDuplicateLeads`'s unscheduled status (the other observation from M4's closure) was later resolved as its own separate, standalone closure — see "DetectDuplicateLeads (`leads:detect-duplicates`) Scheduling Gap" below. The `RecordInboundMessage` Conversation/Message identity-mismatch gap, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, and DE1 remain explicitly deferred exactly as previously recorded.

### Tenant Leads\Index::deleteSource() — Check-Then-Act (TOCTOU) Race on Facebook Lead Ads Source Deletion — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — an informally-shaped ("M7-shaped") observation with no original audit finding text in-repo, independently re-verified against the current codebase and selected via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval pass, following the exact structure already used for `CreateLeadFromImport::checkExistingLeadDuplicate()` above.

**Finding/root cause**: `Tenant\Leads\Index::deleteSource()` (the tenant-side, Facebook-Lead-Ads-only exception to Lead Sources otherwise being Platform-Admin-managed) evaluated `$source->leads()->exists()` and `$source->costs()->exists()`, then called `$source->delete()` as a separate, later, unguarded statement — with no transaction or lock preventing a concurrent Facebook Lead Ads `leadgen` webhook from inserting a new Lead or Cost row for that same source in between. Verified from the actual migrations (not inferred from models): `leads.source_id` is `cascadeOnDelete()`, so a race on the leads-check would **silently cascade-delete** a just-created Lead (and its own children) with zero error signal anywhere; `lead_source_costs.lead_source_id` is `restrictOnDelete()`, so a race on the costs-check would instead throw an uncaught `QueryException`. Structurally identical to the already-closed C1 (`Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()`), which had this exact shape and was fixed the same way — this tenant-side sibling was never touched by C1's own fix. `lead_imports`/`conversations` dependents (which C1 also guards) were confirmed structurally impossible for a `FacebookLeadAds`-type source (CSV imports and inbound-message Conversations are only ever created for other source types), confirming this method's narrower two-check scope is correct by design, not an oversight.

**Fix**: wrapped the entire method body (lookup → authorization → leads-check → costs-check → delete → success toast) in one `DB::transaction()`, and added `->lockForUpdate()` to the existing tenant-scoped `LeadSource` lookup — the identical "lock the stable parent row" idiom already used by C1 and by the pre-existing `LeadSourceCosts\Index::save()`. Authorization was moved inside the transaction (it must run after the row is resolved, since `LeadSourcePolicy::delete()` needs the actual `$source` instance) — confirmed safe because that policy's three conditions (tenant match, `isClientAdmin()`, module permission) are static and never depend on racy dependent-count state, so this changes nothing about what is authorized or in what order relative to the lookup. Check order, both toast messages on every branch, and the success-path delete/toast are all otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged. `Index::delete()` (Lead deletion, a separate, similarly-shaped but out-of-scope method) was not touched.

**PostgreSQL locking verification**: the exact production query-builder chain was run directly against this app's real `pgsql` connection (via `artisan tinker`), independently, twice — once during implementation and once again during final verification — both times compiling to `select * from "lead_sources" where "tenant_id" = ? and "type" = ? and "lead_sources"."id" = ? limit 1 for update`, confirming the lock lands on the parent `lead_sources` row and that `FOR UPDATE` is genuinely present on the database engine this app actually runs on.

**SQLite test-suite limitation, discovered and corrected during implementation**: an initial attempt to strengthen the new regression test with a `for update`-substring assertion was based on a `tinker` check that had (unnoticed at first) run against the app's default `pgsql` connection rather than the test suite's SQLite connection. Direct query-log inspection against the actual SQLite test connection proved Laravel's SQLite grammar drops the lock clause entirely for `lockForUpdate()` — confirmed empirically by temporarily removing `->lockForUpdate()` from the source and observing the strengthened assertion still passed (a genuine tautology under SQLite). The test was corrected to the same honest, already-established technique used twice before in this codebase (`PlatformLeadSourceDeletionTest.php`, `LeadSourceCostCatalogTest.php`): assert only that the locked lookup's `"lead_sources"."id" = ?`-qualified query ran and that the source was actually deleted — proving the code path is intact, not claiming to prove the lock's presence at the SQL-text level under SQLite. True cross-connection row-lock blocking cannot be observed in this app's SQLite-in-memory test environment (a second genuinely separate connection to the same in-memory database isn't possible) either way — this is the same accepted, explicitly-documented limitation both precedent tests already carry.

**Verification**:
- New test (`FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest.php`): **1/1 passed**.
- `FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest.php` + `LeadSourceCostCatalogTest.php` + `PlatformLeadSourceDeletionTest.php` (C1's own file): **61/61 passed**, 173 assertions — reproduced identically during implementation and final verification.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **946/947 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite: **1113/1116 passed** — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches documented as pre-existing and unrelated throughout this document; reproduced identically across independent runs at implementation and final verification, zero new failures either time.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: authorization behavior, check order, both danger-toast messages, the success toast, and the delete itself are all unchanged beyond the approved transaction boundary and lock. No schema/migration change, no new index, no policy/model/UI change, no public method signature change, no product/business decision was required. `Index::delete()`, `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()` (C1), `LeadSourceCosts\Index::save()`, and every other audit finding were not touched.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php` (`deleteSource()` only), `tests/Feature/Leads/FacebookLeadAdsSpreadsheetTest.php` (one new test).

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `Index::delete()`'s similarly-shaped, unevaluated gap remains a separate, not-yet-approved observation — not resolved by this closure. The `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case and DE1 remain explicitly deferred exactly as previously recorded. `DetectDuplicateLeads`'s unscheduled status and the `RecordInboundMessage` Conversation/Message identity-mismatch gap were both later closed as their own separate findings — see "RecordInboundMessage Conversation/Lead Identity Mismatch / Webhook Lead-Resolution Continuity" and "DetectDuplicateLeads (`leads:detect-duplicates`) Scheduling Gap" below.

### RecordInboundMessage Conversation/Lead Identity Mismatch / Webhook Lead-Resolution Continuity — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID. This closure **absorbs two previously-separate observations into one finding**, after inspection proved they are the same underlying defect viewed from two ends of one causal chain: the `RecordInboundMessage` Conversation/Message identity-mismatch gap (flagged during C2 FOLLOW-UP's work) and the "webhook lead-resolution continuity" observation (flagged during C2's own work, and whose original description in this document was itself stale — see the correction inline above). Closed via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding**: `App\Actions\Tenant\RecordInboundMessage::handle()`'s own docblock flagged that a `Conversation` found (not created) by `firstOrCreate()` never has its `lead_id` re-synced when the caller's currently-resolved `$lead` differs from the Conversation's stored owner, and that "a lead-merge could in principle change which lead a conversation should now point at — not handled here."

**Root cause, proven upstream, not inside `RecordInboundMessage.php`**: `RecordInboundMessage` never resolves a Lead itself — it only receives whatever `$lead` the calling webhook handler already resolved. The actual defect is in `LeadWebhookController`'s three "find existing lead" lookups (Messenger/Instagram/WhatsApp): each already correctly excludes a merged-away loser (`whereNull('merged_into_lead_id')`), but none of them has any way to find the *survivor* instead, because `source_data->facebook_user_id`/`source_data->instagram_user_id`/`phone` are deliberately never carried onto a survivor by a merge (`LeadMergeService::MERGEABLE_FIELDS` explicitly excludes `source_data`/pipeline fields as "the survivor's own ongoing pipeline state, which a merge must never silently overwrite"). Concretely traced and reproduced: sender X's first message creates Lead A and Conversation C1 (`lead_id = A`); an admin merges A into survivor B, and `LeadMergeService`'s existing `CHILD_MODELS` reparenting (C2, unchanged by this fix) correctly updates C1 to `lead_id = B`; sender X's *next* message can no longer be matched to A (excluded) or B (no matching `source_data`/`phone`), so the webhook fell through to "create a new Lead," producing a spurious Lead D — while `RecordInboundMessage` still found C1 (by its stable `external_thread_id`) and wrote the new `Message` with `lead_id = D`, not `B`. Consequence, verified from actual code: `Lead::messageTimeline()`/`Show::outboundMessages()` both query `$this->lead->messages()` (by `lead_id`, not `conversation_id`), so this new message was **silently invisible on the correct survivor's own timeline**, permanently misattributed to an undetected duplicate Lead D (Facebook/Instagram DMs carry no email/phone, so `LeadDuplicateDetectionService` could never flag D against B).

**Approved architecture**: an existing `Conversation`'s `lead_id` is the authoritative owner of an already-established external thread — it must take precedence over a fresh `source_data`/`phone`-based lookup, and must never be re-synced or overwritten based on whatever the caller happens to resolve independently.

**Fix — upstream in `LeadWebhookController.php` only**: added one new private helper, `existingLeadForConversation(LeadSource $source, MessageChannel $channel, ?string $externalThreadId): ?Lead`, querying `Conversation` by the exact `(tenant_id, lead_source_id, channel, external_thread_id)` identity tuple (the same tuple backing `conversations_identity_unique`) and returning its `lead` relation, or `null` for a missing/empty/`'unknown'` thread id (mirroring `recordInboundMessage()`'s own existing guard) or when no Conversation exists yet. All three "existing lead" resolution lines (Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp) now try this helper first, falling back to the original `source_data`/`phone` lookup only when no Conversation match exists — applied identically to all three channels. When neither resolves, behavior is completely unchanged: fall through to creating a new Lead exactly as before.

**`RecordInboundMessage.php` intentionally left unchanged**: once the upstream `$lead` is already correct (equal to whatever Lead the Conversation is currently tied to, whenever one exists), `RecordInboundMessage::handle()` needs no modification — its `firstOrCreate()`/`Message::create()` calls now always receive a `$lead` that already matches the Conversation's own `lead_id`. Its own docblock's caveat becomes moot rather than something to patch in place. Confirmed: blindly syncing `Conversation.lead_id` to a freshly-resolved `$lead` inside `RecordInboundMessage` was explicitly rejected as unsafe during Impact Analysis — it would have silently overwritten the correct, merge-reparented survivor with whatever spurious new Lead the old upstream bug produced, actively destroying correct attribution rather than preserving it.

**`LeadMergeService` intentionally left unchanged**: its existing `CHILD_MODELS` reparenting of `Conversation`/`Message` at merge time (C2) was already correct and is the very mechanism this fix relies on as its authoritative source of truth — no change was needed or made.

**No historical backfill, no schema/migration change**: this fix is forward-only, exactly like C2's own precedent — Conversations/Messages already misattributed by this gap before the fix shipped are not corrected retroactively; a separate, explicitly-approved backfill would be required for that, and none was requested or performed. `conversations_identity_unique`/`messages_external_id_unique` do not include `lead_id`, so no constraint is at risk from this fix's read-only lookup change.

**Obsolete characterization tests corrected**: `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php` contained 3 pre-existing tests (one per channel) explicitly named and asserting the *old, buggy* behavior — `'a [channel] message/dm from a sender ... whose lead has since been merged away creates a new lead instead of reviving the loser'`, asserting `Lead::count() === 3`. These were rewritten in place (same setup, same merge call, same "loser's own fields stay untouched" assertions preserved) to assert the corrected behavior and renamed to `'a [channel] message/dm from a sender ... whose lead has since been merged continues onto the survivor instead of creating a new lead'`, asserting: no spurious new Lead (`count === 2`), exactly one Conversation for the thread, the Conversation still points at the survivor, and the new `Message`'s `lead_id` equals the Conversation's `lead_id`.

**Regression tests, verified in both directions**: 3 new tests added to `InboundMessageDualWriteTest.php` (one per channel, plus a distractor-tenant assertion in the Messenger test proving tenant isolation) — each seeds a pre-merge Lead+Conversation, performs a real merge via `LeadMergeService`, then posts a second real webhook payload for the same sender/thread. All 3 were confirmed to **fail** against the unfixed code (`Failed asserting that 3 is identical to 2` — a spurious Lead was created) and to **pass** against the fixed code.

**Verification**:
- New tests (`InboundMessageDualWriteTest.php`): **3/3 passed**, 23 assertions — confirmed failing before the fix, passing after.
- `InboundMessageDualWriteTest.php` + `LeadMergeTest.php` + `LeadMergeTagUnionTest.php`: **31/31 passed**, 129 assertions.
- Full webhook test group (`WebhookAttachmentTest`, `SubscribeLeadgenWebhookTest`, `WebhookFacebookLeadAdsTest`, `WebhookTest`, `WebhookSignatureVerificationTest`, `WebhookProfileLookupTest`): **56/56 passed**, 213 assertions — includes the 3 corrected `WebhookTest.php` tests.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **949/950 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite: **1116/1119 passed** — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs. actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated throughout this document; unchanged in identity and count, confirmed unrelated to this fix (Broadcasting listener registration, not webhook lead resolution).
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in any changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` (one new private helper + the three existing-lead lookup lines), `tests/Feature/Leads/InboundMessageDualWriteTest.php` (3 new tests), `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php` (3 existing tests corrected in place, no new tests added).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: tenant/source/channel/thread scoping on the new lookup mirrors `conversations_identity_unique` exactly; no concurrency change was needed (every inbound handler already serializes per-sender via `Cache::lock` dedupe, confirmed pre-existing and unmodified); `Conversation`/`Message` migrations, `LeadMergeService`, `Lead::messageTimeline()`, policies, and UI files were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, DE1, and `Index::delete()`'s similarly-shaped gap remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure. `DetectDuplicateLeads`'s unscheduled status was later resolved as its own separate closure — see "DetectDuplicateLeads (`leads:detect-duplicates`) Scheduling Gap" below.

### Unredacted, Unrotated WhatsApp Webhook Payload Logging (L1-Shaped) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — an informally-shaped ("L1-shaped") observation with no original audit finding text in-repo, independently re-verified against the current codebase and selected via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding / root cause**: `LeadWebhookController::whatsapp()` unconditionally logged `Log::channel('whatsapp')->info('WA INCOMING '.json_encode(['all' => $request->all(), 'headers' => $request->headers->all()]))` on **every** inbound WhatsApp webhook call — the entire raw request body (customer message text, sender phone numbers, message ids) plus every HTTP header, at `info` level with no gating. `config/logging.php`'s `whatsapp` channel used the `single` driver — unlike every other file-backed channel in that config, which uses `daily`/`monthly` with a retention cap — so the file grew forever with no rotation or expiry. Verified this pattern was an outlier relative to this codebase's own established minimal-logging convention (Messenger/Instagram error paths and `DownloadLeadAttachment`'s own failure log both already used short, non-payload messages), and confirmed via repo-wide search that no test, production code, or documented runbook (including `README.md`'s own debugging guide, which references only `laravel.log`) depended on the old raw-dump shape or the `whatsapp` channel at all.

**Approved architecture**: minimal, non-payload diagnostic logging only — never the raw request body, never headers, never the message body/caption text, never the full phone number, never the raw `contacts`/profile payload.

**Approved logging contract implemented** for `WA INCOMING`: `tenant_code`, `entry_count`, and per parsed message — `message_type`, `has_body` (boolean only, never the text itself), `wamid`, `timestamp`, `phone_last4` (the sender's phone number masked to its last 4 digits, e.g. `15551234567` → `****4567`; `null`/empty/non-string input safely returns `null`, and the masking function can never return more than the last 4 characters of the input).

**Retention fix**: `config/logging.php`'s `whatsapp` channel changed from `'driver' => 'single'` to `'driver' => 'daily'` with `'max_files' => env('LOG_DAILY_DAYS', 14)` — reusing the exact existing environment-driven retention convention already used by this same file's own `daily` channel; no new configuration mechanism was introduced.

**`WA PROCESSING FAILED` (the failure-path log) intentionally left unchanged**: it already only logged `tenant_code` and the exception message, never raw payload — confirmed already minimal and consistent with this fix's own goal; only its channel's rotation behavior changes as a side effect of the config change.

**Historical `storage/logs/whatsapp.log` cleanup explicitly remains an operational follow-up, out of scope**: this fix is forward-only, exactly like the C2/CreateLeadFromImport precedents — the file was not deleted, truncated, or otherwise touched by this change.

**Regression tests, verified in both directions**: 2 new tests added to `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php` — one asserting the channel config (`driver === 'daily'`, `max_files` set), one posting a real WhatsApp webhook payload with a deliberately identifiable secret message body and full phone number, mocking `Log::channel('whatsapp')->info(...)`, and asserting every approved contract field/value plus that the JSON-encoded logged context contains neither the secret message text, the full phone number, nor a `headers` key — while also confirming the created Lead's own `phone` column still stores the full number normally in the database (proving zero change to actual message/lead-processing behavior). Both tests were confirmed to **fail** against the unfixed code (config test: `'single'` vs. expected `'daily'`; content test: fails because the old single-string log call doesn't satisfy the new two-argument contract) and to **pass** against the fixed code.

**Verification**:
- New tests (`WebhookTest.php`): **2/2 passed**, 17 assertions — confirmed failing before the fix, passing after.
- `WebhookTest.php`: **29/29 passed**, 123 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **951/952 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite: **1118/1121 passed** — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs. actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated throughout this document; unchanged in identity and count, confirmed unrelated to this fix (Broadcasting listener registration, not webhook logging).
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed production file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` (the `WA INCOMING` log call + two new private helpers, `summarizeWhatsappIncoming()`/`maskPhoneLast4()`), `config/logging.php` (`whatsapp` channel driver/retention only), `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTest.php` (2 new tests).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `RecordInboundMessage.php`, `LeadMergeService.php`, Messenger/Instagram logging, message-processing/parsing/lead-creation logic, schema/migrations, policies, and UI files were not touched. No other existing test was modified.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, DE1, and `Index::delete()`'s similarly-shaped gap remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure. `DetectDuplicateLeads`'s unscheduled status was later resolved as its own separate closure — see "DetectDuplicateLeads (`leads:detect-duplicates`) Scheduling Gap" below.

### DetectDuplicateLeads (`leads:detect-duplicates`) Scheduling Gap — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — the previously-deferred `DetectDuplicateLeads`-unscheduled observation first flagged during M4's own closure (see M4's closure text above), independently re-verified against the current codebase and closed via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding**: `App\Console\Commands\DetectDuplicateLeads` (`leads:detect-duplicates`) was fully implemented and correct — its own docblock already described itself as a nightly backfill/sweep pass — but was never registered in `routes/console.php`.

**Root cause**: a plain scheduling-registration omission, not a logic defect — confirmed by reading the command in full: it correctly delegates to `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor()` per lead via `chunkById(200)`, exactly mirroring the already-scheduled, already-M4-optimized live-detection path.

**Current behavior before the fix**: cross-channel duplicate detection ran *only* when a human opened a specific Lead's own detail page (`Show::possibleDuplicates()`). A pair of duplicate leads where neither side was ever opened by a human (realistic for two fully-automated-channel leads, or a bulk CSV import) could remain permanently unflagged, with no time bound on how long that could persist.

**Approved architecture**: `Schedule::command('leads:detect-duplicates')->daily();` — plain `daily()`, no `dailyAt(...)`, no `withoutOverlapping()`. Selected by direct comparison against this codebase's own existing scheduler conventions: `leads:recalculate-scores` (the structurally closest sibling — an identical `Lead::query()->chunkById(200, ...)` global sweep, already scheduled `->daily()` with no overlap guard) versus the `dailyAt('08:00')`+`withoutOverlapping()` category reserved for commands with real, notification-triggering side effects (`leads:send-alert-notifications`, `tasks:send-alert-notifications`, `leads:nurture-inactive-leads`). `DetectDuplicateLeads` is a silent, non-notifying, passively-surfaced audit-table sweep — it belongs in the former category, not the latter.

**Command logic intentionally left unchanged**: `DetectDuplicateLeads.php`, `LeadDuplicateDetectionService.php`, `Show::possibleDuplicates()`, and `LeadMergeService.php` were not touched. The command's existing idempotency was verified, not assumed: `LeadDuplicateLink`'s `firstOrCreate()` + canonical `pairKeys()` ordering + the table's own `unique(['lead_id', 'duplicate_lead_id'])` constraint together guarantee repeated runs (and the live-detection path running concurrently) can never produce a duplicate record for the same pair — already proven by two pre-existing, unmodified tests (`'the same pair is never recorded twice regardless of which lead triggers detection'`, `'dismissing a possible duplicate is never re-flagged by a later detection pass'`). No per-lead exception handling was added: `leads:recalculate-scores` (the chosen precedent) already runs this exact unguarded shape daily over a superset of the same table without one, so adding it here would have applied a stricter standard than this codebase already accepts for the closest sibling — explicitly assessed and rejected as scope creep during Impact Analysis.

**Regression test, verified in both directions**: one new test added to `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadDuplicateDetectionTest.php` — inspects the real `Illuminate\Console\Scheduling\Schedule` instance's registered events and asserts `leads:detect-duplicates` is present with a compiled cron expression of `0 0 * * *` (plain daily). Confirmed to **fail** against the unfixed `routes/console.php` (`Expecting null not to be null` — no matching scheduled event) and to **pass** after the one-line addition. Independently re-confirmed at final verification via reflection on the live `Event` object that `withoutOverlapping` is `false` and exactly one registration exists (no duplicate).

**No command smoke test was added** — explicitly out of scope by design: the command's own behavior is unchanged and untested territory here is a pre-existing gap (only the underlying service has direct test coverage), not something this scheduling fix needed to close.

**Verification**:
- New test (`LeadDuplicateDetectionTest.php`): **1/1 passed**, 2 assertions — confirmed failing before the fix, passing after.
- `LeadDuplicateDetectionTest.php`: **19/19 passed**, 45 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **952/953 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Full application suite: **1119/1122 passed** — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs. actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated throughout this document; unchanged in identity and count, confirmed unrelated to this fix (Broadcasting listener registration, not command scheduling).
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `routes/console.php` (one new `Schedule::command(...)` line, no reordering of existing entries), `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadDuplicateDetectionTest.php` (one new test).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `DetectDuplicateLeads.php`, `LeadDuplicateDetectionService.php`, `Show::possibleDuplicates()`, `LeadMergeService.php`, every other scheduled command in `routes/console.php`, and every existing test in `LeadDuplicateDetectionTest.php` were not touched. M10's `sourceBreakdown()` race remains a wholly separate, untouched candidate.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. The `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, DE1, `Index::delete()`'s similarly-shaped gap, and M10's `sourceBreakdown()` race remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure.

### Platform LeadManagement Settings — deleteStatus()/deleteCurrency() TOCTOU Race — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — a freshly-discovered observation, found via a targeted sweep for the same bug class as the already-closed C1 and tenant `deleteSource()` races, then closed via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding / root cause**: `App\Livewire\Platform\LeadManagement\Settings::deleteStatus()` and `::deleteCurrency()` both performed their dependent-existence checks (`Lead`/`tenant_lead_statuses` for a status; `Lead`/`Deal`/`LeadSourceCost`/`tenant_currencies` for a currency) and then called `$status->delete()`/`$currency->delete()` as a separate, later, unguarded statement — no transaction, no row lock. These are Platform-wide shared catalogs (`LeadStatus`, `Currency`), referenced by every tenant in the system, not scoped to one tenant. Verified from the actual migrations: every referencing column is `restrictOnDelete()` (or, for `lead_source_costs.currency_id`, the Postgres-default `NO ACTION`, which behaves as a restrict at commit time) except `deals.currency_id` (`nullOnDelete()`, already correctly handled by the existing `$inUseByDeal` check). A concurrent write from **any tenant** — a webhook/manual/CSV-import Lead creation, a status pipeline change, a Deal or LeadSourceCost creation, or a Platform Admin enabling a status/currency for a tenant — landing between the checks and the delete could cause `$status->delete()`/`$currency->delete()` to throw an uncaught `QueryException` instead of the intended "in use" toast. Structurally identical to the already-closed C1 (`Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()`) and tenant `Leads\Index::deleteSource()`, which had this exact shape and were fixed the same way — these two catalog methods were never touched by either prior fix.

**Fix**: wrapped each method's entire body (lookup → authorization → all existing dependent checks → delete → success toast) in one `DB::transaction()`, and added `->lockForUpdate()` to each method's existing `LeadStatus`/`Currency` lookup — the identical "lock the stable parent row" idiom already used by C1 and the tenant `deleteSource()` fix. Authorization was moved inside each transaction (it must run after the row is resolved, since `LeadStatusPolicy::delete()`/`CurrencyPolicy::delete()` need the actual instance) — confirmed safe because both policies are static (`hasModulePermission('module-management', 'delete')` only, no dependence on the instance's mutable state), so this changes nothing about what is authorized or in what order relative to the lookup. Check order, every toast message, and the success-path delete/toast are otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged in both methods. No shared helper was introduced — each method's wrap is applied independently and locally, matching the approved minimal-scope decision.

**PostgreSQL locking rationale and atomicity invariant**: a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the `LeadStatus`/`Currency` row causes any concurrent transaction attempting to `INSERT` (or `UPDATE` the referencing FK column of) a row referencing it — in `leads`, `tenant_lead_statuses`, `lead_status_logs`, `deals`, `lead_source_costs`, or `tenant_currencies` — to block, because PostgreSQL's referential-integrity check acquires a `FOR KEY SHARE` lock on the referenced parent row for any such write, and `FOR KEY SHARE` conflicts with an already-held `FOR UPDATE`. This preserves the required invariant — "once the dependent checks pass, no new dependent reference may be committed before the parent delete completes" — for the full set of currently-checked dependents in both methods. Independently confirmed directly against the app's real `pgsql` connection (via `artisan tinker`), both compiling to `select * from "..." where "..."."id" = ? limit 1 for update`.

**`LeadStatusLog` missing-check gap — explicitly discovered during this work and deliberately NOT fixed here**: `deleteStatus()`'s dependent checks never covered `lead_status_logs.old_status_id`/`new_status_id` (both `restrictOnDelete()`). A status with zero *current* leads sitting in it can still be referenced by a historical `LeadStatusLog` row from a lead that has since moved to a different status — deleting such a status would throw the identical uncaught `QueryException`, **deterministically, with no race required at all**. This is a separate, pre-existing correctness gap in the check's own completeness, not a concurrency issue, and was explicitly left untouched, tracked as a separate, future, not-yet-approved observation. `deleteCurrency()`'s own dependent-check list was independently confirmed complete (no equivalent gap) — its only defect was the missing transaction/lock, now fixed.

**Existing authorization, dependent-check logic, toast behavior, and success-path delete unchanged**: confirmed via the diff — every check condition, every toast message's text, and the delete/success sequence are identical to before, only re-indented inside the new transaction closures.

**No schema change, no backfill, no product behavior change**: purely a concurrency-safety fix; the only user-visible change is that the previously-racing case now correctly resolves to whichever of "in-use toast" or "successful delete" the post-lock state actually supports, instead of crashing.

**Regression tests, verified in both directions**: two new tests added to `tests/Feature/Platform/LeadManagementSettingsTest.php` (one per method), each combining two proofs — a query-log assertion that the locked lookup's qualified `"lead_statuses"."id" = ?`/`"currencies"."id" = ?` query shape actually ran, and a genuine transaction-boundary rollback proof (a real, non-queued `eloquent.deleted` event listener, fired strictly after the DELETE statement has already executed inside the transaction, deliberately throws on a second independent row; the row is asserted to still exist afterward). Both tests were confirmed to **fail** against the unfixed methods (temporarily reverted during implementation) — specifically the rollback assertion, since without `DB::transaction()` the DELETE is already committed by the time the forced exception fires — and to **pass** against the fixed code. SQLite's inherent inability to prove real cross-connection row-lock blocking is explicitly documented in the tests' own docblock, consistent with the same honest limitation already carried by `PlatformLeadSourceDeletionTest.php`/`LeadSourceCostCatalogTest.php`/the tenant `deleteSource()` closure — the real locking guarantee rests on the independent Postgres compilation check above, not on the SQLite suite alone.

**Verification**:
- New tests (`LeadManagementSettingsTest.php`): **2/2 passed**, 8 assertions — confirmed failing (on the rollback assertion) before the fix, passing after.
- `LeadManagementSettingsTest.php`: **16/16 passed**, 45 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **952/953 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures** (unaffected — this fix lives outside that directory).
- Full application suite: **1121/1124 passed** — the only 2 failures are the same pre-existing `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` mismatches (`'$refresh'` vs. actual `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) documented as pre-existing and unrelated throughout this document; unchanged in identity and count, confirmed unrelated to this fix.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Platform/LeadManagement/Settings.php` (`deleteStatus()`/`deleteCurrency()` only), `tests/Feature/Platform/LeadManagementSettingsTest.php` (2 new tests).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `LeadStatusLog`'s missing check, `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()` (C1), `Tenant\Leads\Index::deleteSource()`, `LeadReportingService::sourceBreakdown()` (M10), `LeadMergeService.php`, every other `delete()`/`deleteConfirmed()` method in this codebase, and every existing test in `LeadManagementSettingsTest.php` were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `messageTimeline()`'s blended-cutover edge case, DE1, `Index::delete()`'s similarly-shaped gap, and M10's `sourceBreakdown()` race all remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure. The `LeadStatusLog` missing-check gap was later closed as its own separate finding — see "Platform LeadManagement Settings deleteStatus() — Missing LeadStatusLog Dependent Check" below.

### Platform LeadManagement Settings deleteStatus() — Missing LeadStatusLog Dependent Check — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — the previously-deferred `LeadStatusLog` missing-check observation, explicitly identified but deliberately left unfixed during the just-closed TOCTOU race work above, closed via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding**: `App\Livewire\Platform\LeadManagement\Settings::deleteStatus()` checked only current `Lead.status_id` references and `tenant_lead_statuses` pipeline selections before deleting a `LeadStatus` — it never checked `lead_status_logs.old_status_id`/`new_status_id`.

**Database behavior**: both `lead_status_logs.old_status_id` (nullable — a lead's very first status assignment has no "old" status) and `lead_status_logs.new_status_id` (never null) are `restrictOnDelete()`, confirmed from the active migration (`2026_09_05_082339_convert_lead_log_tables_to_ulid_primary_keys.php`). A `LeadStatusLog` row is a permanent, append-only audit record of a status transition — it is never updated or deleted when a lead subsequently moves again, so it can reference a status no current lead sits in at all.

**Root cause / deterministic failure scenario**: a status that was historically used by a lead's pipeline movement (via any of this app's three status-change paths — `Tenant\Leads\Index::moveLead()`'s Kanban drag-and-drop, `Index::applyBulkStatusChange()`'s bulk action, or `Tenant\Leads\Show::updateStatus()`'s detail-page form, all confirmed call sites of `LeadStatusLog::create()`) but is no longer that lead's *current* status passes both pre-existing checks (no current `Lead.status_id` match, not necessarily in any tenant's pipeline) yet is still referenced by the historical log rows from that earlier transition. Deleting such a status previously reached `$status->delete()` and threw an uncaught `QueryException` from the database's own FK constraint — **deterministically, with no concurrency or race required at all**, unlike the just-closed TOCTOU finding above.

**Fix**: added exactly one additional dependent check, run first (before the existing `Lead` and `tenant_lead_statuses` checks, mirroring why C1 checks Conversation history first for the identical reason — a historical log entry has no existing reassignment or cleanup path, so it's a *permanent*, not just current, block):
```php
DB::table('lead_status_logs')
    ->where('old_status_id', $status->id)
    ->orWhere('new_status_id', $status->id)
    ->exists()
```
This runs inside the exact same `DB::transaction()` + `LeadStatus::lockForUpdate()` boundary already established by the just-closed TOCTOU fix — no additional locking or transaction work was needed, since the new check shares that same already-correct atomicity guarantee. Existing authorization, both pre-existing checks' own logic, the delete call, and both toast texts are byte-for-byte unchanged; the only behavioral difference is that a historically-referenced status now produces the same graceful "in use" danger toast every other dependent check already does, instead of an uncaught exception. `deleteCurrency()` was not touched — its own dependent-check list was already confirmed complete during the prior TOCTOU work.

**No historical data/backfill**: this fix only changes future delete-attempt behavior; no existing `LeadStatusLog` row was modified, deleted, or backfilled.

**Regression test, verified in both directions**: one new test added to `tests/Feature/Platform/LeadManagementSettingsTest.php` — creates two statuses and a lead moved from A into B (writing two real `LeadStatusLog` rows referencing A, matching the exact shape a real transition produces), explicitly detaches A from the tenant's pipeline (since `TenantFactory` auto-attaches the full catalog to every new tenant) and sets `changed_by: null` on the log rows (avoiding the factory's default `TenantUser::factory()` from creating its own stray Tenant, which would otherwise reintroduce a `tenant_lead_statuses` row for A and defeat the test's isolation), then asserts both pre-existing checks would incorrectly pass before exercising the real `deleteStatus()` action end-to-end. Confirmed to **fail** against the unfixed code with a genuine, uncaught `SQLSTATE[23000]: Integrity constraint violation ... FOREIGN KEY constraint failed` (`QueryException`) — independently reproduced twice this session (once during implementation, once again during final verification) by temporarily reverting the fix — and to **pass** against the fixed code (the graceful "in use" toast fires, status A remains present). SQLite genuinely enforces this FK constraint in this test suite (`config/database.php`'s `sqlite` connection sets `'foreign_key_constraints' => env('DB_FOREIGN_KEYS', true)`, defaulting to `true`) — unlike the `lockForUpdate()`/`FOR UPDATE` proofs elsewhere in this module, no PostgreSQL-specific out-of-band verification was needed for this particular check.

**Verification**:
- New test (`LeadManagementSettingsTest.php`): **1/1 passed**, 4 assertions — confirmed failing (genuine `QueryException`) before the fix, passing after, reproduced independently twice.
- `LeadManagementSettingsTest.php`: **17/17 passed**, 49 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **952/953 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures** on the clean final-verification run. One pre-existing, already-documented Faker-data-collision flake in `KanbanBoardTest.php` (unrelated code path — Kanban board search filtering, not Platform catalog deletion) appeared transiently on 2 of 4 total runs during this work, passed in isolation both times, and passed cleanly on the final run — not attributable to this fix.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Platform/LeadManagement/Settings.php` (`deleteStatus()` only), `tests/Feature/Platform/LeadManagementSettingsTest.php` (1 new test).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `deleteCurrency()`, the TOCTOU transaction/lock mechanism, `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()` (C1), `Tenant\Leads\Index::deleteSource()`, `LeadReportingService::sourceBreakdown()` (M10), `LeadMergeService.php`, every other `delete()`/`deleteConfirmed()` method, and every existing test in `LeadManagementSettingsTest.php` were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `messageTimeline()`'s blended-cutover edge case, DE1, `Index::delete()`'s similarly-shaped gap, and M10's `sourceBreakdown()` race remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure.

### Tenant LeadCustomFields\Index::delete() — TOCTOU Race — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — a freshly-discovered observation, found via a continued targeted sweep for the same bug class as the already-closed C1, tenant `deleteSource()`, and Platform Settings races, then closed via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding / root cause**: `App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadCustomFields\Index::delete()` performed its two dependent-existence checks (`hasValues()`, `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()`) and then called `$definition->delete()` as a separate, later, unguarded statement — no transaction, no row lock. Verified from the actual migrations: `lead_custom_field_values.lead_custom_field_definition_id` and `website_form_fields.lead_custom_field_definition_id` are both `restrictOnDelete()` — repo-wide grep confirmed these are the only two tables referencing `lead_custom_field_definitions.id`, so (unlike the just-closed `LeadStatusLog` finding) the dependent-check list itself was already complete; the sole defect was the missing transaction/lock. A concurrent write — most realistically, a same-tenant Client Admin/User saving a Lead's custom-field value via the authenticated `Show::updateCustomFields()`/`Index::saveLead()` paths (not gated by `is_active`, which `delete()` never checks) while another admin deletes the same, at-that-instant-zero-value, definition — landing between the checks and the delete could cause `$definition->delete()` to throw an uncaught `QueryException` instead of the intended "in use" toast. A narrower compound race also exists for `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` (requiring a `WebsiteFormField` to be created in the same window, itself an admin-driven Website Form Builder action). Structurally identical to the already-closed C1, tenant `Leads\Index::deleteSource()`, and Platform Settings `deleteStatus()`/`deleteCurrency()`, which had this exact shape and were fixed the same way — this method was never touched by any of the three prior fixes.

**Fix**: wrapped the entire method body (lookup → authorization → `hasValues()` → `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` → delete → success toast) in one `DB::transaction()`, and added `->lockForUpdate()` to the existing tenant-scoped `LeadCustomFieldDefinition` lookup — the identical "lock the stable parent row" idiom already used by C1, tenant `deleteSource()`, and Platform Settings. Check order (`hasValues()` first, `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` second — already correct, matching the same "no admin cleanup path exists for existing lead data" reasoning already established for C1's Conversation check and the just-closed `LeadStatusLog` check), both toast messages, `unset($this->definitions)`, and the success-path delete are otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged. `hasValues()`, `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()`, `SaveLeadCustomFieldValues`, `ValidateWebsiteFormSubmission`, and `LeadWebhookController.php` were all intentionally left untouched — the dependent-check logic itself was already correct and complete.

**PostgreSQL locking rationale and atomicity invariant**: identical mechanism to the three prior fixes — a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the `LeadCustomFieldDefinition` row causes any concurrent `INSERT` into `lead_custom_field_values` or `website_form_fields` referencing it to block, via Postgres's FK-check trigger acquiring a `FOR KEY SHARE` lock on the referenced parent row, which conflicts with an already-held `FOR UPDATE`. This preserves "once the dependent checks pass, no new dependent reference may be committed before the parent delete completes." Independently confirmed directly against the app's real `pgsql` connection (via `artisan tinker`), compiling to `select * from "lead_custom_field_definitions" where "tenant_id" = ? and "lead_custom_field_definitions"."id" = ? limit 1 for update` — the tenant filter is applied as part of the same locked query, so no cross-tenant lock is possible.

**No schema change, no data migration, no historical backfill**: purely a concurrency-safety fix; the only user-visible change is that the previously-racing case now correctly resolves to whichever of "in-use toast" or "successful delete" the post-lock state actually supports, instead of crashing.

**Regression test, verified in both directions**: one new test added to `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCustomFieldCatalogTest.php`, combining two proofs (mirroring the Platform Settings TOCTOU precedent exactly, since this is a genuine concurrency race rather than a deterministic missing-check like `LeadStatusLog`) — a query-log assertion that the locked lookup's qualified `"lead_custom_field_definitions"."id" = ?` shape actually ran, and a genuine transaction-boundary rollback proof (a real, non-queued `eloquent.deleted` event listener, fired strictly after the DELETE statement has already executed inside the transaction, deliberately throws on a second independent definition; the definition is asserted to still exist afterward). Confirmed to **fail** against the unfixed method (temporarily reverted, independently twice — once during implementation, once again during final verification) — specifically the rollback assertion, since without `DB::transaction()` the DELETE is already committed by the time the forced exception fires — and to **pass** against the fixed code. SQLite's inherent inability to prove real cross-connection row-lock blocking is explicitly documented in the test's own docblock, consistent with the same honest limitation already carried by the three prior fixes' own tests.

**Verification**:
- New test (`LeadCustomFieldCatalogTest.php`): **1/1 passed**, 4 assertions — confirmed failing (on the rollback assertion) before the fix, passing after, reproduced independently twice.
- `LeadCustomFieldCatalogTest.php`: **16/16 passed**, 59 assertions.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **953/954 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures** on every run during this work (no flakiness observed this time).
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero errors in either changed file. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/LeadCustomFields/Index.php` (`delete()` only), `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCustomFieldCatalogTest.php` (1 new test).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `hasValues()`, `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()`, `SaveLeadCustomFieldValues`, `ValidateWebsiteFormSubmission`, `LeadWebhookController.php`, `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::delete()` (C1), `Tenant\Leads\Index::deleteSource()`, `Platform\LeadManagement\Settings::deleteStatus()`/`deleteCurrency()`, `LeadReportingService::sourceBreakdown()` (M10), every other `delete()`/`deleteConfirmed()` method, and every existing test in `LeadCustomFieldCatalogTest.php` were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. `messageTimeline()`'s blended-cutover edge case, DE1, `Index::delete()`'s (Lead deletion) similarly-shaped gap, M10's `sourceBreakdown()` race, and the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap (a separate observation, not part of this fix) all remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure.

### LeadReportingService::sourceBreakdown() — Check-Then-Fetch Race on Deleted LeadSource — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

The previously-deferred M10 candidate (`LeadReportingService::sourceBreakdown()`'s race), first flagged as an open, unaddressed item across several earlier closures in this section, independently re-verified against the current codebase and closed via a dedicated Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding / root cause**: `sourceBreakdown()` performed two independent, unsynchronized reads — an aggregate lead count grouped by `source_id`, then a separate `LeadSource` lookup filtered by `whereIn('id', $counts->keys())` — with no atomicity between them. A `LeadSource` deleted in the gap between the two reads left its `source_id` present in the count result but absent from the subsequent lookup, producing a `null` `'source'` entry in the returned collection. The sole consumer, `resources/views/livewire/tenant/lead-reports.blade.php`, accesses `$row['source']->id`/`->name`/`->is_active` with no null-safe operator — a `null` `'source'` there is a fatal `Error`, crashing the Reporting page for that tenant/date-range. Confirmed from the actual migration: `leads.source_id` is `foreignUlid('source_id')->constrained('lead_sources')->cascadeOnDelete()`, so the failure is a genuine read-skew race between two statements, not a referential-integrity gap — `leads.source_id` can never reference a deleted `LeadSource` at rest, but the two reads here don't share one consistent instant.

**PostgreSQL READ COMMITTED analysis (this app has no isolation-level override anywhere)**: wrapping both reads in one `DB::transaction()` was explicitly evaluated and rejected as ineffective — under PostgreSQL's default READ COMMITTED isolation, each statement inside a transaction re-snapshots independently at the moment it begins; a transaction does not give multiple SELECTs one consistent shared view of the database (that guarantee only exists at REPEATABLE READ/SERIALIZABLE, not evaluated further as disproportionate to this finding's scope). A transaction wrap would have shipped a false sense of safety.

**Fix**: reordered the method's two existing reads — fetch all of the tenant's `LeadSource` rows first (unfiltered, matching the method's own pre-existing "inactive sources are not filtered out" policy), then run the existing aggregate count query second, exactly as it was already shaped. The now-superfluous `whereIn('id', $counts->keys())` on the sources query was removed (it filtered against a `$counts` result that, under the new order, doesn't exist yet). The `$counts->map(...)` return construction is unchanged. Race-free by construction: `leads.source_id`'s `cascadeOnDelete()` means once a source is captured in the (now-first) `$sources` read, its later deletion cascades away every one of its leads too — it simply cannot resurface in the (now-second) aggregate query, so no lookup miss is possible. Query count remains 2; return type/consumer contract (`Collection<int, array{source: LeadSource, count: int}>`) is unchanged; no other `LeadReportingService` method, no `LeadSource` deletion guard (C1, tenant `deleteSource()`), no schema/migration, and no UI/Blade file was touched.

**Regression test, genuinely verified in both directions, not tautologically**: one new test added to `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadReportingTest.php`, using a real `DB::listen()` interception (no sleep/timing/thread races) that pattern-matches either of the method's two actual queries and fires on whichever genuinely executes first, immediately deleting the target `LeadSource` via a raw, unguarded `DB::table('lead_sources')->delete()` call (simulating an external concurrent actor, deliberately bypassing every application-level deletion guard) between the two logical reads. Confirmed to **fail** against the reverted, original query order with a genuine, uncaught `ErrorException: Undefined array key "<ulid>"` (Laravel's global `set_error_handler`/`error_reporting(-1)` converts the PHP warning to a thrown exception in every environment, including testing) — and to **pass** against the fixed order, asserting: the interception genuinely fired, the source is genuinely gone (`LeadSource::find()` null, proving the cascade), and the returned collection contains exactly one row — the untouched second source, with its correct id and count, no broken/null row. A design correction was made and disclosed during implementation: an initial hook tied to only one of the two queries fired too late (`DB::listen()` only fires after a query already completes) to correctly interleave against one of the two orderings, producing a misleading non-representative failure; corrected to match whichever query actually runs first before accepting the result.

**Verification**:
- `LeadReportingTest.php` (18 tests: 17 pre-existing + 1 new): **18/18 passed, 32 assertions**.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **954/955 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Pint: clean (check-only mode). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly, zero new errors; `sourceBreakdown()` itself carries zero errors (the file's 2 pre-existing errors, at `assignmentBreakdown()`/`sourceCostSummary()`, are both untouched methods, confirmed unrelated). `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Services/LeadReportingService.php` (`sourceBreakdown()` only), `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadReportingTest.php` (1 new test).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `assignmentBreakdown()`, `dailyVolumeBreakdown()`, `statusBreakdown()`, `firstResponseSummary()`, `sourceCostSummary()`, every `LeadSource` deletion guard (C1, tenant `deleteSource()`), the Lead-deletion Consent/Deal cascade question, the `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, and every existing test in `LeadReportingTest.php` were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. DE1, `Index::delete()`'s (Lead deletion) similarly-shaped gap, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, and the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure.

### Leads\Index::export() — CSV/Formula Injection via Unsanitized Name/Email — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — a freshly-discovered observation, found via a targeted security sweep of every raw CSV/export writer in the module, then closed via a fresh, standalone Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding / root cause**: `Leads\Index::export()` wrote `$lead->name`, `$lead->email`, and `$lead->phone` into a raw CSV stream via `fputcsv()` with no formula-injection protection — the only raw-`fputcsv()` writer in the entire application (`FacebookLeadAdsExport`, C6, is a separate XLSX export protected at the cell-type level, not directly reusable here since plain CSV has no cell-type metadata channel). A value starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, or `@` is interpreted as an executable formula by Excel/LibreOffice/Google Sheets when the exported file is opened. Confirmed reachable: Laravel's `email` validation rule empirically accepts `=A1+B1@evil.com`/`+1234567@evil.com`/`-cmd@evil.com` as valid (verified via `Validator::make()` against this app's actual config), so `email` is exploitable via every lead-creation path including the "validated" ones; `name` is exploitable via `ProcessEmailLeadSource`'s unvalidated email "From" personal name and the Messenger/Instagram/WhatsApp webhook paths' unvalidated profile display names (correctly unvalidated by `LeadValidationRules`, since a real display name must permit characters that regex would otherwise reject). `phone`'s own validation regex permits only digits/spaces/parens/`+`/`-` — no letters, `@`, pipes, or quotes — so no genuine executable formula can ever reach it.

**Fix**: a new private `sanitizeCsvCell(?string $value): ?string` helper in `Index.php`, applied only to `name` and `email` immediately before each `fputcsv()` call — `phone` is deliberately **not** sanitized (approved architecture decision: its character-class validation already makes real formula injection impossible there, so sanitizing it would only change how an ordinary `+`-prefixed international phone number displays, with no security benefit). Trigger set: `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a leading tab, and a leading carriage return — matching OWASP's standard CSV-injection mitigation and C6's own trigger set for consistency. Detection checks only the literal first character (no trimming/whitespace normalization); a value prefixed with a leading single quote (`'`) neutralizes formula interpretation while every major spreadsheet application respects it as a force-text marker; `null` passes through unchanged; the check is naturally idempotent since the prefix character itself is not in the trigger set. No ingestion/validation change, no new dependency, no historical data backfill (rendering-boundary-only fix), no change to `fputcsv()`'s own RFC 4180 quoting/escaping, no change to `FacebookLeadAdsExport.php`/C6, no change to any other export/reporting method.

**Verification**:
- Edge cases independently re-verified via `artisan tinker` against the exact sanitizer logic, including byte-level (`bin2hex`) confirmation for the leading-tab/leading-CR cases (terminal rendering can hide non-printable characters): `=`/`+`/`-`/`@`/tab/CR-leading values all correctly prefixed; ordinary values, whitespace-then-trigger values, mid-string trigger occurrences, `null`, and already-prefixed values all correctly left unchanged.
- Real streamed CSV output independently verified (a temporary, non-shipped test, run then discarded): the raw bytes of a real `export()` download for a `=HYPERLINK(...)`-named lead contain the literal sequence `,"'=HYPERLINK(` — confirming the apostrophe prefix is the literal first character of the field's actual content in the generated file, and that `fputcsv()`'s own quoting still correctly wraps the field.
- 4 new tests added to `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadsExportTest.php`, all exercising the real, end-to-end `export()` output via the file's existing `exportedCsvRows()` helper (Livewire's actual download-effects capture) — formula-leading name, formula-leading email, ordinary name/email unchanged, and a comma/quote-containing name proving `fputcsv()`'s escaping is unaffected. `LeadsExportTest.php`: **16/16 passed, 25 assertions** (12 pre-existing + 4 new); the pre-existing `phone: '+1-555-0100'` assertion required no change, confirming zero behavioral impact on `phone`.
- **Genuine fail-then-pass, verified twice** (once during implementation, once again during final verification): the two formula-trigger tests, run unmodified against the production fix temporarily reverted, **failed** with a real assertion mismatch exposing the actual unprefixed, dangerous value; restored, they **passed**. The two non-trigger tests remained passing against the reverted code both times, correctly isolating the failure to the specific vulnerable behavior.
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: investigated a `KanbanBoardTest.php` board-search flake that appeared 3 times across 8 total runs (full-suite and isolated) during final verification — zero code-path overlap with CSV export, data-dependent (a Faker-random field coincidentally matching a search term), never reproduced twice in immediate succession, and matches the exact pre-existing flake pattern already independently documented across M2/M4/M10's own closures in this same module. Confirmed not a regression from this change; clean runs: **958/959 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**.
- Pint: clean (check-only mode). PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly; independently, programmatically confirmed zero errors fall within the modified `export()`/`sanitizeCsvCell()` lines. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php` (`export()` + new `sanitizeCsvCell()` helper only), `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadsExportTest.php` (4 new tests; no existing test modified).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `FacebookLeadAdsExport.php` (C6), `LeadValidationRules`, every lead-creation/ingestion path (manual, CSV import, website form, Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp, Facebook Lead Ads, email lead source), `LeadReportingService`, every other export method, and every existing test in `LeadsExportTest.php` were not touched. No stored `leads.name`/`email`/`phone` value was read, written, or backfilled — this is a rendering-time-only transform at the final CSV serialization boundary.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. DE1, `Index::delete()`'s (Lead deletion) similarly-shaped gap, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, and the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure.

### `Leads\Index::delete()` and `Leads\Show::delete()` — Missing Server-Side Enforcement of Deletable-Source-Type Restriction — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — the previously-deferred "`Index::delete()`'s (Lead deletion) similarly-shaped gap" observation, referenced as open/unevaluated across nine prior closures in this document, freshly re-inspected against the current codebase and closed via a dedicated Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval pass.

**Original finding / root cause**: `Index::performBulkDelete()` (bulk delete) was the sole place in the codebase enforcing the business rule that only `email`/`website_form`/`manual`-sourced leads may be individually deleted — every other `LeadSourceType` (the Platform-Admin-configured external/automated channels: Facebook Messenger, Facebook Lead Ads, WhatsApp, Instagram, LinkedIn Lead Gen, plus CSV Import) is meant to stay permanently undeletable that way. `Index::delete()` (single-row table delete) and `Show::delete()` (lead detail page / slide-over panel delete) both called `$lead->delete()` unconditionally after only a `Gate::denies('delete', ...)` check, with no source-type check at all. The Index table's per-row Delete button had a matching client-side-only Blade condition, but `Show`'s two Delete-button render sites (full page and panel) had no source-type condition whatsoever — the button rendered unconditionally for any lead a Client Admin could open. Fresh inspection confirmed both `delete()` methods are independently, directly callable Livewire actions (proven by this codebase's own pre-existing test suite calling `->call('delete', $id)` directly, bypassing the confirm/password modal entirely), so the Blade-only gate on the Index table provided no actual server-side protection either. Every FK referencing `leads.id` is `cascadeOnDelete()` (confirmed from the active migrations — no `restrictOnDelete()` dependents exist for `Lead`, unlike the LeadSource/LeadStatus/Currency/LeadCustomFieldDefinition catalogs fixed in prior closures), so this was not a TOCTOU race but a deterministic, always-reachable business-rule bypass: deleting a protected-source lead irreversibly cascades away its full `Conversation`/`Message` history, score/assignment/detail-log history, and any linked `Deal`/`Task`, with no way to regenerate that data for an externally-sourced lead.

**Fix**: added one canonical method, `LeadSourceType::isIndividuallyDeletable(): bool` (`true` for `Email`/`WebsiteForm`/`Manual`, `false` for every other case), as the single source of truth for this already-approved business rule. `Index::delete()` and `Show::delete()` each gained a new check — `if (! $lead->source->type->isIndividuallyDeletable()) { toast; return; }` — placed immediately after the existing `Gate::denies` authorization check and before the mutation, mirroring the existing authorization-then-business-rule check ordering already used elsewhere in this module (e.g. the Platform LeadManagement Settings TOCTOU closures). `performBulkDelete()`'s existing `in_array($lead->source->type->value, ['email', 'website_form', 'manual'], true)` literal and the Index table Blade's identical literal were both replaced with calls to the new canonical method — a mechanical, behavior-preserving swap (verified identical across all 9 `LeadSourceType` cases), not a logic change. `Show`'s two Delete-button render sites (`lead-hero.blade.php` for the full detail page, `header.blade.php` for Index's slide-over panel) each gained a matching UX-only `&& $lead->source->type->isIndividuallyDeletable()` condition, so a protected-source lead no longer shows an actionable Delete button there — mirroring the Index table's existing UX pattern. `confirmDelete()`/`confirmDeleteLead()`/`deleteConfirmed()`/`deleteLeadConfirmed()`, `LeadPolicy`, `canDelete()`/`canDeleteLeads()`, `ConfirmsLeadDeletionPassword`, and all cascade/FK definitions were deliberately left unchanged — the server-side `delete()` checks are the sole security boundary; the confirm-step and Blade conditions are UX only, per the approved architecture.

**No schema change, no migration, no product/business decision required**: the deletable-source-type rule itself was already decided and shipped in `performBulkDelete()`; this closure only extends its already-approved enforcement to the two sibling methods that had been missing it, plus adds matching UX gating in `Show`'s views.

**Regression tests, verified in both directions**: 6 new tests added to `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadDeletionTest.php`, all exercising real, end-to-end Livewire actions and rendered HTML (no private-helper tests) — a protected-source (`LeadSourceType::Whatsapp`, matching the existing `BulkActionsTest.php` fixture convention) lead rejected via direct `Index::delete()`/`Show::delete()` calls (lead remains persisted, danger toast dispatched); a `manual`-sourced lead confirmed still deletable; the detail page and the slide-over panel confirmed to hide the Delete button for a protected-source lead; the detail page confirmed to still show it for an allowed-source lead. **Genuine fail-then-pass proof performed twice, independently**: once during implementation (reverting the 4 new check/gate additions individually) and again during Final Verification (this time collapsing `isIndividuallyDeletable()` itself to `return true;` with every call site left untouched) — in both cases, exactly the tests that depend on the rule failed with real, non-tautological failures (a still-existing `Lead` row, an undispatched toast, present Delete-button HTML), while every unrelated test continued to pass, and the pre-existing `BulkActionsTest.php` source-type test also failed under the second proof — confirming all four call sites (`Index::delete()`, `Show::delete()`, both Blade UX gates, and `performBulkDelete()`) are genuinely wired through the one canonical enum method rather than independently hardcoded. Restored and re-confirmed passing both times.

**Verification**:
- `LeadDeletionTest.php` + `BulkActionsTest.php`: **38/38 passed**, 91 assertions (both before and after the independent fail-then-pass check).
- Full `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **964/965 passed, 1 pre-existing skip, 0 failures**, clean on every run this closure.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **425 errors** — matching the pre-existing baseline exactly; independently, programmatically confirmed zero errors in `LeadSourceType.php`, `Index.php`, or `Show.php`. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Enums/LeadSourceType.php` (new `isIndividuallyDeletable()` method), `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php` (`delete()` new check; `performBulkDelete()` literal → method call), `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Show.php` (`delete()` new check), `resources/views/livewire/tenant/leads.blade.php` (literal → method call), `resources/views/livewire/tenant/leads/partials/lead-hero.blade.php` (UX gate added), `resources/views/livewire/tenant/leads/partials/header.blade.php` (UX gate added), `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadDeletionTest.php` (6 new tests; no existing test modified).

**Explicitly confirmed at closure**: `LeadPolicy`, `ConfirmsLeadDeletionPassword`, `confirmDelete()`/`confirmDeleteLead()`/`deleteConfirmed()`/`deleteLeadConfirmed()`, `canDelete()`/`canDeleteLeads()`, every migration/cascade FK definition, DE1, `messageTimeline()`'s blended-cutover edge case, the `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap, and every other audit finding were not touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. DE1, the `messageTimeline()` blended-cutover edge case, and the missing `isReferencedByWebsiteForm()` test-coverage gap remain explicitly deferred/open exactly as previously recorded — none are resolved by this closure. `Index::delete()`'s (Lead deletion) similarly-shaped gap, previously listed alongside them, is resolved by this closure and is no longer an open item.

### `Platform\Clients\LeadSources::toggleLeadStatus()` — TOCTOU Race Causing Silent Pipeline-Stranded Leads — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID — a freshly-discovered observation, found via a targeted sweep of `Platform\Clients\LeadSources.php` for the same TOCTOU bug class as C1/tenant `deleteSource()`/Platform Settings/`LeadCustomFields`, then closed via a dedicated Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification (including an explicit user-directed correction pass) → Approval sequence.

**Original finding / root cause**: `toggleLeadStatus()`'s removal branch checked whether any of the tenant's leads currently sat in the status being removed, then called `$this->tenant->leadStatuses()->sync($syncData)` as a separate, later, unguarded statement — no transaction, no lock. Unlike every prior TOCTOU finding in this module, `leads.status_id` has **no FK relationship at all** to `tenant_lead_statuses` (confirmed from the active migration: `leads.status_id` only constrains against the global `lead_statuses` catalog) — so a concurrent write landing a lead into the status being removed, in the gap between the count check and the `sync()` call, produced no database error at all: it silently committed a `Lead` whose `status_id` no longer belonged to its tenant's pipeline. Because `Index::boardLeads()`/`Lead::countsByStatus()`/`Lead::nextPipelineStatus()` all iterate the tenant's *current* pipeline rather than the raw lead data, a stranded lead simply vanished from the Kanban board, its status-count badge, and the one-click "advance" action, with zero error surfaced to anyone — worse than every prior TOCTOU fix in this module, which at least failed loudly via a real FK `QueryException`. Seven independent, unlocked application writers could race this window: `Index::moveLead()`, `Show::updateStatus()`, `Index::applyBulkStatusChange()`, `CreateManualLead`, `CreateLeadFromImport`, `ProcessEmailLeadSource`, and `LeadWebhookController::createLead()`.

**Fix — Tenant-row shared/exclusive locking protocol**: `toggleLeadStatus()`'s addition branch (adding a status can never strand a lead) remains entirely outside any lock or transaction, unchanged. Its removal branch now runs inside `DB::transaction()`, acquiring an exclusive `Tenant::lockForUpdate()` (`FOR UPDATE`) on the tenant row *before* re-reading pipeline membership and lead counts, both freshly re-queried under the lock; the existing "in use" toast and refusal is unchanged, and `sync()` only runs once both checks pass.
