### ARCH-2 — Leads/Index.php Bulk Authorization-Shape Inconsistency — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

One of the findings surfaced by the same fresh forensic audit that produced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4/QA-1/QA-2/ARCH-1 above, investigated and closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Original finding** (Category: Architecture / Maintainability, Severity: LOW, Status: CONFIRMED): `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Index` expressed the actor-level requirement for the bulk Assign action in two different shapes. The Blade Assign control (`bulk-actions-toolbar.blade.php`) is shown only when `isClientAdmin && canUpdateLeads`, while `openBulkAssignModal()` checked only `isClientAdmin` (plus a non-empty selection), so a Client Admin without the Lead Management `update` permission could open the modal by invoking the Livewire action directly even though the UI never offered it.

**Security status at closure**: no unauthorized-write path, cross-tenant mutation path, or privilege-escalation path was found; this closure is not a security fix and does not claim one. Every final bulk assignment write was already, and remains, authorized per-Lead by `Gate::denies('assign', $lead)` (`LeadPolicy::assign()` requires tenant match, `isClientAdmin()` and the `lead-management` `update` permission) inside `applyBulkAssign()` and `bulkUnassign()`, and `eligibleSelectedLeads()` still re-derives the selection from the tenant-scoped query. UI visibility and action-entry guards are convenience/consistency layers; the per-Lead Gate is the write boundary. The impact was maintainability/cognitive load only.

**Approved remediation — Strategy B, minimal entry-layer alignment**: `openBulkAssignModal()` now requires Client Admin, `canUpdateLeads`, and a non-empty selection: `if (! $this->isClientAdmin || ! $this->canUpdateLeads() || $this->selectedLeadIds === []) { return; }`. The new usage calls `canUpdateLeads()` as a method rather than reading it property-style. A first implementation using `$this->canUpdateLeads` measured 435 PHPStan errors against a 434 HEAD baseline, the single new diagnostic being `property.notFound` for that computed property; the method-call form follows the repository's documented convention for `#[Computed]` methods called from PHP code (`.ai/rules/leads.md`, precedent `Show::isManualLead()`), was confirmed equivalent in result within a request, and restored the count to 434. Its only runtime difference is that a direct call bypasses the per-request computed cache, costing two extra small permission-check queries per `openBulkAssignModal()` call.

**Explicitly unchanged**: `LeadPolicy` (no change), the Blade toolbar (its condition already expressed the intended actor-level requirement), `eligibleSelectedLeads()`, `applyBulkAssign()`, `bulkUnassign()` (its entry guard intentionally left as-is), the status/priority/tag/delete bulk actions, partial-success/skip semantics, tenant re-derivation, and every service. No new authorization abstraction, trait, helper, or service was introduced, and no annotation, suppression, or PHPStan configuration change was made.

**Test added**: one focused regression test in `tests/Feature/Leads/BulkActionsTest.php`, `'a client admin without update permission cannot open the bulk assign modal'` — a Client Admin whose role grants only `view` selects a Lead and calls `openBulkAssignModal()` directly; `showBulkAssignModal` stays `false`, `assigned_to` stays null, and no `LeadAssignment` row exists. It was confirmed to fail with the guard removed and pass with it in place. Existing QA-1/QA-2 tests were not modified.

**Verification**:
- Focused ARCH-2 test: **1 passed, 3 assertions**.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/BulkActionsTest.php`: **23 passed, 56 assertions**.
- Relevant authorization/assignment tests (`LeadAssignmentTest`, `AuthorizationTest`, `LeadPermissionUiTest`, `LeadStatusAssignmentMergedFormTest`): **34 passed, 67 assertions**.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1101 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one, unchanged).
- Pint: clean, no changes needed.
- PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434** with this closure's full diff — **0 net-new diagnostics** (full diagnostic-level comparison, none added or removed).
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for ARCH-2**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php` (one line in `openBulkAssignModal()`), `tests/Feature/Leads/BulkActionsTest.php` (one test plus its imports), and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of ARCH-2 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. Every other finding surfaced by the fresh forensic audit this was discovered in (UX-1, PROD-1, INC-1, OPS-1, OPS-2) remains open and is explicitly **not** addressed by this closure.

### UX-1 — Stale Merged Lead Show Mutation — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

One of the findings surfaced by the same fresh forensic audit that produced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4/QA-1/QA-2/ARCH-1/ARCH-2 above, investigated and closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Final Pre-Implementation Inspection → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Original finding** (Category: UX / Correctness, Severity: MEDIUM — lower end, Status: CONFIRMED by runtime reproduction): a `Leads\Show` component (full page or the Kanban panel) that was already mounted on lead L kept acting on L after another session merged L into a survivor. `Show::mount()` redirects a *fresh* visit to a merged lead to its survivor, and Livewire re-hydrates `$this->lead` from the database on every request (so it correctly carried `merged_into_lead_id`), but no `Show` mutation method ever read that value and `LeadPolicy` has no merged rule. Reproducing it needs only two sessions — one left open on the lead while the other merges it — not a timing race.

**Confirmed behavior before the fix** (runtime probes, Livewire test harness): every probed mutation family succeeded against the merged loser with a success toast and no exception, and wrote only to the loser row (the survivor was never changed) — note, status, priority, assignment, tag, task, deal, custom field, consent grant, contact edit, and delete. Some emitted events and notifications for a lead hidden from every list and report; a stale delete removed the loser and cascaded the merge-link row away. Messaging was already refused separately by `OutboundEligibilityService`. No cross-tenant path, role bypass, or privilege escalation existed — the tenant and role checks still ran against the loser row — so this was a UX / data-orphaning correctness issue, not a security issue.

**Approved remediation — Show-level private merged-state guard (Strategy 1)**: one new private helper, `leadIsMutable(): bool`, in `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Show.php`. It returns true when `$this->lead->merged_into_lead_id` is null; otherwise it shows a danger toast — `__('This lead has been merged and can no longer be changed.')` — and returns false. The merged-state rule is a mutation-state rule, not a tenant/role authorization rule, so it lives in `Show` (which owns mutation orchestration) and is *additional to* the existing Gate checks, never a replacement for them. Ordering is fixed at every site: existing authorization/capability check first, merged-state guard second, then validation / modal / transaction / write logic — so an unauthorized actor keeps the existing permission-denial message.

**Guard coverage — 31 call sites in `Show.php`**:
- The 23 direct write entry points: `updateCustomFields`, `updateStatus`, `updateContact`, `updateCompany`, `updateDeal`, `assign`, `cancelMeeting`, `markMeetingDone`, `cancelFollowUp`, `markFollowUpDone`, `addNote`, `attachTag`, `detachTag`, `mutateConsent`, `saveDeal`, `changeDealStatus`, `deleteDeal`, `saveTask`, `changeTaskStatus`, `deleteTask`, `recalculateScore`, `delete`, `dismissDuplicate`.
- `mutateConsent` is guarded once, after its own Gate, so `grantConsent()` and `withdrawConsent()` are both covered. The private funnels (`applyDetailChanges`, `recordDateAlertAction`, `notifyIfDateScheduled`) and the `*Confirmed` wrappers inherit the guard from their guarded callers and carry none of their own.
- `delete()` is guarded immediately after its delete Gate and before the individual-deletion check, the deletion audit, and the transaction; `confirmDelete()` (its opener) is guarded too, and `deleteConfirmed()` inherits via `delete()`.
- Six form/modal openers, purely to stop a user starting an action that can no longer be submitted: `createDeal`, `editDeal`, `createTask`, `editTask`, `openMergeModal`, `confirmDelete`.
- `confirmMerge()`, so a stale merged loser gets the same early, clear toast before reaching `LeadMergeService` (whose own locked-row guard is unchanged).
- `updateStatusAndAssignment()`: one guard at the wrapper, applied only when the actor can update or assign (an unauthorized actor keeps the existing silent no-op), while `updateStatus()` and `assign()` keep their own guards for direct Livewire invocation — so a merged lead never produces a duplicate refusal toast.
- Deliberately not given the helper: `sendMessage()`/`resendMessage()` (already refused for merged leads by `OutboundEligibilityService`, wording and behavior unchanged) and read-only/UI-state methods.

**Stale panel protection**: the Kanban panel is the same `Show` class with `$panel = true`, so it is covered by the identical guard. A refused panel mutation shows the danger toast and dispatches nothing to the parent board. Refusal is toast-only in both modes — no redirect (a redirect could send a rep assigned only to the loser to a 403 on the survivor, and a child panel cannot redirect reliably), no panel close, and no `lead-panel-deleted` (or any replacement) event — so the current lead and any unsaved draft or open modal stay exactly as they were.

**Delete protection**: a stale `Show` can no longer delete an already-merged lead. The refusal happens before any destructive work — no `LeadDeletionAudit` row is written, the loser row stays, and the merge-link row stays. Existing delete semantics for non-merged leads are unchanged.

**ARCH-1 compatibility**: `refreshActivity()` and its 16 call sites are unchanged and the helper is not part of it. A refused mutation returns at the top of its method, before any activity invalidation.

**Dedicated regression coverage** — one new file, `tests/Feature/Leads/StaleMergedLeadShowTest.php`, 79 tests / 237 assertions. Each scenario mounts `Show` on a lead, merges it through `LeadMergeService`, and then drives the OLD component; rows the merge would have reparented are seeded after the merge so each action genuinely has something to change:
- 28 data-driven stale full-page mutations (every guarded write entry point, including the `*Confirmed` wrappers and consent grant and withdraw): exactly one danger toast with the exact merged message, both leads' rows and the global counters unchanged, and no `LeadUpdated`.
- The identical 28 actions succeeding on a normal, non-merged lead (control).
- 6 stale panel mutations by a non-admin assigned rep (the toast is the only dispatch), plus a control proving `lead-panel-updated` still fires normally.
- 4 stale delete refusals (page/panel × `delete`/`deleteConfirmed` with the right password): no redirect, no `lead-panel-deleted`, no `LeadDeletionAudit`, merge-link row intact; plus a control that a normal lead still deletes.
- The six openers (refuse on a merged lead, open normally otherwise), a refused save keeping its open modal and draft, `updateStatusAndAssignment()` yielding exactly one refusal for an admin and for an update-only actor (and still applying both halves on a normal lead), and an actor without update keeping the existing permission-denial toasts (and the wrapper's silent no-op) on a merged lead.
- Run against the unfixed `Show.php`, 47 of the 79 fail (every refusal group); the 32 that pass are the controls and the authorization-ordering test.

**Verification**:
- Focused UX-1 file: **79 passed, 237 assertions**.
- Relevant existing subset (31 files: merge, editing, status, deals, tasks, custom fields, consent, deletion audit, meeting/follow-up, notes, tags, assignment, permission UI, authorization, scoring triggers, outbound eligibility, and others): **402 passed**.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1181 tests, 1180 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one, unchanged) — the prior 1102 plus the 79 new tests.
- Pint: clean, no changes needed.
- PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434** with this closure's full diff — **0 net-new diagnostics** (compared at diagnostic level by file, message, and identifier).
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for UX-1**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Show.php` (157 insertions, 2 deletions — the two removed lines are the wrapper's `if ($this->canUpdate())` / `if ($this->canAssign())`, now evaluated once into variables), `tests/Feature/Leads/StaleMergedLeadShowTest.php` (new), and this file. `LeadPolicy`, `LeadMergeService`, `Index.php`, every Blade file, and every other service/action were confirmed untouched. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of UX-1 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**Explicitly out of scope and unchanged**: `LeadPolicy`, `LeadMergeService`, `Index.php` and the Kanban flow, merge-time notification / loser-channel broadcast, Echo behavior, general delete redesign, and general consent redesign.

**Documented remaining limitations — not fixed by this closure**:
- **Accepted race window**: a merge that commits *after* a request has re-hydrated `$this->lead` but *before* that request's mutation write is not closed. Closing it would need a lead-row lock (and transactional changes) in every mutator, which was judged out of proportion to a MEDIUM-lower-end finding and would reach into closed transaction work; no row locks were introduced. The guard makes the stale-session scenario (a tab left open across a merge) safe; it does not make a genuinely simultaneous merge-and-write safe.
- The forms still render on an already-stale merged page, so the user learns of the merge when they submit (by the refusal toast) rather than before; reloading self-heals through the existing `mount()` redirect.
- `deleteConfirmed()` still verifies the deletion password before delegating to `delete()`, so a stale open delete modal with a wrong password shows the password error before the merged message.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression. Every other finding surfaced by the fresh forensic audit this was discovered in (PROD-1, INC-1, OPS-1, OPS-2) remains open and is explicitly **not** addressed by this closure.

### PROD-1 — Merged Lead Deletion Lifecycle (Hard Delete vs. Merge Retirement) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

One of the findings surfaced by the same fresh forensic audit that produced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4/QA-1/QA-2/ARCH-1/ARCH-2/UX-1 above, investigated and closed via the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Final Pre-Implementation Check → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Original finding** (Category: Product / Data-lifecycle consistency, Severity: LOW — provisional, with the survivor-deletion case the one element that could justify MEDIUM (lower end), Status: CONFIRMED behavior; whether it was a *defect* was a product decision): the repository deliberately hard-deletes ordinary Leads permanently (DB-1: no `SoftDeletes`, no restore, cascade destruction and merge-link loss accepted), while the merge design retires a merged "loser" instead of deleting it (`LeadMergeService`: the loser is "never hard-deleted", stays queryable, hidden from every list and report). Nothing reconciled the two for merged leads. Confirmed by inspection and runtime probes: `Show::delete()` refused a merged loser (UX-1) and bulk delete excluded it, but `Index::delete($id)` deleted it (success toast, merge link cascaded away, survivor timeline lost its `lead_merged` entry, audit row with no merge context); and deleting a merged *survivor* through the normal UI silently turned every hidden loser back into an ordinary, visible lead (`merged_into_lead_id` is `nullOnDelete`) while destroying the history that had been reparented onto the survivor and leaving no trace of the merge. There is no unmerge feature and no per-row provenance, so that transition was irreversible. No cross-tenant or privilege-escalation path existed at any point.

**Approved product decisions** (explicit, from the user):
- **A — merged loser deletion**: a merged loser is **permanently undeletable** through every normal Lead deletion path.
- **B — merged survivor deletion**: a survivor with one or more **direct** merged losers is **undeletable** too. Deletion is refused; losers are never resurrected, unmerged, or deleted together with the survivor, and there is no warning-then-allow flow. A normal lead with no merged losers is fully deletable, exactly as before.
- **C — merge-history retention**: **deferred**. No tombstone table, no detached merge-link records, no merge-history schema, no merge metadata on `LeadDeletionAudit`.
- **D — retirement/archive**: **not approved**. No all-Lead archive state, no `SoftDeletes`, no restore workflow, no generic Lead `is_active`. Ordinary Leads stay permanently hard-deletable.
- **Explicit semantic consequence, recorded as a product decision, not a side effect**: because A and B together mean a merged cluster cannot be deleted through the normal Lead deletion flow, a merged cluster (a survivor and all its losers, including every node of a merge chain) is now **permanently non-deletable, with no escape hatch**. There is deliberately no privileged or administrative deletion path. Existing merged leads and survivors are **retroactively** protected by the new rule. Leads that had already resurfaced through an earlier survivor deletion are ordinary leads and remain deletable; no migration or backfill was performed or needed.

**Remediation — one shared, transaction-time eligibility check**: new `App\Services\LeadDeletionEligibilityService::evaluate(Lead $lead, bool $lock = true): EligibilityResult` (reusing the existing `App\Support\EligibilityResult`, the same shape `OutboundEligibilityService` already returns). It decides on business *state* only; `Gate('delete')`, the individual-deletion source-type restriction, and the password confirmation stay exactly where they were, and no state rule was moved into `LeadPolicy`. It re-fetches the target lead (tenant-scoped) with `lockForUpdate()`, then:
- a missing row is **eligible**, so the existing "already gone" handling in `RecordLeadDeletionAudit` (which returns `null`) stays the single owner of that case;
- a lead with `merged_into_lead_id` set (a merged loser) is refused with `__('This lead has been merged and can no longer be changed.')` — deliberately the same wording UX-1's Show guard already uses, so one condition has one message on every path;
- a lead that is the `merged_into_lead_id` of at least one other same-tenant lead (a survivor) is refused with `__('This lead has merged leads and can no longer be deleted.')`.
Both predicates are **direct**; no recursion or transitive traversal was needed. Every lead in a merge chain (A→B→C) is either itself a loser (A and B) or has a direct loser (C), so all three are protected — verified against the merge model, which allows a survivor to be merged into another lead.

**Delete-path architecture — all three paths aligned, ordering preserved**: at each site the order is `Gate('delete')` → the existing source-type restriction (plus UX-1's `leadIsMutable()` guard in `Show`) → inside the deletion `DB::transaction`: the evaluator (which locks the target lead row first) → `RecordLeadDeletionAudit::handle()` → `$lead->delete()`. A refusal returns before the audit, the delete, the redirect, the panel event, the panel close, and the success toast, so no audit row is written and no child row is touched. Methods changed: `Show::delete()` and `Show::confirmDelete()`; `Index::delete()`, `Index::confirmDeleteLead()`, and `Index::performBulkDelete()`. The password wrappers (`deleteConfirmed()`, `deleteLeadConfirmed()`) are covered because they call these methods. `Show::confirmDelete()` and `Index::confirmDeleteLead()` also run an **advisory** (`lock: false`) evaluation for an early refusal, before a password is typed; that is UX-only, and the transaction-time evaluation is the authoritative check. One accepted, cosmetic observation: for a protected-source merged survivor, the opener shows the merged message where `delete()` would have shown the source-type message; the authoritative ordering is unchanged and was not redesigned for it. `Index::delete()` was brought into parity with `Show`: it now refuses both merged losers and merged survivors.

**Bulk behavior and messaging**: merged losers remain excluded by the existing `eligibleSelectedLeads()` query, and count as skipped. A merged survivor reaches the loop, is refused inside its own per-lead transaction, is not counted as deleted, and is counted as skipped. Normal leads in the same selection delete normally, and the existing "X of Y Leads deleted. Z skipped." summary is unchanged. When one or more survivors are refused, one additional danger toast follows the summary, using the repository's `trans_choice` convention: singular `1 selected lead has merged leads and cannot be deleted.`, plural `{count} selected leads have merged leads and cannot be deleted.` A loser refused only through a race (merged after the selection query ran) is counted as skipped without that extra toast. No delete-button hiding and no modal redesign was made.

**Concurrency design and verification**: the evaluator locks **only** the target lead row and reads losers with a plain query, so DB-1's single-lock audit invariant and `LeadMergeService`'s ordered two-row lock are untouched and no new lock target or lock ordering was introduced. Locking the survivor row is sufficient because `LeadMergeService` (line 327) is the sole writer of `merged_into_lead_id` and always holds the survivor's row lock while writing it. If a merge commits first, the deletion waits, then sees the loser and refuses. If the deletion commits first, the merge's write pointing the loser at the deleted survivor is rejected by the `merged_into_lead_id` foreign key (SQLSTATE 23503) and rolls back, so no invalid state is ever committed. As a side benefit, the locked check closes UX-1's documented race window for `delete` only; UX-1's documented limitation for every other mutation is unchanged. Verified against two genuinely independent real PostgreSQL sessions by the committed `MergedLeadDeletionConcurrencyTest.php` (3 tests, 12 assertions: the evaluator locks exactly the target row and no other; a merge that committed first is fully visible to the evaluator; a deletion that committed first leaves a concurrent merge unable to commit an invalid state). It uses `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT`, so it is deterministic and instant, skips cleanly without a real PostgreSQL connection, and its fixtures use an explicit unique `tenant_code` and are fully cleaned up. With the lock removed from the evaluator, its lock test fails. Additionally, a **disposable, uncommitted** verification (not part of the repository, deleted afterwards) raced two real OS processes — the real deletion path against the real `LeadMergeService::merge()` — for **24 rounds** on the real dev PostgreSQL with randomized start offsets: 21 rounds delete-won (survivor deleted, merge failed on the FK), 3 rounds merge-won (deletion refused, no audit written), **0 invariant violations**, no deadlocks, and the database left at exactly its pre-run row counts. As with the M8/DEADLOCK-1/DB-1 closures, this end-to-end two-process guarantee is established by that manual verification, not by a permanently committed automated test.

**Known limitation, existing and unchanged — not introduced by this closure**: in a delete-versus-merge race where the deletion commits first, the merging user receives an unhandled foreign-key `QueryException` rather than a friendly message. This is the narrow, pre-existing `LeadMergeService` blind spot that DB-1's own closure already documented (a nullsafe already-merged check that does not distinguish "not merged" from "row no longer exists"). It is not made worse — no invalid state is committed — and it was neither fixed nor touched here.

**No schema change**: no migration and no index. An index on `merged_into_lead_id` was evaluated and deliberately not added: every existing `leads` index is `tenant_id`-prefixed, the survivor check runs only on a rare, password-gated admin action (bulk is capped at 100), and every lead delete already pays an FK scan of `leads` for the SET NULL action. If scale ever justifies it, it belongs in its own change.

**Regression coverage** — `tests/Feature/Leads/MergedLeadDeletionProtectionTest.php`, 30 tests / 86 assertions. Every refusal is asserted the same strong way: exactly the expected danger toast, and every row of both leads (and their notes, tasks, consent, links) identical afterwards, with no `LeadDeletionAudit` written:
- **Merged loser**: `Index::delete`, the password-confirmation path, the early opener, and a loser forced into a bulk selection (excluded, no survivor toast).
- **Merged survivor**: `Show` page and panel (`delete` and `deleteConfirmed`) with no redirect and no `lead-panel-deleted`; the `Show::confirmDelete` opener; `Index::delete`, the password path, and the `Index::confirmDeleteLead` opener; the loser stays merged and hidden with its link intact.
- **Bulk**: survivor alone (0 of 1, plus the danger toast), mixed normal + survivor (only the normal lead deleted, exactly one audit row), several survivors (plural toast).
- **Chains**: A→B→C — A and B refused as losers, C as a survivor, nothing deleted, in single and bulk deletion.
- **Controls**: a normal lead still deletes through `Show`, `Index` and bulk, with its openers still opening; a lead with only a Pending or Dismissed duplicate link (no merged losers) is still deletable.
- **Authorization and scope**: an actor without delete permission gets the existing permission-denial message, not a merged message; the source-type restriction still wins over the merged rule; another tenant's admin still cannot reach a merged lead; the evaluator's survivor lookup is tenant-scoped.
- **Evaluator**: eligible normal lead, ineligible loser, ineligible survivor, eligible missing row, and the advisory form.
- Run against the original `Show.php`/`Index.php`, 16 of these 30 fail (every refusal group); the remaining 14 are the controls and existing-behavior checks.

**Verification**:
- `MergedLeadDeletionProtectionTest`: **30 passed, 86 assertions**.
- `MergedLeadDeletionConcurrencyTest` (real PostgreSQL, not skipped): **3 passed, 12 assertions**, stable across repeated runs.
- Existing deletion, merge, bulk, consent, and UX-1 focused files (`LeadDeletionAuditTest`, `LeadDeletionTest`, `BulkActionsTest`, `LeadMergeTest`, `LeadConsentTest`, `StaleMergedLeadShowTest`, `DealMergeTest`, `TaskMergeTest`, `LeadCustomFieldMergeTest`, `LeadNotesDisplayTest`): **195 passed**, all unmodified.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1215 tests, 1214 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one, unchanged) — the prior 1182 plus the 33 new tests.
- Pint: clean, no changes needed.
- PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434** with this closure's full diff — **0 net-new diagnostics** (compared at diagnostic level by file, message, and identifier).
- `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for PROD-1**: new — `app/Services/LeadDeletionEligibilityService.php` (72 lines), `tests/Feature/Leads/MergedLeadDeletionProtectionTest.php` (490 lines), `tests/Feature/Leads/MergedLeadDeletionConcurrencyTest.php` (259 lines). Modified — `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Show.php` (`delete()`, `confirmDelete()`; 31 insertions, 1 deletion) and `app/Livewire/Tenant/Leads/Index.php` (`delete()`, `confirmDeleteLead()`, `performBulkDelete()`; 64 insertions, 2 deletions), plus this file. Confirmed **unchanged**: `RecordLeadDeletionAudit` (its contract, including `null` meaning "already gone", is intact), `LeadMergeService`, `LeadPolicy`, the `Lead` model, every migration, and every Blade file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of PROD-1 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**Effect on already-closed decisions**: DB-1 — **extension only**: hard delete, cascade destruction, link loss for the deletions that are still permitted, the audit contract, and the single-lock audit invariant are all unchanged; a refusal simply happens before the audit, so merged clusters no longer reach it. UX-1 — **extension only**: `Show::delete()`'s merged-loser guard and its message are untouched and its 79 tests remain green. DE1, ARCH-1, and ARCH-2 — unchanged. Feature #14 (Consent/GDPR) — unchanged; as a consequence, the consent-state divergence that a resurfaced loser could exhibit can no longer arise through deletion, because a survivor with losers can no longer be deleted. M8 and DEADLOCK-1 — unchanged (no new lock target).

**Explicitly not done and remaining out of scope**: merge-history / tombstone / link retention (Decision C, deferred), an all-Lead retirement, archive, or restore model (Decision D, rejected), a `merged_into_lead_id` index, hiding the delete button for survivors, any privileged or administrative deletion path, and the adjacent items noted during this investigation (the blank Kanban flyout for a merged lead, `SendTaskAlertNotifications`'s missing merged-lead exclusion) — none were touched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression against the approved behavior above (i.e., unless a merged loser or a survivor with merged losers becomes deletable through a normal deletion path, or a normal non-merged lead becomes undeletable — not merely because a merged cluster can no longer be deleted, which is the explicitly approved, intended behavior). Every other finding surfaced by the fresh forensic audit this was discovered in (INC-1, OPS-1, OPS-2) remains open and is explicitly **not** addressed by this closure.

### INC-1 — CSV Duplicate-Handling Inconsistency — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED — ALL FIVE CANDIDATES CLOSED: A1 (IN-FILE CONFLICT CLASSIFICATION), B2 (UPLOAD-LEVEL FILE-FINGERPRINT WARNING), C (PARTIAL-MATCH TRACEABILITY), D (MERGED-IDENTITY ALIAS RESOLUTION) AND E (FEATURE #1 INTEGRATION — CLOSED AS ALREADY HANDLED, BY VERIFICATION)**

One of the findings surfaced by the same fresh forensic audit that produced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4/QA-1/QA-2/ARCH-1/ARCH-2/UX-1/PROD-1 above, investigated through the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Final Pre-Implementation Check → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence. **This entry closes ONLY the approved A1 scope. The broader INC-1 duplicate-handling problem remains open, and its four remaining candidates were deliberately deferred by explicit product decision — not implemented, not partially implemented, not resolved.** (Same partial-closure convention as M1's "atomicity sub-problem only" entry above.)

**What INC-1 is (derived from the repository)**: the repository never recorded an original INC-1 text — it appears only in "remains open" lists, and DB-2's closure names it "a separate, unresolved finding" right after noting the other creation paths were untouched. Tracked as "CSV duplicate handling inconsistency", it is the CSV importer applying its own duplicate policy — reject at creation time, record a `Duplicate` row, never link, merge, or update — which differs from the module's canonical model, where duplicates are detected after the fact (`LeadDuplicateDetectionService`, run on lead-detail view and the daily sweep only), linked as `LeadDuplicateLink`, and reviewed by a human. Investigation (read-only inspection plus disposable runtime probes, all removed) identified five duplicate-semantics candidates, A–E, separate from three adjacent import-engine findings.

**Approved and CLOSED — A1: in-file conflict classification normalization.** `CreateLeadFromImport::checkInFileDuplicate()` previously ran one `exists()` over the import's own earlier `Imported` rows (normalized email OR phone) and classified any hit as `Duplicate of an earlier row in this same CSV file.` — so a row whose email matched one earlier row and whose phone matched a *different* earlier row was mislabeled as an ordinary in-file duplicate, while the identical shape against existing leads was already classified as a conflict (the same row shape read differently depending only on whether the matching lead pre-dated the import). Confirmed by runtime probe (the same row was labeled "duplicate of an earlier row" in one import and "conflict" when the CSV was re-uploaded).
- **The change**: the in-file query keeps its exact shape (`lead_import_id` + `status = Imported` + the same normalized email/phone OR, same indexes, same single query per identity-bearing row) but now returns the small set of matching rows ordered by `row_number` (`id`, `row_number`, `normalized_email`, `normalized_phone`). The earliest matching row per identity is determined in PHP; if the email's earliest match and the phone's earliest match are **different rows**, the row is an in-file **conflict**, recorded with the exact approved message `Email matches an earlier row in this CSV file and phone matches a different earlier row — rejected as a duplicate conflict.` — the in-file counterpart of the existing-lead conflict rule. Any other in-file match (only one identity matching, or both matching the same earlier row) remains an ordinary in-file duplicate with its unchanged message; no match returns null and the row continues down the unchanged flow.
- **Precedence (documented in the code and approved)**: the in-file check remains **first and final** for a row. A row that matches an earlier `Imported` row is never additionally run through the existing-lead check, so an existing-lead conflict on top of an in-file match is not separately reported — exactly today's behavior for that shape. Only `Imported` rows count: `Failed` and `Duplicate` earlier rows never participate, so first-valid-row-wins is unchanged (a `Failed` earlier row still does not block a later valid row). When several earlier rows share an identity value (not possible under first-valid-row-wins, but the code is deterministic even for anomalous data), the earliest row number is used for each identity.
- **Unchanged**: `duplicate_of_lead_id` stays NULL for in-file duplicates and conflicts; no Lead is created for a duplicate or conflict row; status values, counters (`duplicate_count` increments as before), `raw_data` persistence, `checkExistingLeadDuplicate()`, and DB-2's post-lock existing-lead re-check are untouched, and no A1 logic moved into the locked section (the in-file check remains pre-lock and read-only on the import's own rows, relying on the sequential chunk chain as before); tenant scoping (in-file matching is scoped to the import id), same-import retry idempotency, chunked sequential processing, and "never name-only" are unchanged. No schema change, no migration, no Blade change (a conflict row has no linked lead, so the existing import-detail `errors` column renders the message exactly as it already does for existing-lead conflict rows), no new resolver or service, and no change to `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`, Feature #1, PROD-1, the consent code, or any other closed feature.
- **Performance**: no added queries — `exists()` became a `get()` of at most a couple of small rows. Measured exactly with a disposable replica: 313 queries (email-only fixture, matching the query-budget test's shape) and 353 (email+phone) for 20 rows, **identical with and without the change** in the SQLite test environment; `LeadCsvImportEngineTest`'s query-budget guard still passes at its unchanged `<330` threshold.

**Regression coverage** (12 new tests): `CreateLeadFromImportTest.php` (+9, 28 → 37) — same earlier row matching both fields stays an ordinary in-file duplicate; email→row A + phone→different row B is a conflict (exact message, `duplicate_of_lead_id` null, no Lead created, counters accurate, `raw_data` kept); a single-identity match stays ordinary; precedence (in-file match plus an existing-lead match is not surfaced as an existing-lead conflict); the existing-lead conflict message and behavior are unchanged; earliest-row-number selection is deterministic regardless of insertion order; earlier `Failed` and `Duplicate` rows do not participate or block; in-file matching is scoped to the import (another tenant's rows and another import's rows never count); a retried conflict row is idempotent. `LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php` (+2) — an end-to-end conflict with accurate counters and unchanged ordinary in-file duplicates, and a conflict detected across the 200-row chunk boundary. `LeadImportUploadTest.php` (+1) — the conflict message renders through the existing errors column with no lead link. Against the original code exactly the 4 new conflict-shape tests fail (the unit conflict test, both engine tests, the render test); the rest are characterization tests that pass before and after; reversing the `row_number` ordering makes the determinism test fail.

**Verification**:
- The three CSV import test files: **55 passed** (188 assertions); focused import/merge/broadcast/pipeline set: 88 passed.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1227 tests, 1226 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one) — the prior 1215 plus the 12 new tests.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434** with this closure's diff, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for A1**: `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateLeadFromImport.php` (`checkInFileDuplicate()` and its docblock, plus the in-file comment in `processRow()`), `tests/Feature/Leads/CreateLeadFromImportTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportUploadTest.php`, and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of INC-1 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**DEFERRED — explicitly NOT implemented in this cycle, and NOT resolved by this closure** (each is a product decision, deferred on purpose; current behavior is intentionally left exactly as it was):
- **B — identity-less re-upload** *(as of this A1 entry; subsequently addressed by the approved B2 closure below — the deferral text is kept as the historical record)*: rows with neither email nor phone are always imported and are never checked for duplicates, so a separate upload of the same CSV imports them again (the same import id retried remains idempotent through the `(lead_import_id, row_number)` guard). No `file_hash`, `row_fingerprint`, cache marker, or upload-level idempotency was added.
- **C — partial-match handling** *(as of this A1 entry; subsequently addressed, as a traceability display improvement only, by the approved C closure below — the deferral text is kept as the historical record)*: a row whose email or phone matches an existing lead is skipped entirely as a duplicate; the row's other, non-matching identity value survives only in `LeadImportRow.raw_data`, which the import detail page does not display beyond the name. The existing lead is never updated, filled, or overwritten, and no review flow or UI change was made.
- **D — merged-loser re-import** *(as of this A1 entry; subsequently addressed by the approved D closure below — the deferral text is kept as the historical record)*: merged-away leads remain excluded from CSV duplicate matching (consistent with `LeadDuplicateDetectionService` and covered by an existing test), so an identity held only by a retired lead can create a new active lead. Merged identities are not resolved to their survivor; `LeadDuplicateDetectionService` and PROD-1 were not changed.
- **E — Feature #1 integration** *(as of this A1 entry; subsequently closed as already handled, by verification, per the approved E closure below — the deferral text is kept as the historical record)*: skipped CSV rows do not become Leads for review, no review artifact or table exists, no `LeadDuplicateLink` is created for them, and the Feature #1 post-hoc review workflow is unchanged (a `LeadDuplicateLink` needs two lead rows, which a skipped row is not).
- These remain **open product decisions**; none of them is closed, and the broader INC-1 finding must not be read as fully resolved by this entry.

**Adjacent findings — separate, not addressed, not part of INC-1**: `CreateLeadFromImport`/`LeadCsvImport::model()` silently dropping rows (with the import reporting "Completed") when the importing user no longer exists; blank CSV lines producing phantom `Failed` rows with `total_rows` smaller than the recorded rows; and raw exception messages being stored and rendered as row errors. Discovered and reproduced during INC-1's inspection, deliberately kept out of scope.

**This entry closes A1 only.** Do not reopen A1 unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. INC-1's deferred candidates (B, C, D, E) remain open pending product decisions, and OPS-1 and OPS-2 remain open and explicitly **not** addressed.

**Approved and CLOSED — B2: upload-level file-fingerprint warning (identity-less re-upload).** Decided by explicit product decision (B1 do-nothing, B3 row fingerprint and B4 cache marker were not chosen; a uniqueness constraint was rejected because it would forbid a deliberate, confirmed re-import). A tenant-scoped SHA-256 fingerprint of the exact uploaded file is now stored on `LeadImport`, and re-uploading byte-identical content shows an explicit warning and requires confirmation before anything is created. This is **best-effort recognition, not strict prevention** — no lock and no unique constraint were added, so two simultaneous first-ever uploads of the same file may both proceed, and two users after a prior import exists may both be warned and both confirm.
- **Schema**: one migration (`2026_09_19_101303_add_file_hash_to_lead_imports_table.php`) adds `lead_imports.file_hash` (nullable `varchar(64)`, SHA-256 hex) and a **non-unique** composite index on `(tenant_id, file_hash)`; `down()` drops both. Historical rows stay NULL — no backfill — and a NULL hash never triggers a warning. `file_hash` was added to `LeadImport`'s `#[Fillable]` and `@property` docblock.
- **Hashing**: `hash_file('sha256', …)` over the exact raw uploaded bytes, no normalization. The same bytes under a different filename are recognized; a changed cell, reordered rows, or different line endings are different files and are not recognized (intentional). It is computed once per initial upload request, after the existing validation/header/row-count checks and before anything is created, stored or queued; there is no row-level hashing and no per-row query.
- **Flow** (`App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadImports\Index`): authorize → validate → read headers → count rows → hash → one indexed, strictly tenant-scoped lookup of the latest prior `LeadImport` with that hash. No prior import: `startImport()` runs exactly as before. A prior import in **any** status (Pending, Processing, Completed, CompletedWithErrors, Failed) opens the warning — never auto-blocked by status — and creates no `LeadImport`, stores no file and queues nothing until confirmed. Only the same tenant's own filename/date is shown; `file_path` and other tenants' data are never read.
- **Confirmation** (`confirmDuplicateUpload()`): the pending hash, filename and date are server-set `#[Locked]` properties (the client can never supply them). Confirm re-authorizes, re-validates the file, re-hashes the CURRENT file and compares with `hash_equals` against the pending hash; a missing/stale pending state is a no-op, a mismatch is refused with a danger toast, and there is no second prior-import lookup. Selecting a different file (`updatedCsvFile`) clears the pending state and closes the warning; Cancel/dismiss (`updatedShowDuplicateUploadModal(false)`) clears it but keeps the selected file. A confirmed re-import runs the ordinary engine unchanged: rows whose email or phone match existing leads are still skipped as duplicates and only identity-less rows repeat.
- **Failure behavior**: an unreadable file gives the normal "The file could not be read." error and creates/stores/queues nothing; the prior-import lookup has no `try/catch`, so a database failure **fails closed** (propagates) rather than silently bypassing the protection.
- **UX**: the existing `x-confirm-modal` is reused (no new modal logic) with title "This file was already imported", body ":filename was imported on :date with identical content. Importing it again may create duplicate leads for rows that have no email or phone. Rows whose email or phone match existing leads will still be skipped as duplicates.", and "Import again" / "Cancel" actions. The previous tail of `import()` was extracted, behavior-preserving, into private `startImport(string $fileHash, int $dataRowCount)` (the row count is passed because `total_rows` and the toast need it; the confirm path recomputes it from the same, hash-verified file).
- **Unchanged**: `CreateLeadFromImport`, `LeadCsvImport`, `LeadImportRow`, `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`, A1's in-file conflict rule, DB-2, Feature #1, PROD-1, UX-1, the consent code, same-import-id retry idempotency (the `(lead_import_id, row_number)` guard; verified `file_hash` survives a re-run), chunking, counters, lead creation/assignment/tags/scoring.
- **Performance**: one extra indexed query per initial upload (query-budget test: 319 → 320, threshold unchanged at `<330`); the confirm path adds no lookup.

**Regression coverage** (16 new test definitions, 22 executed cases with datasets): `LeadImportUploadTest.php` (+14 definitions, 11 → 25) — first upload stores the hash of the exact bytes; identical re-upload warns with nothing created, stored or queued and only imports after confirmation; same bytes/different filename recognized; changed cell / reordered rows / CRLF not recognized; another user of the same tenant warned and can confirm; another tenant never warned and no filename leak; all five prior statuses warn and can be confirmed; NULL historical hash never warns; Cancel keeps the file and a stale confirm is a no-op; selecting a different file invalidates the pending confirmation; confirm with no pending state is a no-op; the Locked pending hash cannot be set from the client; an unreadable file creates/stores/queues nothing; a failing lookup fails closed. `LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php` (+2, 7 → 9) — a confirmed re-import of a mixed CSV skips identity rows as duplicates and repeats only identity-less rows; re-running the queued import for the same `LeadImport` id creates no extra leads and keeps `file_hash`.

**Verification**:
- `LeadImportUploadTest` + `LeadCsvImportEngineTest`: **40 passed** (140 assertions); with `CreateLeadFromImportTest`: 77 passed.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1252 tests, 1251 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** on the verification run (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one). One later full-suite rerun showed a single failure in `KanbanBoardTest` ("a client user's board search does not match location, win probability, or deal value…"), a pre-existing, unrelated flaky test (it searches for `77` against random factory data); it passed 6 of 6 reruns in isolation, was not touched, and is not a B2 regression.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 → 434, 0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean. Migration up/down/up verified on SQLite; PostgreSQL DDL verified with `migrate --pretend` (`varchar(64) null` plus the non-unique index).

**Exact files changed for B2**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/LeadImports/Index.php`, `app/Models/LeadImport.php`, `resources/views/livewire/tenant/lead-imports/index.blade.php`, `database/migrations/2026_09_19_101303_add_file_hash_to_lead_imports_table.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportUploadTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php`, and this file. The pre-existing uncommitted `CLAUDE.md` edit was not touched, staged, or committed.

**Non-blocking verification notes**: the direct `hash_equals` mismatch branch and the `hashSelectedFile()`-null branch have no dedicated test. Both are structurally unreachable through the supported lifecycle (the pending hash is only set from the currently selected file, and every change to `csvFile` clears it via `updatedCsvFile`; a null hash requires the file to vanish mid-request) and both fail safe (refuse / read error, nothing created, stored or queued).

**This entry closes B2 only.** Do not reopen B2 unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. **The broader INC-1 finding remains OPEN**: C (partial-match handling), D (merged-loser re-import) and E (Feature #1 integration) remain explicitly deferred product decisions — not implemented, not resolved; the adjacent importer-deleted silent drop, blank-line phantom failures and raw exception exposure remain separate and unaddressed; OPS-1, OPS-2 and UX-2 remain open and untouched.

**INC-1 C — Partial-Match Traceability — COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED.** Decided by explicit product decision (Option A of the C brief; Option B's persisted matched-field metadata, applying the leftover value to the existing lead, human review and skipped-row export were not chosen). C is closed as a **traceability / import-detail display improvement only**: the importer's behavior toward a partially-matching row is exactly what it was.
- **The gap C closed**: a skipped CSV row that matched an existing lead on only one identity field (for example the email matched but the incoming phone did not) was shown on the import detail page as just "Duplicate of <lead name>" — nothing said which field matched or what incoming value was left over. The unmatched value was never lost (it sits in `LeadImportRow.raw_data` and, normalized, in the row's `normalized_email`/`normalized_phone` columns) but was never surfaced.
- **What the import detail page now shows** (`App\Livewire\Tenant\LeadImports\Show::partialMatches()` and the row Details cell in `show.blade.php`, below the unchanged "Duplicate of <name>" link): **email-only match** → `Matched by: Email` plus `Unmatched phone: <incoming phone as typed>`; **phone-only match** → `Matched by: Phone` plus `Unmatched email: <incoming email>`; **both identifiers matching the same lead** → `Matched by: Email + Phone`, no unmatched line; only one identifier supplied → only the matched label, nothing invented. **A row that matches nothing** is Imported as before and receives no partial-match UI.
- **What is deliberately unchanged**: **A1 conflict rows** (email and phone matching two different existing leads, `duplicate_of_lead_id = NULL`) keep the existing conflict message; **in-file duplicate rows** keep their existing A1 behavior and message; **Failed rows** are unchanged. None of them gets a derived label, because no lead is linked to them.
- **Derived at render time, no persisted state**: labels are computed from data `rows()` already eager-loads — the row's own normalized values against the linked lead's `email_normalized`/`phone_normalized` — in a private, side-effect-free method. **No importer change** (`CreateLeadFromImport`, `LeadCsvImport`, `LeadImportRow`, models, services and jobs are untouched), **no migration and no schema change**, no row status, counter, `duplicate_of_lead_id` or retry/idempotency change. **No Lead is modified or created by this feature** — Feature #7's rule that an existing Lead is never merged, overwritten or filled by a CSV import stands.
- **Privacy / tenant isolation**: the only newly displayed values are the match label and the incoming row's own unmatched email/phone (HTML-escaped). **The existing lead's email, phone, company, location and every other detail are not newly exposed**, nor are the skipped row's other columns (company, location, budget, tags, notes); the pre-existing "Duplicate of <name>" disclosure is not widened. The cross-tenant 404 on the import detail page is unchanged, and detection stays tenant-scoped.
- **Performance**: **0 import-time queries added**, and **no N+1 on the detail page** — measured at runtime: the page runs 3 queries on refresh with 1 partial-match row and 3 with 8, identical to the pre-change code, so the count stays constant with row count; the existing `with(['lead','duplicateOfLead'])` eager load is sufficient.
- **Inherent render-time limitation**: the label reflects the linked lead's **current** identity state, not a historical snapshot. If that lead's email or phone is edited after the import, the label can change or disappear (it is then omitted rather than shown misleadingly, by design and by test).

**Regression coverage** (13 new tests, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportPartialMatchDisplayTest.php`, real importer output through the real `Show` component): email-only match; phone-only match; a single supplied identifier; both matching the same lead; no match (Imported, no partial-match UI); conflict, in-file duplicate and Failed rows keep their existing message and get no derived detail; identity-only scope (skipped-row columns and existing-lead details not surfaced); an edited linked lead never yields a misleading label; no existing lead modified and no lead created; a rerun of a recorded row is a no-op; cross-tenant 404 and no cross-tenant leak; constant query count between 1 and 8 rows; HTML escaping of incoming values. Against the original code the 6 positive display tests fail while the 6 safety/regression tests pass by design. Final verification also replaced one latent flaky assertion (a bare `'555'` that a random ULID in the page HTML could very rarely match) with specific values.

**Verification**:
- `LeadImportPartialMatchDisplayTest`: **13 passed** (56 assertions); import/A1/B2/DB-2/Feature #1/PROD-1/UX-1 related suites: 226 passed; `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1271 tests, 1270 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one).
- Full suite: the only failures are the 2 known stale `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` tests (they expect `'$refresh'` while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) — **pre-existing, unrelated, NOT an INC-1 C regression** — plus, in one run, `KanbanBoardTest`'s "a client user's board search does not match location, win probability, or deal value…", a **known pre-existing random-data flake** (it searches for `77` against random factory data; it passed 8 of 8 isolated reruns and neither it nor `Leads\Index` is in the diff) — also **not an INC-1 C regression**.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434**, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for C**: `app/Livewire/Tenant/LeadImports/Show.php`, `resources/views/livewire/tenant/lead-imports/show.blade.php`, new `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportPartialMatchDisplayTest.php`, and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of INC-1 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**C does NOT close, and explicitly leaves open** (not implemented, not resolved): matched-lead attribution for A1 conflict rows; matched attribution for in-file duplicate rows; persisted matched-field metadata; human review of skipped rows; a skipped-row export; any Lead update/fill/merge behavior for partial-match rows; display of non-identity skipped-row columns; and any redesign of phone normalization (matching is digits-only, so a number with a country-code prefix does not match the same number without one).

**This entry closes C only.** Do not reopen C unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. **The broader INC-1 finding remains OPEN**: D (merged-loser re-import) and E (Feature #1 integration) remain explicitly deferred product decisions — not implemented, not resolved; the adjacent importer-deleted silent drop, blank-line phantom failures and raw exception exposure remain separate and unaddressed.

**INC-1 D — Merged-Identity Alias Resolution — COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED.** Decided by explicit product decision (Option A of the D brief: use the existing `merged_into_lead_id` relationship during import matching). Option B (a persisted alias table) and Option C (visibility only) were not chosen, and the ambiguous-survivor policy below was decided after final review rejected an unapproved first version.
- **The gap D closed**: a merge leaves the loser's own email and phone on the loser row (the survivor takes a loser value only when someone explicitly resolves that field to "loser"), and every duplicate matcher excludes merged rows. A CSV row carrying only a merged-away lead's identity therefore matched nothing and was imported as a brand-new active lead — silently, and unflagged, because `LeadDuplicateDetectionService::detectFor()` also excludes merged candidates, so nothing linked the new lead to the merged cluster. Merging thus weakened the import duplicate gate for exactly the identities it retired. (The test previously cited as covering this, `a lead already merged away … imported as new`, covers only a loser that *shares* its identity with the survivor; it never covered the loser-only case.)
- **The rule** (`CreateLeadFromImport::checkExistingLeadDuplicate()` and two small helpers): merged-away identities are treated as **aliases of the active top survivor**. A CSV row matching a merged lead's email and/or phone is skipped as a **Duplicate of that survivor**, `duplicate_of_lead_id` points at the resolved active survivor, and **no new Lead is created**. This covers loser email only, loser phone only, both loser identities, a loser identity plus the survivor's identity, and a loser identity plus a brand-new other identity.
- **Chain resolution**: chains are supported (a survivor can itself later be merged away, and a merge never re-points earlier losers, so B → A → C resolves B's identity to C). `resolveActiveSurvivorId()` follows `merged_into_lead_id` one tenant-scoped primary-key read per hop, with a visited-id cycle guard and a depth cap of 25 hops. It is **fail-closed**: a cycle, a missing target, a pointer that leaves the tenant, or an over-deep chain (26 hops fails, 25 resolves) can never be turned into a match or a new lead — the row is recorded **Failed** (through `handle()`'s existing catch) with the fixed, data-free message "This row matches a merged lead whose merge chain could not be resolved, so it was not imported.", no lead is created or modified, `failed_count` increments, and only the matched lead's id and a reason code (`cycle`/`too_deep`/`missing_target`) are logged.
- **Ambiguous survivors — the approved "intersect, else conflict" rule**: each identity resolves to a **set** of candidate active survivors (an active holder is a candidate directly; merged holders resolve through their chains; holders resolving to the same survivor collapse into one candidate; an active holder never automatically outranks a merged holder that resolves elsewhere). With one identity present or the other unmatched, a single candidate is a Duplicate of it and several candidates are a conflict. With both matched, **exactly one survivor common to both sets** is the Duplicate target (email → {S1, S2} with phone → S2 is a Duplicate of S2); no common survivor, or more than one, is an **A1-style conflict** (`duplicate_of_lead_id` NULL, no lead created) — including email → S1 with phone → S2, the original A1 case, whose message is unchanged. **No survivor is ever selected by age, ULID order, database order or first-match**: a first version of this fix picked the lowest-ULID survivor, which had no repository support and was rejected at final review (it could silently flip a conflict into a duplicate or the reverse depending only on lead age) and replaced. A final search of the importer confirms the only index access into a candidate list is guarded by an explicit "exactly one" check.
- **A1 / DB-2 / Feature #1**: A1's conflict semantics are intact (the in-file check still runs first and is final; conflict rows keep NULL `duplicate_of_lead_id` and the existing message). **DB-2's pre-lock check and in-lock re-check call the same `checkExistingLeadDuplicate()`**, so the two cannot diverge; no lock or lock hierarchy was added (the new reads are plain reads; merge locks lead rows only, the import locks the tenant row — either commit order gives a valid outcome, as before). Feature #1's merge semantics, `LeadMergeService`, `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`, PROD-1, UX-1 and the closed A1/B2/C behavior are untouched; **the importer is intentionally stricter than `detectFor()`**, an explicit approved D policy and not a Feature #1 merge-semantic change. No schema change, no migration and no alias table was required — the loser rows already hold the identity and the existing `(tenant_id, email_normalized)`/`(tenant_id, phone_normalized)` indexes serve the lookup.
- **Data safety / tenant isolation**: no Lead is created, updated, overwritten or merged by resolving a merged identity (a test compares every lead attribute before and after); every identity lookup and every chain hop carries the tenant constraint, another tenant's identical identity imports as new there, and a corrupt pointer that leaves the tenant fails closed.
- **Performance**: the moderate-import query-budget test still measures **320 queries** (limit 330), unchanged. Per row: active match 5 and no-match 14 (both identical to before), one-hop merged 6, three-hop merged 8, ambiguous two-survivor identity 7, intersection duplicate 8 — a merged holder costs one primary-key read per hop and nothing else, with no N+1.

**Regression coverage** (31 test cases, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportMergedIdentityTest.php`, real `LeadMergeService::merge()` plus the real importer): the former-bug regression (loser email only, phone only, both — Duplicate of the survivor, no new lead, `duplicate_of_lead_id` = survivor, sanity-checked that the survivor never took the loser's identity); a loser identity plus a brand-new other identity; loser + survivor identity combinations resolving to the same survivor; email and phone resolving to two different survivors (conflict); multi-hop chains; survivor changed after merge; an identity chosen onto the survivor at merge time; the full ambiguous-survivor matrix (one identity → several survivors is a conflict, unique intersection is a Duplicate for either survivor, no intersection or an unmatched other identity is a conflict, both identities ambiguous over the same set is a conflict, an active holder versus a merged holder resolving elsewhere); the 25/26-hop depth boundary; a cycle (Failed row, exact message, nothing mutated, counters, only ids logged); a broken chain appearing only after the pre-lock check (fails safe from inside the tenant lock); DB-2's in-lock re-check honoring a merge committed between the two checks; in-file interaction; retry/idempotency; tenant isolation including a corrupt cross-tenant pointer; no lead modified; the import detail page showing "Duplicate of <survivor>"; and query cost. Against the original importer 25 of the 31 fail; against an emulated lowest-ULID rule the 6 ambiguous-survivor tests fail.

**Verification**:
- `LeadImportMergedIdentityTest`: **31 passed** (135 assertions); importer/A1/B2/C/DB-2/Feature #1/PROD-1/UX-1 related suites: 239 passed; `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1302 tests, 1301 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one).
- Full suite: the only failures are the 2 known stale `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` tests (they expect `'$refresh'` while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`) — **pre-existing, unrelated, NOT an INC-1 D regression**; `KanbanBoardTest`'s random-data flake appeared once in a `tests/Feature/Leads/` run and passed 5 of 5 in isolation — also not a D regression.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434**, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for D**: `app/Actions/Tenant/CreateLeadFromImport.php`, new `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportMergedIdentityTest.php`, and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of INC-1 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**D limitations — separate or intentional, NOT part of this closure**: merged identities remain **permanently claimed** — a survivor changing its own email or phone does not release the loser's old identity, and there is no override workflow (an intentional identity-claim decision; an address legitimately reused later by someone else stays blocked); the importer remains **stricter than `detectFor()`**; the **existing A1 conflict wording is reused** for every conflict, including a single-identity ambiguity, where it is slightly loose; **C's partial-match labels do not apply to alias duplicates** (the linked survivor does not hold the loser's value, so the row shows "Duplicate of <survivor>" without a label); and there is **no true two-process merge/import race test** — the deterministic in-lock test covers the recheck, but not a genuine concurrent race. The separate observation that `ProcessEmailLeadSource`'s inbound-email lookup has no merged-lead exclusion (so an email from a merged loser's address can attach to the retired loser) is a **separate finding requiring its own inspection**, was not touched, and is not part of D.

**This entry closes D only.** Do not reopen D unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. **The broader INC-1 finding remains OPEN**: E (Feature #1 review integration) remains an explicitly deferred product decision — not implemented, not resolved; the adjacent importer-deleted silent drop, blank-line phantom failures and raw exception exposure remain separate and unaddressed.

**INC-1 E — Feature #1 Integration — COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED (closed as already handled, by verification).** Decided by explicit product decision after inspection: no production or runtime code was changed for E. E's definition was refined by inspection — `PROGRESS.md` only recorded it as "skipped CSV rows … no review artifact or table … no `LeadDuplicateLink`" — and inspection showed that what E could mean is either already handled by Feature #1 or a separate, unapproved new workflow.
- **Verified: imported leads already participate in Feature #1.** A lead created by a CSV import is an ordinary lead to `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`: `detectFor()` (which the lead detail page runs live) flags it when a later lead shares its normalized email or phone — the same pending, canonically-ordered, never-duplicated `LeadDuplicateLink` as for any channel — and the scheduled `leads:detect-duplicates` sweep (daily; scans every active lead with an email or phone, with no source filter, and whose docblock names bulk imports) finds the pair even if nobody opens either lead. The importer itself never runs or enqueues detection, and it cannot need to: it rejects any identity match at creation (including merged-alias matches, per D), so an imported lead can only become a duplicate candidate later (a later lead, or an edit) — exactly what live detection and the sweep already cover. A lead with no email or phone can never match anything, inherent to identity-based detection.
- **Verified: skipped rows stay LeadImportRow-only.** A row skipped as a duplicate — existing-lead duplicate, partial match (C), A1 conflict, in-file duplicate, or merged-identity alias (D) — creates no Lead and no `LeadDuplicateLink`, changes no existing link, and is never seen by Feature #1, live or by the sweep. There is no lead to review, and a `LeadDuplicateLink` needs two lead rows; the row's outcome is explained on the import detail page (duplicate-of link, C's partial-match labels, D's alias to the survivor). This is the recorded Feature #7 policy ("never merges or overwrites an existing Lead", "no automatic merge behavior") standing, not a gap.
- **Not implemented, by decision (not needed, or separate/unapproved)**: enqueueing duplicate detection after an import (near-zero value — the importer already rejects identity matches — for a queue pass per imported lead); import provenance (file name, row, date) in Feature #1's duplicate banner (which shows only the other lead's identifier, name and "Matched on Email/Phone"; the other lead's own page already shows its source, and the import file is reachable only through an unindexed reverse lookup on `lead_import_rows.lead_id`); a skipped-row review workflow; skipped-row export; creating leads from skipped rows (which would reverse Feature #7's skip-only policy and DB-2's purpose). Provenance display, review and export remain separate future items if ever wanted, each needing its own decision.
- **Regression coverage** (7 tests, 64 assertions, new `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportFeatureOneIntegrationTest.php`, real importer + real detection + real sweep): an imported lead is found by Feature #1 when a later lead shares its normalized email or phone (formats deliberately differing; importing writes 0 links; the same single pair from either side); the sweep finds an imported lead's duplicate without either lead being opened, idempotently, within the tenant only; the sweep scans imported leads themselves as subjects (two imported leads that later share an identity are flagged); an import never writes a duplicate link; no kind of skipped row (existing-lead duplicate, partial match, A1 conflict, merged identity, in-file duplicate) creates a lead or a link, is seen by `detectFor()` or the sweep, or changes an unrelated lead, with C's and D's row outcomes exactly as before; and a skipped row leaves an existing pending Feature #1 pair untouched while the import page still shows C's labels and D's survivor. The tests were proven sensitive by temporarily mutating production code and restoring it: excluding CSV-imported leads from detection fails the live-detection tests, and excluding them from the sweep fails the sweep test that scans imported subjects (a first version of the suite did not catch the latter, so that case was added).
- **Verification**:
  - `LeadImportFeatureOneIntegrationTest`: **7 passed** (64 assertions); detection/merge/importer/A1/B2/C/D/PROD-1/UX-1 related suites: 262 passed (867 assertions).
  - `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1309 tests, 1308 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one).
  - Full suite: **1561 tests, 1558 passed, 1 skipped, 2 failed** — both the known stale `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` tests (they expect `'$refresh'` while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`), pre-existing, unrelated, NOT an INC-1 E regression.
  - Import query budget: the moderate-import test still measures **320 queries** (limit 330), unchanged. Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434**, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.
- **Exact files changed for E**: new `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadImportFeatureOneIntegrationTest.php` and this file — **no production code, schema, migration, config, view or documentation other than this file changed**. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree; it was not part of INC-1 and was not touched, staged, or committed. A1, B2, C, D, DB-2, Feature #1, PROD-1 and UX-1 behavior is unchanged.

**This entry closes E, and with it the last open candidate of INC-1: the INC-1 finding as a whole (A1, B2, C, D and E) is now CLOSED.** Do not reopen INC-1 or any of its parts unless a future change causes a real regression against the approved behavior recorded above. Not part of INC-1, and still separate and unaddressed: the adjacent importer-deleted silent drop, blank-line phantom failures and raw exception exposure in the importer; the inbound-email merged-loser finding (`ProcessEmailLeadSource` can attach an email from a merged loser's address to the retired loser); and the stale `LeadsIndexListenerTest`.

### OPS-1 — Scheduled Command Failure / Heartbeat Monitoring — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

One of the findings surfaced by the same fresh forensic audit as SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4/QA-1/QA-2/ARCH-1/ARCH-2/UX-1/PROD-1/INC-1 above, investigated through the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence. (`PROGRESS.md` itself only ever named OPS-1 as a bare ID; the approved definition — "scheduled command failure / heartbeat monitoring" — was confirmed by the user against the original audit row before implementation.)

**Original finding**: none of the scheduled commands in `routes/console.php` (8 by the time of closure, after EMAIL-LOSS-1 added `leads:retry-failed-emails`) had any failure hook, ping or alert, and no monitoring integration existed anywhere in the repo, so a scheduler that stopped running, a command that kept failing, or an IMAP source that could no longer connect would silently halt email ingestion, alerts, scoring, duplicate sweeps and nurturing until someone noticed. Inspection refined it: Laravel's own `schedule:run` already `report()`s a non-zero exit to the log (nothing alerted anyone), and the audit's headline scenario — expired IMAP credentials — would **not** have been caught by a scheduler-level hook at all, because `leads:fetch-emails` only dispatches `ProcessEmailLeadSource` jobs and `imap_open()` failing inside the job was logged and returned normally (no exception, no failed job, no exit code).

**Approved scope — Option A (scheduler-level observability) plus a narrowly-scoped Option B (IMAP connection-failure visibility). No general monitoring platform, no dashboard, no notification center, no migration.**
- **Scheduler failure hooks (all 8 commands)**: every command is registered through one helper in `routes/console.php` that attaches an `onFailure` hook calling `App\Services\ScheduledTaskMonitor::failed()`. A non-zero exit is always logged (`Log::error` carrying only the command name, exit code and timestamp — command output is never captured) and, when configured, emailed at most once per `ops.scheduler_alert_throttle_minutes` (default 60) per command. Cadences and the existing `withoutOverlapping()` flags are unchanged; a run skipped by `withoutOverlapping()` is not a failure. Verified end to end with a real `schedule:run` whose `leads:fetch-emails` subprocess genuinely failed.
- **`OPS_ALERT_EMAIL`** (optional, via the new `config/ops.php`; documented in `.env.example`): the only recipient — never hard-coded. Unset, empty or invalid = log only; nothing fails. Sent through `App\Services\OpsAlertNotifier` / `App\Notifications\OpsAlertNotification` with `notifyNow()` (deliberately not queued, so a dead worker cannot suppress an alert). Delivery is synchronous and best-effort: a delivery exception is caught and logged by class only, never propagating into the scheduler.
- **Throttle correction found during final review**: the first implementation released the throttle marker when an email send failed, so a broken mailer plus a command failing every minute would have retried delivery every minute (each attempt a synchronous SMTP call inside the sequential scheduler). The marker is now kept whether or not the send succeeds — the throttle limits attempts, not just delivered emails: every failure is still logged, delivery is attempted once per window (2 attempts over 120 minutes of continuous failure, versus 120 before), and a regression test fails against the old behavior.
- **Scheduler heartbeat (optional, `leads:fetch-emails` only)**: `OPS_SCHEDULER_HEARTBEAT_URL` receives a bare `GET` (no body, no query string, no tenant data; 5s timeout, 3s connect timeout) only when the every-minute command finishes successfully; unset/empty disables it; a failed run and the other 7 commands never ping. A non-2xx response or any transport error is logged as a warning without the URL and never fails the command. Implemented directly rather than with Laravel's `pingOnSuccess()` for a concrete reason: that callback `report()`s the Guzzle exception, whose message embeds the full URL (verified empirically — a secret token would land in the logs), catches only Guzzle transfer exceptions (a malformed URL would escape and fail a healthy command), and uses 10s/30s timeouts. Verified end to end against a local receiver (exactly one bare GET). It proves the **scheduler** is executing — it says nothing about the queue worker.
- **IMAP connection-failure visibility** (`ProcessEmailLeadSource`, `App\Services\ImapConnectionHealthMonitor`): only the existing `imap_open()`-failure branch changed — after its unchanged `Log::error` it records a consecutive-failure count and returns exactly as before; a successful connection resets the state (with an info log if an alert had fired). One cache counter per lead source (`ops:imap-connection-failures:{source id}`) holds only the count — no credentials, host, error text or email content. The alert fires (`Log::critical` plus the optional email) when the count reaches `ops.imap_failure_threshold` (default 5, ~5 minutes of continuous failure since a failing source is re-polled every minute). Deduplication needs no separate marker: `Cache::increment()` is atomic (database store: a `lockForUpdate()` transaction) and hands each concurrent caller a distinct value, so exactly one observes the threshold. The window is fixed at the first failure by `Cache::add()`'s TTL (`ops.imap_failure_window_minutes`, default 1440) — increments never touch `expiration`, so continued failures neither extend nor reset it and a still-broken source re-alerts about once per window, not every minute. Verified against the real PostgreSQL database cache store (expiration identical across increments; an expired row restarts at 1 with a fresh window; recovery deletes the row) and a real 8-process concurrency probe (200 increments, 200 unique values, the threshold value seen exactly once). Retry runs (`leads:retry-failed-emails`) that cannot connect share the same per-source counter.
