- **Tenant-safe payloads**: every log line, alert email and the heartbeat carry only platform-level facts — command name, exit code, timestamps, and (IMAP) the lead source's random ULID plus a failure count. Never a tenant name/email, lead identifier/name, message content, credential, IMAP host or connection string, or exception message. The source ULID identifies no tenant or person and is the minimum an operator needs to locate the failing source.
- **Unchanged**: EMAIL-LOSS-1 (per-message failure/retry semantics, `FailedEmailMessage`, `last_synced_at` behavior on a failed connection), successful email ingestion, `nurture-inactive-leads` exit semantics (it still catches per-tenant errors and returns success), INC-1 A1/B2, DB-2, Feature #1, PROD-1, UX-1, OPS-2 and UX-2. No migration and no schema change.

**Regression coverage** (27 new test definitions, 32 executed cases, in the new `tests/Feature/Ops/`): `ScheduledTaskMonitoringTest.php` — exactly the 8 approved commands are scheduled and every one reports its own failure; the log line has only command/exit code/timestamp; the alert email carries no tenant data, credentials or output (real tenant, lead and source data seeded to prove it); the configured recipient receives it; unset/empty/invalid `OPS_ALERT_EMAIL` still logs and breaks nothing; a successful run alerts nothing; the per-command email throttle (and its expiry); a broken mailer never propagates, is logged without its message, and cannot cause a per-minute retry storm; the heartbeat is a bare GET sent only by `leads:fetch-emails` on success, disabled when unset, and never fails the command nor logs the URL (connection error, 500 and malformed URL). `ImapConnectionHealthTest.php` — a real `imap_open()` failure is counted and raises exactly one alert at the threshold (none below it, none after); the exact 1→5 walk; alert payload safety; dedup across many failures; recovery reset and re-alert on the next outage; intermittent failures never alert; per-source isolation; re-alert once per window and the window not being extended by further failures; cache reset mid-outage restarts safely; log-only mode with the address unset; a cache/mail failure never reaches the job; the connection-failure path itself unchanged (returns normally, `last_synced_at` untouched, no `FailedEmailMessage`); retry runs share the counter.

**Verification**:
- `tests/Feature/Ops/`: **32 passed** (84 assertions); relevant email-ingestion/retry/unit set: 49 passed, 1 skipped.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1251 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one).
- Full suite: 1497 tests, 1494 passed, 1 skipped, **2 failed — both `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php`** (expects `'$refresh'` for the `lead.received`/`lead.updated` listeners while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`). Reproduced identically on a clean checkout of `a691dd0` before any OPS-1 change: a **pre-existing, unrelated stale test outside the OPS-1 diff, NOT an OPS-1 regression, and deliberately not touched.**
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434** with this closure's diff, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for OPS-1**: `routes/console.php`, `app/Jobs/ProcessEmailLeadSource.php` (an import and two calls), `.env.example`, new `config/ops.php`, `app/Services/ScheduledTaskMonitor.php`, `app/Services/ImapConnectionHealthMonitor.php`, `app/Services/OpsAlertNotifier.php`, `app/Notifications/OpsAlertNotification.php`, `docs/lead-management/OPS-MONITORING.md` (operator documentation), `tests/Feature/Ops/ScheduledTaskMonitoringTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Ops/ImapConnectionHealthTest.php`, and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of OPS-1 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**Known limitations — explicitly documented, deferred, NOT part of this closure**:
- **Queue-worker liveness is not implemented.** Nothing inside the application can report that its own worker died; the heartbeat proves only that the scheduler is executing. A completely dead queue worker (which also silently stops IMAP polling, notifications and CSV imports) requires independent external monitoring.
- **Scheduler failure before a command starts** (e.g. a mutex/cache error raised before the command process begins) is logged by Laravel's own handler but is **not** emailed — `onFailure` only fires for a command that actually ran and exited non-zero.
- **Alert email delivery is synchronous** (inline, by design): a hung SMTP server can delay the sequential `schedule:run` for the mailer's timeout, at most once per throttle window per command.
- **`MAIL_MAILER=log` (the shipped default) means email alerts remain log-only** until a real mailer is configured.
- **IMAP health state is cache-based**: a cache reset or cache-store outage can cause one repeated or delayed alert (worst case); monitoring errors are swallowed and logged as warnings, never affecting a command or job. No real external IMAP server was available for integration testing — the connection-failure path was exercised against a closed local port, recovery at monitor level.
- `nurture-inactive-leads`' exit semantics (it swallows per-tenant errors and returns success) were deliberately not changed. OPS-2 and UX-2 remain separate and untouched.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression against the approved behavior above. Every other finding from the fresh forensic audit that remains open — the broader INC-1 (C, D, E deferred), OPS-2 and UX-2 — is explicitly **not** addressed by this closure.

### OPS-2 — `.env.example` Broadcast / Reverb Configuration Consistency — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

One of the findings surfaced by the same fresh forensic audit as SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4/QA-1/QA-2/ARCH-1/ARCH-2/UX-1/PROD-1/INC-1/OPS-1 above, investigated through the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence. (`PROGRESS.md` itself only ever named OPS-2 as a bare ID; the user confirmed the definition — ".env.example broadcast configuration inconsistency" — against the original audit row before implementation.)

**Original finding (LOW, Operational/Deployment)**: `laravel/reverb` is a direct dependency and real-time updates (notification bell, `lead.received`/`lead.updated`/import-status broadcasts) are core UX, yet the committed `.env.example` shipped `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=log` — the log broadcaster writes events to the log and pushes nothing to any client — so a fresh clone or deployment that copied it got a working app with silently non-functional real-time features. The audit also claimed no script started `reverb:start`.

**Inspection refined it in both directions.** (1) The `reverb:start` half was **obsolete**: `composer run dev` now runs `php artisan dev`, whose registered processes already include `reverb → php artisan reverb:start` (alongside `server`, `queue`, `logs`, `vite`); it is not treated as an active defect. (2) The `.env.example` half was **larger than the audit described**: the file contained *none* of the Reverb variables — neither the six server-side `REVERB_*` nor the four `VITE_REVERB_*` that `resources/js/echo.js` reads (baked in at `npm run build`). With `VITE_REVERB_APP_KEY` undefined, `pusher-js` throws `You must pass your app key when you instantiate Pusher.` at client load, and simply flipping `BROADCAST_CONNECTION` to `reverb` without the variables would make every queued broadcast throw (`MalformedUriException` on an empty host).

**Approved scope — Option A only (a configuration/template correction; no code change).** Committed `.env.example` is now a coherent, Reverb-ready local-development template:
- `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=reverb` (was `log`).
- A complete local Reverb block: `REVERB_APP_ID=local-dev-app-id`, `REVERB_APP_KEY=local-dev-key`, `REVERB_APP_SECRET=local-dev-secret` — clearly marked, in a comment directly above them, as **local-development-only placeholders, not production credentials** (production must use unique generated credentials and must not reuse them; `php artisan reverb:install` can generate/regenerate proper local configuration) — plus `REVERB_HOST=localhost`, `REVERB_PORT=8080`, `REVERB_SCHEME=http` (the exact set and values `reverb:install` itself writes).
- `VITE_REVERB_APP_KEY`, `VITE_REVERB_HOST`, `VITE_REVERB_PORT`, `VITE_REVERB_SCHEME` referencing the corresponding public `REVERB_*` values with `"${…}"` interpolation (verified with both Laravel's Dotenv and Vite's own `loadEnv`). **`VITE_REVERB_APP_SECRET` is intentionally absent** — the Reverb secret is never exposed to the browser (a Vite build with these values contained the public key and no occurrence of the secret).
- Comments documenting that `php artisan dev` already starts the Reverb server, and that `npm run build` (or a `npm run dev` restart) must happen after the `VITE_REVERB_*` values are configured because Vite bakes them into the frontend build.
- **No real production secret was added**, and the existing real `.env` (git-ignored) and the running Reverb process were not touched.

**Explicitly NOT implemented — Option B**: no client-side guard was added to `resources/js/echo.js` (a build without Reverb configured still throws at load, by decision). **No application, config, routing, composer or frontend source code was changed**: `resources/js/echo.js`, `config/broadcasting.php`, `config/reverb.php`, `routes/channels.php` and `composer.json` are unchanged, as are the Reverb server, the broadcast events and channel authorization.

**Regression coverage** (7 new tests, new `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/EnvExampleReverbConfigTest.php`): the example contains every Reverb variable the broadcasting config, the Reverb config and `echo.js` consume (names are discovered from those consumers, so a rename cannot silently drift); the browser never receives the secret (no `VITE_REVERB_APP_SECRET`, no `VITE_` line referencing a secret); no real secret ships (every credential-like value is empty, `null` or an explicit `local-dev-*` placeholder); an application booted with `.env.example` loaded as its environment file selects `reverb` with the publisher, the Reverb server's app registry and the listening port consistent; the browser variables resolve to the same public values; real environment variables still override the example values (normal Laravel precedence preserved); and `php artisan dev` still lists `reverb:start`. Against the previous example 3 of the 7 fail; a leaky variant (a `VITE_` secret plus a real-looking secret) fails 5 of the 7.

**Fresh-environment and live validation (disposable, nothing left behind)**: with example-derived values on a spare port, a real `reverb:start` accepted a backend broadcast, rejected one signed with a wrong secret ("Authentication signature invalid"), accepted a `pusher-js` client using only the public values, and rejected a client with a wrong key. A simulated fresh-clone CI (`.env.example` as the environment file, with a generated `APP_KEY` as `composer setup` does) kept the broadcasting/notification suites green — the tests stay insulated by `phpunit.xml`'s `BROADCAST_CONNECTION=null`. No code path assumes the log broadcaster. Recorded compatibility note (a consequence of selecting `reverb`, not a defect): if the Reverb server is not running, queued broadcast jobs fail with a `BroadcastException` where the log driver never would — which is why the example documents that `php artisan dev` starts Reverb.

**Verification**:
- `EnvExampleReverbConfigTest`: **7 passed** (91 assertions); `tests/Feature/Notifications`: **45 passed**; `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/`: 25 tests, 23 passed, the **2 known `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures** the only failures (23 of 23 without it).
- Full suite: 1504 tests, 1501 passed, 1 skipped, 2 failed — both `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` (it expects `'$refresh'` for the `lead.received`/`lead.updated` listeners while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`). **Pre-existing, unrelated stale test debt** — reproduced on clean checkouts of `a691dd0` and `1f50661` before any OPS-2 change, outside the OPS-2 diff, **NOT an OPS-2 regression**, and deliberately not touched.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434** with this closure's diff, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for OPS-2**: `.env.example`, new `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/EnvExampleReverbConfigTest.php`, and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of OPS-2 and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**Separate, deferred, NOT part of this closure** (not reopened, not absorbed): the Option B `echo.js` client guard; `config/reverb.php` `allowed_origins => ['*']`; the missing Reverb entry in `OPS-MONITORING.md`'s "Required processes"; `CLAUDE.md`'s stale dev-loop description; the `REVERB_PORT` config default (443) versus the Reverb server default (8080) outside the example values; the stale `LeadsIndexListenerTest`; UX-2 and any other unrelated finding.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression against the approved behavior above. UX-2 remains open and is explicitly **not** addressed by this closure.

### UX-2 — Missing `wire:loading` Disabled-State Guards (Confirm Modal / Bulk Actions / Resend) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

One of the findings surfaced by the same fresh forensic audit as SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4/QA-1/QA-2/ARCH-1/ARCH-2/UX-1/PROD-1/INC-1/OPS-1/OPS-2 above, investigated through the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence. (`PROGRESS.md` itself only ever named UX-2 as a bare ID; the definition below comes from the original audit row — LOW, Livewire/UI–Backend contract — confirmed by the user before implementation.)

**Original finding**: the shared `x-confirm-modal` Confirm button (used app-wide, including Lead delete), the bulk-action toolbar "Apply" buttons and the message "Resend" button carried no `wire:loading.attr="disabled"` guard against a double click firing two identical requests. The audit itself rated it cosmetic: the backend is independently idempotent everywhere it checked (`applyBulk*` are natural no-ops on repeat, `confirmMerge()` is protected by the closed M8 fix, `resendMessage()` by the closed `resend_message_id` lock-and-check, hard delete is a no-op the second time).

**Inspection found the audit's premise partially obsolete.** Its evidence was a grep for the literal `wire:loading.attr="disabled"` string, which missed the framework-supplied guards already in the installed stack (verified against the rendered HTML, the installed Flux button view, the built CSS and Livewire's JS): (1) **Flux** gives every `wire:click` button `wire:loading.attr="data-flux-loading"` plus a `wire:target` scoped to its click expression, and its CSS applies `pointer-events: none` and a spinner while the request is in flight; Livewire 4 also sets `data-loading` on the clicking element. Mouse and touch double-clicks were therefore already blocked. (2) The bulk **Apply** buttons are `type="submit"` buttons inside `<form wire:submit>` forms, where Livewire's built-in `disableForm()` marks submit buttons `disabled` from the submit event until the request completes — natively guarded, exactly the audited lines 63/78/97/114.

**The real residual gap was narrow**: on `wire:click` buttons the in-flight state is a `data-*` attribute plus `pointer-events: none`, which does **not** stop a second **keyboard** (Enter/Space) activation and is not exposed to assistive technology as a disabled state.

**Approved scope — Option B, limited to the two audited residual gaps; nothing else.** Explicit `wire:loading.attr="disabled"` was added to exactly two buttons (one line each):
1. the shared `x-confirm-modal` Confirm button (`resources/views/components/confirm-modal.blade.php`);
2. the lead message Resend button (`resources/views/livewire/tenant/leads/partials/message-composer-card.blade.php`).

Livewire now sets the native `disabled` attribute when the button's own request starts and removes it when the request finishes, blocking keyboard reactivation and exposing the disabled state to assistive technology. The existing `wire:target` and `wire:click` action are unchanged, the Flux spinner still renders (Livewire's `data-loading` still drives it), and the button additionally takes Flux's standard `disabled:opacity-50` dimming during the request (an accepted, small visual state). **Bulk Apply buttons were intentionally NOT modified** — their `wire:submit` forms already use Livewire's native `disableForm()` — and a test locks that structure for all four bulk forms (status, priority, tag, assign).

**Compatibility**: all 24 `x-confirm-modal` usages across 20 views were reviewed. Every one passes a plain, parameterless `confirm-action`; none uses keyboard handlers around the button or depends on it staying activatable during its own request. A validation-error path (`confirmDuplicateUpload`) re-enables the button when the request ends, and a redirecting path stays disabled until navigation (a repeat is harmless — the INC-1 B2 confirm clears its pending state first). No caller was changed. Backend behavior, authorization and idempotency are unchanged; **no JavaScript, schema, config, route or backend change was needed.**

**Regression coverage** (6 test cases, new `tests/Feature/Leads/InFlightActionGuardTest.php`): the shared Confirm button carries `wire:loading.attr="disabled"`, keeps its exact `wire:target`/`wire:click`/`type="button"` and renders its spinner markup inside the button itself, for all three variants in use (danger, primary, filled) while the Cancel button is unchanged; a real usage (the Users delete modal) renders the guarded Confirm; the Resend button on the real lead page carries the attribute with its exact action and target; and all four bulk `wire:submit` forms keep their submit-type Apply button with no explicit `wire:loading` attribute. Against the original markup 5 of the 6 fail (the bulk lock passes both ways by design); removing the spinner from Confirm fails all three confirm cases. The tests lock the rendered contract Livewire acts on in the browser — a component test cannot run two overlapping browser requests, and none is claimed.

**Verification**:
- `InFlightActionGuardTest`: **6 passed** (38 assertions); related suites (message composer, bulk actions, both CSV import files, `CreateLeadFromImportTest`, merged-lead deletion protection, `StaleMergedLeadShowTest`, `FailedEmailMessageRetryTest`): 243 passed.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1258 tests, 1257 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failures** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one).
- Full suite: 1510 tests, 1507 passed, 1 skipped, **2 failed — both `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php`** (it expects `'$refresh'` for the `lead.received`/`lead.updated` listeners while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`). **Pre-existing, unrelated stale test debt** — reproduced on clean checkouts before any UX-2 change, outside the UX-2 diff, **NOT a UX-2 regression**, and deliberately not touched.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434** with this closure's diff, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed for UX-2**: `resources/views/components/confirm-modal.blade.php`, `resources/views/livewire/tenant/leads/partials/message-composer-card.blade.php`, new `tests/Feature/Leads/InFlightActionGuardTest.php`, and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree during this work; it was not part of UX-2 and was not touched, staged, or committed. UX-1, PROD-1, INC-1 A1/B2, OPS-1, OPS-2, EMAIL-LOSS-1, DB-2, Feature #1 and Feature #15 are unchanged.

**Known limitations — documented, NOT part of this closure**: in some browsers a disabled focused button can lose keyboard focus (inherent to `disabled`; no JS, `aria-busy` or focus management was added). No live-browser double-click test exists — the guard's behavior rests on the rendered markup and Livewire's directive source.

**Separate, deferred, NOT part of this closure** (not reopened, not absorbed): the two raw `<button wire:click>` elements in `notification-bell.blade.php` (no Flux automatic guard); `bulkUnassign`'s native `wire:confirm` (contradicts the project's no-native-confirm rule); the other ~130 `wire:click` buttons that rely on the framework's automatic guards; general accessibility cleanup; and the stale `LeadsIndexListenerTest`.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression against the approved behavior above.

### Webhook `tenant_code` Enumeration Oracle (Non-Uniform Webhook Resolver 404 Disclosure) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new roadmap feature and not an independently-numbered audit ID: the "recommended future cross-cutting fix" recorded in §20 ("Known Technical Debt / Risks") since Feature #13's closure, started by an explicit user request after the roadmap had no active item, and taken through the full Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture/Decision Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Regression → Final Verification → Approval sequence.

**Original finding**: the public webhook resolvers answered "this does not resolve" with `firstOrFail()`, whose `ModelNotFoundException` message names the model that was missing. An anonymous caller could therefore tell "no such `tenant_code`" (`No query results for model [App\Models\Tenant]`) from "the tenant exists but has no such source/form" (`…[App\Models\LeadSource]` / `…[App\Models\WebsiteForm]`) — a tenant-existence oracle that also leaked model class names. **Verified at runtime, with `APP_DEBUG=false`**, on the legacy website-form POST, the WhatsApp and Instagram verification GETs and the form-builder POST (the Messenger/WhatsApp/Instagram chat POSTs already returned an identical 200, and the Facebook verification GET already returned a uniform empty 404). Tenant codes are sequential (`TEN-000001`, …), so the incremental exposure was limited, but the disclosure was real and the leak of model names unnecessary.

**Fix — controller-level resolver hardening in `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` only (Option A)**: exactly two private methods changed. `resolveSource()` and `resolveWebsiteForm()` now use `first()` plus a single `abort_if(…, 404)` instead of `firstOrFail()`: an unknown tenant code, an inactive/suspended tenant, a missing or inactive source or form, an unknown or inactive form token, and another tenant's form token (the form-token/tenant pairing check is kept inside the same condition) all produce the **same plain 404 with a model-free body — exactly `{"message": ""}`**, the body the Facebook verification GET already returned. The always-200 chat handlers and `resolveSourceOrNull()` catch `Throwable`, so an HTTP 404 is caught exactly as the model exception was. **No global exception renderer was introduced** (inspection showed the controller-level approach covers every intended path), **no migration or schema change**, and no change to tenant-code generation, webhook URLs, valid webhook behavior, verification-token semantics, signature verification (`VerifyMetaWebhookSignature`), throttling, payload processing or tenant isolation.

**Intentionally unchanged (not a new finding)**: the credentials' own responses on a *resolved* source — a wrong verification token's 403, a wrong legacy API key's 401, a bad Meta signature's 403 and the 200 success responses — are the authentication contract, not resolution, and remain as they were; so does a sub-millisecond timing difference between an unknown tenant (one query, short-circuited) and a source-less tenant (two queries), which is not a response-body signal.

**Regression coverage** (10 test cases, 252 assertions, new `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTenantOracleTest.php`, rendering errors production-style with `app.debug` off): for each of the three hub-verification GETs, the legacy website-form POST and the form-builder POST, every unresolvable combination (unknown tenant, existing tenant with no source/form, suspended tenant, inactive source/form, unknown token, another tenant's token) returns the same status and body with no `Tenant`/`LeadSource`/`WebsiteForm`/model-class text; every unresolvable 404 across all route families is one identical response; the chat POST webhooks still answer an unknown tenant and a source-less one identically (200); and valid requests behave exactly as before (valid handshakes return the challenge and a wrong verify token its own 403; the legacy form creates a lead with the right key and returns 401 with a wrong one; a valid form-builder submission creates its lead; no lead is created by any unresolvable request). Against the original resolvers 5 of the 10 fail (the Instagram and WhatsApp verify tests, the legacy form, the form-builder and the cross-family test); Facebook verify, the chat POSTs and the valid-behavior test pass in both states by design.

**Verification**:
- `WebhookTenantOracleTest`: **10 passed** (252 assertions); related webhook / website-form / signature-verification / rate-limit / attachment / profile-lookup / Lead Ads / dual-write coverage: **165 passed** (879 assertions).
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1319 tests, 1318 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed** (the skip is the known pre-existing environment-conditional one).
- Full suite: **1571 tests, 1568 passed, 1 skipped, 2 failed** — both the known stale `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` tests (they expect `'$refresh'` while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`): pre-existing, unrelated, NOT a regression of this fix. `KanbanBoardTest`'s board-search test and `LeadsExportTest`'s "the export respects the search filter" each failed once in a `tests/Feature/Leads/` run — random-data flakes (a search term matching faker-generated data on another lead) that pass individually, sit outside this diff, and were not touched.
- A real production-mode HTTP-kernel probe (`APP_DEBUG=false`) returned the identical `{"message": ""}` 404 for every unknown-versus-existing-tenant case across the verify GETs, the legacy website form and the form-builder, with no existence-dependent response headers.
- Pint: clean. PHPStan: **434 pre-existing errors on unmodified `main` → 434**, **0 net-new diagnostics**. `git diff --check`: clean.

**Exact files changed**: `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` (the two private resolvers only), new `tests/Feature/Leads/WebhookTenantOracleTest.php`, and this file. An unrelated, pre-existing uncommitted documentation edit to `CLAUDE.md` existed in the working tree; it was not part of this fix and was not touched, staged, or committed.

**This finding is CLOSED.** Do not reopen unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. Still separate and unaddressed, and not part of this closure: the CSV importer's adjacent observations (the silent drop when the importing user is deleted mid-import, blank-line phantom failures, raw exception text in row errors), the inbound-email merged-loser lookup in `ProcessEmailLeadSource`, and the stale `LeadsIndexListenerTest`.

### WhatsApp Inbound Calling — Build Step 1 (Data Foundation) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new numbered roadmap feature, and **only Build Step 1 is closed — the capability as a whole is NOT complete.** It is the first of the build steps of an explicitly requested capability (receiving WhatsApp calls in the browser through the WhatsApp Business Calling API on the existing Cloud API `whatsapp` lead source), taken through Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture Proposal → a second, deeper architecture pass (exact call state-transition matrix, frozen routing snapshot, call-specific authorization, a re-verification of Meta's current Calling API contract, idempotency/failure matrices, softphone ownership, lead merge/delete semantics, a manual test plan) → **explicit user approval** → implementation → a correction/verification pass → this closure. Step 1 is the data foundation only: no behavior a user or a webhook can observe changed.

**Approved product decisions (recorded exactly, unchanged by this step)**: a new caller rings all active Client Admins with lead-management module access (auto-assignment is a queued listener and `AutoAssignLead` is deliberately untouched, so the queued assignment must never change an active call's frozen routing snapshot); a caller with no usable phone number / BSUID-only identity is ignored and logged in Phase 1 (no Lead, no call); an opt-in "stay available for calls" session heartbeat with an absolute cap — explicitly NOT presence/availability tracking; STUN-first, TURN-ready, no long-lived TURN credential ever exposed to the browser; a second incoming call while in a call is decline-only (no waiting/hold/transfer); withdrawn consent does not block an inbound, customer-initiated call (it authorizes no outbound message or call); terminal call records cascade-delete with their Lead and a Ringing/Answering/Connected call blocks the Lead's deletion; if `calling_enabled` is false, arriving Meta call events are ignored (no Lead, no call, log only the call id/count — never SDP or a full phone number) and only the source's observed Meta calling status marker is updated.

**Delivered (Step 1 only)**:
- **`lead_calls`** (`2026_09_21_070000_create_lead_calls_table.php`) — ULID key; `tenant_id`, `lead_id`, `lead_source_id`, nullable `conversation_id`; `external_call_id` (Meta's `wacid.…`) with a unique `(lead_source_id, external_call_id)` idempotency key; `status`, `end_reason`, `meta_stage`, `meta_status`, `meta_terminated_at`; the frozen routing snapshot `assignee_id_at_arrival` / `routing_mode` / `routed_user_ids` (JSON); `claimed_by`, `claimed_at`, `connected_at`, `ended_at`, `duration_seconds`, `ringing_expires_at`; indexes `(tenant_id, lead_id, created_at)` and `(status, ringing_expires_at)`. FK semantics: tenant **cascade**, lead **cascade**, lead_source **restrict**, conversation / `assignee_id_at_arrival` / `claimed_by` **nullOnDelete**. Every enum-backed value is a plain string column with no DB CHECK (same shape as `tasks`/`deals`). The SDP offer/answer is never stored on this row.
- **`App\Models\LeadCall`** (`#[Fillable]`, enum/array/datetime casts, an `active()` scope, `isActive()`, relations to lead / source / conversation / claiming user / arrival assignee; no `BelongsToTenant` trait, matching `Conversation`/`Message` — every query filters `tenant_id` explicitly) and `Lead::calls()`.
- **Enums**: `CallStatus` (Ringing/Answering/Connected are active; Completed/Missed/Rejected/Failed terminal; `allowedNext()`/`canTransitionTo()` encode only the *structural* edges of the approved matrix — the guards on each edge belong to the later state-machine service), `CallEndReason`, and two small additions beyond the two enums first asked for, `CallRoutingMode` (`assignee`/`admin_fallback`/`none`) and `CallMetaStage` (`pre_accepted`/`accepted`) — they only type-cast approved string columns and cost no schema. `meta_error_<code>` is collapsed into one `MetaError` case because a backed enum cannot carry a dynamic suffix; the raw Meta code, once its shape is verified, lives verbatim in `meta_status` (never branched on).
- **`lead_sources` calling configuration** (`2026_09_21_070001_add_calling_columns_to_lead_sources_table.php`) — real columns, not keys in the encrypted `config` blob (the platform source form rebuilds `config` from its own field list and could silently drop them): `calling_enabled` (default **false** — every existing source behaves exactly as before), `calling_meta_status` (a plain nullable string holding Meta's `ENABLED`/`DISABLED` verbatim — deliberately NOT an enum cast, since `LeadSource` is scanned by `EnumConstraintVerifier`, which would expect a Postgres CHECK), `calling_meta_checked_at`.
- **Merge lifecycle** — `LeadCall` is a plain `lead_id`-keyed child added to `LeadMergeService::CHILD_MODELS`: a loser's calls, active or finished, are reparented onto the survivor with their id, `external_call_id`, status and routing snapshot untouched.
- **Delete lifecycle** — `LeadDeletionEligibilityService::evaluate()` now also refuses a Lead with a Ringing, Answering or Connected call (`activeCallReason()`, tenant-scoped), on every deletion path that already calls it (the advisory openers `Index::confirmDeleteLead()`/`Show::confirmDelete()` and the authoritative locked check inside `Index::delete()`, bulk delete and `Show::delete()`); terminal calls never block and are deleted with the Lead. `RecordLeadDeletionAudit::cascadeSummary()` now counts `calls`. A bulk delete that skips such a Lead reports it in the existing generic "N of M deleted, K skipped" toast — only merged survivors get a dedicated toast, and adding one is UI work left to a later step.

**Migration application and ordering**: the two migrations were first authored with `2026_09_25_…` names, which were future-dated relative to the work date; before they were ever applied or committed they were renamed to `2026_09_21_070000_…` / `2026_09_21_070001_…` (contents unchanged). Nothing dated on or before the work date can sort after the existing `2026_09_24_…` files, so they sort after `2026_09_21_060000` and before the already-applied `09_22`–`09_24` files; every FK target predates them, so a fresh `migrate` (which the SQLite suite runs on every test) is valid in that order, and on an already-migrated database Laravel simply runs them as pending. They were applied to the configured development PostgreSQL database (`optifin_erp`) with a plain, non-destructive `php artisan migrate` (batch 4; only these two were pending; no reset/refresh/drop/rollback). The live schema was read back from the PostgreSQL catalogs and matches the approved model exactly (columns, types, nullability, all six FK delete rules, the unique index), all 5 existing `lead_sources` rows read `calling_enabled = false`, and `schema:verify-enum-constraints` reports all 20 enum-backed constraints matched (no `EnumConstraintVerifier` regression; `LeadCall` is intentionally not in its model list).

**Regression coverage** (45 test cases, 134 assertions): `tests/Unit/Enums/CallStatusTest.php` (the active/terminal sets, the exact next-status list for all 7 states, terminal states never transitioning, an active call never returning to Ringing), `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCallModelTest.php` (the exact approved column list, source defaults, enum/snapshot/timestamp round-trips, the unique Meta call id per source, the `active()` scope, the three `nullOnDelete` references, the source `restrict`), `LeadCallMergeTest.php` (reparenting active and finished calls, identity and routing snapshot preserved) and `LeadCallDeletionTest.php` (terminal calls cascade, the audit's `calls` count, the active-call refusal on `Index::delete`, both advisory openers, `Show::delete` and bulk delete, a Lead becoming deletable once its call ends, all 7 statuses through the evaluator locked and advisory, tenant-scoped lookup). Each of the three lifecycle changes was mutation-checked (removed → the matching tests failed → restored).

**Verification**:
- Step 1 focused: **45 passed** (134 assertions). `MergedLeadDeletionConcurrencyTest` (two real PostgreSQL sessions against the migrated database): **3 passed**. Existing merge/delete suites: **188 passed**.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1395 tests, 1394 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed.** Full suite: **1664 tests, 1661 passed, 1 skipped, 2 failed** — only the known stale tests below.
- Pint: clean. `git diff --check`: clean (tracked and untracked files).
- **PHPStan: 435 errors, 0 net-new diagnostics from Step 1** — a diagnostic-level comparison (file, message and identifier, ignoring line numbers) of a pristine export of `HEAD` without any Step 1 file (435) against the working tree with Step 1 (435) shows 0 added and 0 resolved, and no diagnostic mentions any Step 1 file or symbol. The historical figure in the entries above is 434; the repository now legitimately reports 435 because of **one unrelated, pre-existing diagnostic introduced by an earlier commit, `ee10997` ("scoring rules button added", 2026-09-20)**: a `property.notFound` for `$tenant_id` on the `PlatformUser|TenantUser` union in `app/Livewire/Tenant/LeadManagement/Dashboard.php` (one more occurrence of a pattern that file already had). Confirmed by analysing the documented commit and each later commit touching analysed paths (everything through `76a2f61` = 434, `ee10997` = 435; same PHPStan 2.2.9, unchanged `composer.lock`). No baseline file or unrelated code was changed, and the earlier 434 closure entries are left as recorded.

**Known pre-existing test conditions — NOT Step 1 regressions** (the first two reproduced identically on a pristine `HEAD` export without any Step 1 file; the third is the environment-conditional skip already recorded as pre-existing in the entries above): the two stale `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` tests (they expect `'$refresh'` while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`); `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadDeletionAuditConcurrencyTest.php` is **order-dependent** — it uses the real development database and its `Tenant::factory()` counter restarts at `TEN-000001` in a fresh process, colliding with the real dev tenant `TEN-000001` when the file is run alone or in a small set (it passes inside the full Leads suite once earlier tests have advanced the counter); and the one `FetchEmailLeadsTest` skip when the IMAP extension is unavailable. None were touched or fixed here.

**Exact files changed**: modified `app/Actions/Tenant/RecordLeadDeletionAudit.php`, `app/Models/Lead.php`, `app/Models/LeadSource.php`, `app/Services/LeadDeletionEligibilityService.php`, `app/Services/LeadMergeService.php` (34 additive lines); new `app/Enums/CallStatus.php`, `CallEndReason.php`, `CallRoutingMode.php`, `CallMetaStage.php`, `app/Models/LeadCall.php`, `database/factories/LeadCallFactory.php`, the two migrations above, `tests/Unit/Enums/CallStatusTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadCallModelTest.php`, `LeadCallMergeTest.php`, `LeadCallDeletionTest.php`; and this file.

**Explicitly NOT implemented and NOT claimed** — no Step 2 work exists in the tree: the inbound `calls` webhook branch and `RecordInboundCall`, the Meta Calling API client, the call endpoints and `LeadCallPolicy`, the Reverb call events, the softphone/WebRTC layer, the missed-call notification, the platform admin calling controls, the stale-call reconciliation command, and the manual Meta test-number validation. **Browser calling is not production-ready and no real Meta media/audio has been validated.** The Meta Calling API contract was re-read from Meta's current developer documentation for the architecture pass, but several behaviors remain unverified until a Meta test number is available (the shape of an error object on a terminate webhook, whether a terminate webhook follows our own `reject`/`terminate`, what a repeated `pre_accept`/`accept` returns, trickle-ICE support, the exact ring timeout, webhook ordering/retry) — the approved state machine already defines a deterministic server-side fallback for each, and none may silently change it. The existing WhatsApp text, status and attachment paths and `AutoAssignLead` are unchanged.

**Build Step 1 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen it unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. Steps beyond it each require their own explicit approval and follow the same process.

### WhatsApp Inbound Calling — Build Step 2 (Inbound Webhook + RecordInboundCall) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new numbered roadmap feature, and **only Build Step 2 is closed — the full WhatsApp Calling capability is NOT complete.** It is the second build step of the capability whose data foundation Step 1 closed above, taken through Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture / Decision Brief (with Meta's current Calling API contract re-verified from its developer documentation, keeping verified facts, repository facts and unresolved Meta assumptions apart) → **explicit user approval** (four decisions: terminate handling included, a read-only `CallRouter`, the stale-CONNECT rule, an `app_secret` defense-in-depth gate) → implementation → an independent code review → a hardening pass on three further approved review decisions (three added regression tests, terminate-while-disabled, a seen-call-id marker) → a narrow SDP-fidelity correction → a final review → this closure.

**Step 2 does NOT make the browser ring.** A recorded inbound call is a `lead_calls` row in state Ringing that only a `terminate` webhook (or a later step) moves on; in practice every Step 2 call ends Missed, because nothing can answer it yet. With `calling_enabled` off by default and no admin UI to turn it on, the step is inert for every existing source.

**Delivered (Step 2 only)**:
- **`calls[]` webhook handling** — a purely additive branch inside `LeadWebhookController::whatsapp()` (five insertion-only hunks, **0 pre-existing lines changed**): the existing `statuses[]`/`messages[]` code, the `WA INCOMING` log, the message cap, inbound dedupe, the unconditional 200 and per-item exception isolation are byte-identical. Each call item has its own try/catch (logging the exception class and call id only) and a malformed or throwing call never stops another call or a message. A separate per-request ceiling reuses the message path's configured limit, counted on calls alone.
- **Gate order per item**: the source must have an `app_secret` (defense-in-depth on top of the unchanged, opt-in signature middleware) → the item must parse → a `phone_number_id` mismatch is ignored when both sides are known → a valid signed `connect` records the observed Meta calling marker (`calling_meta_status='ENABLED'`, refreshed at most once a minute, never changing `calling_enabled`, never touched by an invalid, unsigned or unparseable event) → `calling_enabled` must be true to accept a NEW call.
- **`App\Support\WhatsAppCallEvent`** — a pure parser returning an event or a log-safe ignore reason, never an exception. Requires an id (≤191 chars, no whitespace), a `connect`/`terminate` event, a numeric timestamp, and for `connect` a `sdp_type=offer` session whose SDP starts `v=0` and is ≤16 KB and a usable caller (`from`, falling back to `contacts[].wa_id`, never equal to the business's own number). `direction` is optional (only a non-`USER_INITIATED` value is rejected); username/BSUID-only callers are ignored; unknown extra fields are ignored. Its debug dump hides the SDP and caller number.
- **`App\Actions\Tenant\RecordInboundCall`** — the single writer of `lead_calls` for both events. It reuses the message path's exact phone lock (`lead-webhook-dedupe:{source}:{phone}`, so a message and a call from one new number serialize), the same two lead lookups in the same order (merge-aware conversation first, then exact phone), and the controller's existing `createLead()` through an injected closure (so `LeadReceived` still fires exactly once and `AutoAssignLead` is untouched). One short transaction locks the resolved lead row `FOR UPDATE` (the row lock deletion and merge use), follows a merge chain, upserts the conversation, computes the frozen routing snapshot and inserts; the encrypted offer and the seen marker are written only after that transaction has returned, and no cache, crypt, HTTP or log call happens inside either transactional function. `(lead_source_id, external_call_id)` stays the authoritative identity: a duplicate never overwrites a row, and a payload naming a different caller is reported as a conflict and ignored.
- **CONNECT** — duplicate pre-check → lock → duplicate re-check → seen-marker check → tombstone → stale rule → lead resolution (an unknown caller creates exactly one Lead, with `source_data` and `message` null) → row → post-commit caching. A stale connect (`min(event time, now) + 60s` already past) is recorded Missed (`terminate_before_connect`) for a known caller and ignored for an unknown one, so a delayed delivery can never resurrect an erased lead; a connect that meets a tombstone likewise never creates a Lead. A no-eligible-recipient call is recorded Missed (`no_recipient`).
- **TERMINATE** — the approved matrix applied under a row lock in one short transaction with nothing external inside it: Ringing → Missed (`caller_ended_or_not_answered`) or Failed (`meta_error`) when `errors` is present; Answering → Completed only when `start_time` is present and the status token says COMPLETED, else Missed (`ended_before_connect`) or Failed; Connected → Completed (`remote_terminated`) or Failed (`meta_failed_after_connect`). Meta's undocumented `status` enum is never used to invent a status; the raw token or `ERROR:<code>` is stored verbatim in `meta_status`. A terminal row never changes status and only fill-only writes are allowed; `meta_terminated_at` makes a redelivery a no-op. A terminate that arrives first creates a terminal row for a known lead, or — with no lead — a 600-second tombstone (never a Lead).
- **`App\Services\CallRouter`** (read-only) — the frozen routing snapshot: an active assignee holding the lead-management `update` permission is routed alone; otherwise every active Client Admin with `update`, in id order; otherwise none. A lead created by the call always snapshots the admin fallback (auto-assignment is a queued listener, so it must never change an active call's routing). Nothing rings anyone.
- **Conversation** — a recorded call (ringing, stale, no-recipient or terminate-before-connect) find-or-creates the WhatsApp conversation keyed by the caller and moves `last_inbound_at` forward only, never past now (a call opens the messaging window whether or not it is answered, per Meta's pricing documentation). No `Message` row is created; the message timeline is unchanged and the composer simply follows the existing 24-hour window and consent rules.
- **Encrypted SDP cache** — key `wa-call-offer:{tenant}:{source}:{call id}`, `Crypt::encryptString`, TTL `offer_ttl_seconds` (120), two attempts, and a failure moves the row Ringing → Failed (`sdp_cache_failure`). Laravel's `TrimStrings` middleware strips every trailing whitespace character from a JSON webhook body, so the SDP arrives without its RFC 8866 final CRLF; the parser restores exactly one `\r\n` when (and only when) the SDP has no final line terminator and touches nothing else (an SDP that already ends in a terminator is kept byte-for-byte; the 16 KB limit is applied to the string as received). The SDP is never stored in the database, on `Lead.source_data` or in any log.
- **Seen-call-id marker** — `wa-call-seen:{tenant}:{source}:{call id}`, TTL `seen_ttl_seconds` (7 days, Meta's documented webhook retry window), written only after a call row has committed and only for a row this code created from a valid, fully-gated event; consulted only when no row exists; fail-open (an unreadable cache never blocks a call); healed by the next duplicate CONNECT or terminate for the still-existing row. It exists solely so that a redelivery arriving after the call's row was deleted with its lead cannot recreate the erased lead; it is never the call's identity.
- **Calling switched off** — a new CONNECT is ignored (no Lead, no call, no conversation); a TERMINATE for an already-recorded call is still applied (closing an existing call's lifecycle is not accepting a new call); a TERMINATE for an unrecorded call is ignored (no row, no Lead, no tombstone). `calling_enabled` is never changed by any webhook.
- **Config** — `config/calling.php`: `ring_ttl_seconds` 60, `offer_ttl_seconds` 120, `tombstone_ttl_seconds` 600, `seen_ttl_seconds` 604800 (the last added by the approved seen-marker decision).

**Verification** (final pre-commit run, on the tree that was committed):
- Step 2 focused tests: **241 passed / 241** (`WhatsAppCallEventTest` 67, `CallRouterTest` 10, `RecordInboundCallTest` 94, `WhatsappCallWebhookTest` 70 — dataset rows counted individually). Mutation-checked: each protective behaviour (app-secret gate, `phone_number_id` check, stale rule, tombstone, duplicate terminate, encryption, no-SDP-persistence, merge following, shared lock key, unique-race catch, exception-safe logging, seen marker write/check/heal/fail-open, disabled-calling gates, observed-marker gate order, and the SDP terminator restore and its over-corrections) was temporarily broken and a test failed each time; one mutation the SQLite suite cannot detect is the lead-row `FOR UPDATE` (see risks).
- Existing WhatsApp / webhook / attachment / outbound tests (16 files): **201 passed / 201**, unchanged from before Step 2.
- Merge / delete + Step 1 tests: **236 passed / 236**.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1569 tests, 1568 passed, 1 skipped (the IMAP-extension test), 0 failed** in the final run. The known, unrelated random-data `KanbanBoardTest` flake (its faker-random lead occasionally matches the board search `'77'`) failed in two earlier whole-directory runs during the closure review; it references no Step 2 symbol and reproduced on a pristine export of `HEAD` without any Step 2 file (4 of 30 runs there, 1 of 30 on the working tree).
- Full suite: **1905 tests, 1902 passed, 2 failed, 1 skipped** — the failures are only the two known stale `tests/Feature/Broadcasting/LeadsIndexListenerTest.php` tests (they expect `'$refresh'` while `Leads\Index::getListeners()` returns `onLeadReceived`/`onLeadUpdated`), unrelated and not touched.
- Pint: clean. **PHPStan: 435 errors, 0 net-new diagnostics** (diagnostic-level comparison of the working tree against a pristine export of `HEAD`: 0 added, 0 resolved; the 435 baseline is explained in the Step 1 entry above and the earlier 434 closure entries are left as recorded). `git diff --check`: clean (tracked and untracked files).

**Known pre-existing test conditions — NOT Step 2 regressions and not touched**: the two stale `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures; the random-data `KanbanBoardTest` flake above; `tests/Feature/Leads/LeadDeletionAuditConcurrencyTest.php` remains **order-dependent** (it uses the real development database and its `Tenant::factory()` counter collides with the real dev tenant `TEN-000001` when the file is run alone or in a small set; it passes inside the full suites); and the one IMAP-extension skip.

**Interpretations and deviations recorded during the step** (each reported and approved; none contradicts an approved decision): `CallRouter` uses the lead-management `update` permission as the "can take calls" bar (the bar the later answering step will enforce) for both an assignee and the admin fallback; a terminate carrying `errors` with no existing row is recorded Failed (`meta_error`), extending the approved Ringing + errors rule (the approved matrix specified only the no-error case); the stale-CONNECT end reason is `terminate_before_connect` as directed (`ring_timeout` would be marginally more literal — both are terminal Missed rows and no later step distinguishes them); the phone lock and the two lead lookups live in `RecordInboundCall` (replicating the lock key string and the two queries) with lead creation delegated to the controller's `createLead()` through a closure, so the message path was not refactored; the seen-marker log line `WA CALL SEEN MARKER FAILED` carries ids only; `tests/Support/WhatsAppCallFixtures.php` is a shared fixtures class for the four Step 2 test files.

**Remaining risks — intentionally deferred, NOT Step 2 blockers**:
- **The seen marker is not atomic with the DB commit and depends on cache durability.** A write that fails or a process that dies between the commit and the marker (before any heal), a cache flush/eviction, or a non-persistent cache store leaves the protection absent; the stale rule (a redelivery older than the ring window is ignored for an unknown caller) is the second line of defence, so the residual is a redelivery inside the ring window (or with a skewed Meta timestamp) after the lead was deleted. The database cache store also removes an expired row only when it is read, so markers for deleted calls linger (about one small row per call) until a cache flush.
- **A `terminate`'s value-level `errors` array is applied to every terminate item in the same `changes[]` value.** Meta normally sends one call per change and only Ringing rows exist in Step 2, so the worst case is Failed instead of Missed; revisit in Step 4, when Answering/Connected calls exist.
- **A lost `terminate` leaves the row active** (and, through the Step 1 guard, blocks deleting that lead) until Step 6's reconciliation exists; the always-200 contract means Meta does not retry a delivery our handler failed on.
- **The lead-row `FOR UPDATE` serialization (connect vs. merge, connect vs. delete) was verified by static review and deterministic tests only.** No isolated two-session PostgreSQL test environment exists (CI is SQLite-only and the existing real-Postgres tests write into the development database), and none was built.
- **Real browser / WebRTC / media acceptance is unverified.** Whether a real browser accepts Meta's offer end to end (including the restored final CRLF) can only be confirmed in the later manual Meta test-number validation.

**Explicitly NOT implemented and NOT claimed** — no Step 3+ work exists in the tree: the Meta call-action client (`pre_accept`/`accept`/`reject`/`terminate` API calls), the answer/reject/hangup endpoints, `LeadCallPolicy`, the Reverb call events, the softphone/WebRTC browser layer, the missed-call notification, the stale-call reconciliation sweep, the Platform Admin calling UI/settings, the TURN/STUN endpoint, and outbound calling, recording, voicemail, video and transfer. The full WhatsApp Calling capability is therefore **not** complete and browser calling is **not** production-ready. Step 1's schema, `AutoAssignLead` and the existing WhatsApp text/status/attachment paths are unchanged.

**Exact files changed**: modified `app/Http/Controllers/Api/LeadWebhookController.php` (+184 additive lines); new `app/Actions/Tenant/RecordInboundCall.php`, `app/Services/CallRouter.php`, `app/Support/WhatsAppCallEvent.php`, `config/calling.php`, `tests/Support/WhatsAppCallFixtures.php`, `tests/Unit/Support/WhatsAppCallEventTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/CallRouterTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/RecordInboundCallTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/WhatsappCallWebhookTest.php`; and this file.

**Build Step 2 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen it unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. Build Steps 3 and later are NOT STARTED; each requires its own explicit approval and follows the same process.

### WhatsApp Inbound Calling — Build Step 3 (Calling API Client + Platform Admin Controls) — **COMPLETE / APPROVED / CLOSED**

Not a new numbered roadmap feature, and **only Build Step 3 is closed — the full WhatsApp Calling capability is NOT complete.** It is the third build step of the capability whose data foundation (Step 1) and inbound webhook recording (Step 2) are closed above, taken through Inspect → Impact Analysis → Architecture / Decision Brief → **explicit user approval** (four decisions: D1 safe rollout gate, D2 restrictions shown transiently and never persisted, D3 `account_settings_update` webhook deferred, D4 dedicated Graph version) → implementation → an independent final review (PASS) → this closure.

**Step 3 does NOT make the browser ring.** It gives a Platform Admin a way to enable, check and disable Meta's calling switch for one WhatsApp source, and it adds the Meta Calling API client that later steps will use. In production "Enable" stays unavailable until answering exists (D1), so with the gate closed the step changes nothing for any customer.

**Delivered (Step 3 only)**:
- **`App\Services\WhatsAppCallingClient`** — bound to ONE already-resolved `LeadSource`; reads `phone_number_id`/`access_token` only from that source's decrypted config and accepts no substitute. `getSettings()`, `setCallingStatus(bool)`, and (capability only, **never invoked** in Step 3) `preAccept()`, `accept()`, `reject()`, `terminate()`. `Http::withToken()`, JSON bodies, `https://graph.facebook.com/{calling.graph_version}/{phone_number_id}/settings|calls`, no token in a query string, never `include_sip_credentials`. Only GET settings is retried (once, on a connection failure or 5xx); no POST is ever retried.
- **`App\Support\CallingApiResult` / `App\Enums\CallingApiError`** — deterministic categories (Validation, Authentication, Permission, CallingNotEnabled, TemporarilyDisabled, NotEligible, SipNumber, Payment, Limit, Transient, Network, InvalidResponse, Unknown) with fixed user-facing sentences; Meta's own `error.message` is never surfaced. The result carries category, Meta code, HTTP status, `fbtrace_id`, a small parsed `data` array and duration — never a credential, SDP, customer phone number or raw Meta text. A 2xx with an undocumented body is InvalidResponse; a non-JSON error is classified by HTTP status alone.
- **`App\Actions\Platform\ManageWhatsAppCalling`** (+ `App\Support\CallingActionResult`, `App\Enums\CallingControlState`) — `enable()`, `disable()`, `check()`. Every action authorizes `update` on the tenant server-side and resolves the source through `$tenant->leadSources()`, so a cross-tenant id is a 404; no new permission slug.
  - **Enable**: authorize → resolve → WhatsApp / rollout gate / active / `app_secret` / `phone_number_id` / `access_token` validation → POST `{"calling":{"status":"ENABLED"}}` → confirming GET → only a GET reporting ENABLED sets `calling_enabled`, `calling_meta_status` and `calling_meta_checked_at` (one conditional UPDATE). A failed/unreadable confirmation, a POST failure or a POST timeout leaves local state untouched and tells the admin to run Check.
  - **Disable** (allowed with the gate closed): `calling_enabled=false` is written BEFORE any Meta request; the POST is sent once; success records DISABLED, failure/timeout keeps local off and leaves a visible Mismatch; missing credentials still disable locally with a warning. Never auto-retried, never auto re-enabled.
  - **Check**: read-only with respect to intent — writes only `calling_meta_status` / `calling_meta_checked_at`, never `calling_enabled`; a failed Check preserves the previous snapshot; an absent or non-ENABLED/DISABLED status writes nothing.
- **State model** — `calling_enabled` = local intent, `calling_meta_status` = last observed Meta status, `calling_meta_checked_at` = when. Displayed as Off / On / Mismatch / Unverified. Observation writes carry the time their request STARTED and are conditional (`checked_at IS NULL OR checked_at <= stamp`), so a slow older observation can never overwrite a newer one; no DB lock and no distributed lock is held over Meta HTTP.
- **D1 rollout gate** — `calling.enable_action_available` (env `CALLING_ENABLE_ACTION_AVAILABLE`), default **false**. Enforced server-side in the Enable action (before any Meta request) and mirrored in the UI (Enable disabled, "Calling is prepared but browser answering is not available yet…"). Check and Disable are not gated.
- **Platform Admin UI** (`Platform\Clients\LeadSources` + its view) — WhatsApp rows only: state badge, "This app / WhatsApp / checked …" line, Enable / Disable / Check buttons scoped with `wire:loading.attr="disabled"`, Disable through the shared `x-confirm-modal`, Flux toasts, and transient restriction / SIP warnings held in a `#[Locked]` property (never persisted; they disappear on the next page load). Edit / Test / Activate / Delete are unchanged.
- **Config** — `config/calling.php`: `graph_version` (env `CALLING_GRAPH_VERSION`, default `v25.0`, used only by the calling client — the existing senders' Graph versions are untouched), `api_timeout_seconds` 10, `api_connect_timeout_seconds` 3, `enable_action_available` false.
- **Logging** — one structured `WA CALLING ADMIN` line per action on the `whatsapp` channel (tenant, source, action, outcome, category, Meta code, HTTP status, `fbtrace_id`, duration, platform user id); never a token, app secret, Authorization header, SDP, customer phone number or raw Meta text. No migration; no generic LeadSource audit system.

**Verification** (final pre-commit run, on the tree that was committed — fast-forwarded (`--ff-only`) onto `origin/main` `5cd14f5`, which carried two unrelated UI commits with no file overlap):
- Step 3 focused tests: **149 passed / 149** (`WhatsAppCallingClientTest` 68, `ManageWhatsAppCallingTest` 61, `PlatformWhatsAppCallingControlsTest` 20 — dataset rows counted individually). Mutation-checked: the rollout gate, the stale-observation guard, Check-writes-intent, tenant-scoped source lookup, the authorization call, POST retry, GET retry-once and the UI `disabled` on Enable were each temporarily broken and a test failed each time.
- `tests/Feature/Leads/`: **1718 tests, 1717 passed, 1 skipped (the IMAP-extension test), 0 failed** (includes the WhatsApp / webhook / attachment / outbound tests and the Step 1 and Step 2 tests, unchanged).
- Full suite: **2054 tests, 2050–2051 passed, 1 skipped**; the failures are only the two known stale `LeadsIndexListenerTest` tests, plus — in one of the two full runs on the final tree — the known random-data `KanbanBoardTest` "board search does not match…" flake (faker data occasionally matches the search `'77'`; it references no Step 3 symbol, passed 6 of 6 in isolation immediately afterwards, and passed inside `tests/Feature/Leads/` on the same tree).
- Pint: clean. **PHPStan: 435 errors, 0 net-new diagnostics** (no diagnostic in any Step 3 file; one new closure-return diagnostic found during implementation was fixed before closure). `git diff --check`: clean.

**Known pre-existing test conditions — NOT Step 3 regressions and not touched**: the two stale `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures; the random-data `KanbanBoardTest` flake above; `LeadDeletionAuditConcurrencyTest` remains order-dependent (real development database); the one IMAP-extension skip.

**Interpretations and deviations recorded during the step** (each reported; none contradicts an approved decision): two small support files beyond the planned list (`CallingActionResult` for the UI result, the `CallingControlState` enum for the four display states); Enable authorizes before checking the rollout gate (an unauthorized user always gets a 403, never the gate message); Enable sets `calling_enabled` in the same conditional UPDATE as its observation, so a confirmation older than an existing observation turns nothing on and asks the admin to run Check; the unauthorized-user Livewire test goes through the HTTP route because a mount-time authorization failure breaks the Livewire test harness (the action-level authorization is tested directly).

**Accepted risks and unknowns — explicitly reviewed and accepted by the user at closure, NOT Step 3 blockers**:
- **`CALLING_ENABLE_ACTION_AVAILABLE` uses `(bool) env(...)`.** Unset, empty, `0` and `false` are false (the default is provably false — the key is in no `.env*` or `phpunit.xml`), but a typo value such as `off`/`no` is a non-empty string and parses as true. Optional later hardening: `filter_var(..., FILTER_VALIDATE_BOOLEAN)`.
- **An unmapped HTTP 401 falls back to the Authentication message** ("rejected the access token"), which overstates the case for documented calling codes such as 138006 ("No approved call permission", 401). Not reachable from the settings endpoints Step 3 uses.
- **Meta error-code mappings accepted as they are.** 138000, 138014, 138015, 138018, 131055, 131044, 138002/138005 and the transient codes follow Meta's calling documentation; 190, 10, 200–299 and 100 follow general Graph conventions (not calling-specific), and 138009/138012/138013 (business-initiated-calling codes) are mapped to Limit / NotEligible with deliberately generic messages.
- **Enable/Disable race.** A second admin's Enable confirmation landing while a Disable POST is in flight can leave local intent on and Meta off. It is shown as Mismatch (Meta being off, no calls arrive) and cleared by Check. Accepted; not tested.
- **One-second timestamp granularity.** Observations in the same second are both accepted (the later arrival wins). The conditional UPDATE has only run on SQLite; no isolated PostgreSQL test environment exists.
- **Meta unknowns deferred to the manual test-number validation (Step 7)**: whether the minimal ENABLE body is accepted, where and under which key Meta returns restrictions (the parser tries several spellings and shows nothing on a miss), POST→GET propagation delay (worst case "could not be confirmed", then Check), the minimum Graph version for calling (env-overridable), business-verification / payment prerequisites (surface as 131044 / 138018), and SIP-enabled number behaviour (a warning is shown; 131055 is mapped). Real browser/media validation is likewise deferred.
- **Untested**: the timeout / connect-timeout config values, the Enable/Disable interleave, mobile layout of the new row block (static markup only), and the negative "Check is not disabled" UI assertion has no mutation proof.
- **Restrictions are transient by design (D2)** — they vanish on refresh; `account_settings_update` webhook handling is deferred (D3), so remote status changes are only picked up by Check.

**Explicitly NOT implemented and NOT claimed** — no Step 4+ work exists in the tree: `LeadCallPolicy` and agent call permissions, the answer/reject/hangup endpoints and claim logic, the Reverb call events, the softphone/WebRTC browser layer and browser audio, the missed-call notification, the stale-call reconciliation sweep, the TURN/STUN endpoint, presence, and outbound calling, recording, voicemail, video, transfer and reporting. The call-action client methods exist as capability only and nothing in the application invokes them. The full WhatsApp Calling capability is therefore **not** complete and browser calling is **not** production-ready. `LeadWebhookController`, `WhatsAppSender`, `RecordInboundCall`, `CallRouter`, `WhatsAppCallEvent`, Step 1's schema, `AutoAssignLead` and the existing WhatsApp text/status/attachment paths are unchanged.

**Exact files changed**: modified `app/Livewire/Platform/Clients/LeadSources.php` (+81), `resources/views/livewire/platform/clients/lead-sources.blade.php` (+62), `config/calling.php` (+28); new `app/Services/WhatsAppCallingClient.php`, `app/Support/CallingApiResult.php`, `app/Support/CallingActionResult.php`, `app/Enums/CallingApiError.php`, `app/Enums/CallingControlState.php`, `app/Actions/Platform/ManageWhatsAppCalling.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/WhatsAppCallingClientTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/ManageWhatsAppCallingTest.php`, `tests/Feature/Leads/PlatformWhatsAppCallingControlsTest.php`; and this file. No migration.

**Build Step 3 is CLOSED.** Do not reopen it unless a future change causes a real regression against the behavior above. Build Steps 4 and later are NOT STARTED; each requires its own explicit approval and follows the same process.
