No other audit finding (M6, M9, L2–L4) has been approved for implementation. `App\Livewire\Tenant\Leads\Show::resendMessage()` — duplicate resend / duplicate outbound delivery (selected via the standard finding-selection cycle, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; persisted `messages.resend_message_id` (nullable, self-referencing, `nullOnDelete()`, same shape as `merged_into_lead_id`) + `DB::transaction()` + `Message::lockForUpdate()` enforce exactly one resend per Failed row, checked/set inside the same lock that creates the resend row, mirroring the `LeadCustomFieldDefinition::delete()`/Platform Settings/`toggleLeadStatus()`/`toggleCurrency()` "lock the row before a may-I-do-this check" idiom; genuine two-process PostgreSQL race independently re-verified twice (Implementation and Final Verification, fresh fixture each time) confirms exactly one resend row and zero duplicates; sequential (non-concurrent) duplicate resend also confirmed rejected; `sendMessage()` and `SendOutboundMessage` confirmed unchanged; PHPStan diagnostics on the two changed files unchanged (60/60) versus the pre-existing baseline. `App\Http\Controllers\Api\LeadWebhookController` — inbound webhook message redelivery / duplicate append (selected via the standard finding-selection cycle, not an independently-numbered audit ID) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; a new `alreadyRecordedMessage()` gate, keyed on `tenant_id` + `channel` + `external_message_id` (reusing the existing `messages_external_id_unique` index, no new table/column), checked as the first statement inside each channel's existing per-sender `withDedupeLock()` closure — before `existingLeadForConversation()`, the legacy fallback lookup, `append*Message()`, and `createLead()` — mirroring `processFacebookLeadAd()`'s own `alreadyProcessed` check exactly; null-external-id payloads bypass the gate unchanged, no identifier synthesized; genuine two-process PostgreSQL race independently re-verified twice (Implementation and Final Verification, fresh fixture each time) confirms exactly one Lead/one normalized `Message`/one legacy append with zero duplication even though the pre-existing sender lock alone (confirmed via `Illuminate\Cache\Lock::block()`'s own source) never durably prevented this; duplicate-attachment redelivery confirmed to create no second `LeadAttachment` and dispatch no second `DownloadLeadAttachment` job; `RecordInboundMessage`, `existingLeadForConversation()`, and `VerifyMetaWebhookSignature` confirmed unchanged; PHPStan diagnostics on the touched controller unchanged (0/0) versus the pre-existing baseline. L2 (unbounded logical webhook message/entry processing per request, from the original forensic audit, previously carried forward as not-yet-approved alongside M6/M9/L3/L4) — **CLOSED / FIXED at the application level only**; the original finding's raw HTTP-body-size question is explicitly NOT resolved by this closure and remains a separate infrastructure/operational item outside repository scope; the application-level defect actually closed — unbounded logical message count per request, dynamically measured at 36KB/200 messages producing ~8s processing and 200 real Lead+Message rows — is fixed via a configurable total-per-request logical-message cap (`config/webhooks.php`, default 100, mirroring `Index::MAX_BULK_SELECTION`), checked before any per-message work in `facebookMessenger()`/`instagram()`/`whatsapp()`, counting `messaging[]`/`value.messages[]` across all entries while excluding delivery/read receipts and WhatsApp `statuses[]`; over-limit requests are rejected in full with the existing HTTP 200 acknowledgement preserved, no partial processing; genuine real-Postgres boundary verification independently re-run twice confirms 100 accepted and 101 rejected with no off-by-one; the recently-closed inbound redelivery idempotency gate is unmodified and confirmed still correct in combination with the new cap; M9, L3, and L4 remain open, unaddressed by this closure. M6 (CSV import per-row N+1 / redundant database reads, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; confirmed a genuine performance/scalability and queue-worker resource-contention issue, not a correctness or security one, via real measurement (10 rows = 195 queries, 200 rows = 3,805 queries, linear scaling); fixed by lazily caching `LeadImport`/`Tenant`/`LeadSource` once per `ReadChunk` job (chunk-scoped, not a whole-import shared cache — verified against the installed `maatwebsite/excel` vendor source that each chunk is a separately serialized job) plus reusing already-resolved `source`/`status` relations for scoring via a new opt-in, default-`false` `computeFor($trustLoadedRelations)` parameter used only by CSV import; `TenantUser` deliberately left uncached (no delete guard exists for it, unlike the other three, which are provably undeletable for an import's lifetime) and confirmed still queried once per row at every measured scale including across a chunk boundary; measured reduction 24.1% (N=10) and 26.2% (N=200), independently re-confirmed three times; a real query-count regression test (threshold tightened from `<340` to `<310` after Final Verification identified and closed a blind spot that would have let a `computeFor()`-only rollback at ≈339 queries silently pass) now catches both halves of the optimization being independently reverted; tenant `sharedLock()`, `leadStatuses` resolution, `Lead::nextIdentifier()`, duplicate detection, `LeadImportRow`/`LeadImport` bookkeeping, and the no-`LeadReceived`-per-row design all confirmed unchanged via direct diff inspection; tag/currency/scoring-rule caching, duplicate-detection optimization, identifier-generation optimization, and queue architecture changes were explicitly excluded from scope and not implemented; PHPStan diagnostics on the three touched production files unchanged (0/0) versus the pre-existing baseline. L3 (CSV export `assignedUser` null-safety, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / FALSE POSITIVE**; `$lead->assignedUser->name ?? 'Unassigned'` (`Index.php:1321`) is unconditionally safe under PHP 8.3.31's isset-style `??` semantics, `assignedUser` is already eager-loaded by `export()`'s own `with()` so no lazy load or N+1 can occur (independently confirmed by `BelongsTo::getResults()`'s null-foreign-key short-circuit and a live Postgres reproduction showing 0 additional queries), and a pre-existing test already asserted the exact "Unassigned" output for a null-assignee lead; a live Postgres reproduction additionally confirmed the `nullOnDelete()`-after-user-deletion path resolves identically; no production code, test, or migration was changed. M9 and L4 remain open, unaddressed by this closure. M9 (manual/CSV leads skipping auto-assignment and notification, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / FIXED, narrowed to manual-lead auto-assignment only per approved product decision**; `CreateManualLead::handle()` gained one `if`/`else` branch calling the existing, unmodified `LeadAssignmentService::autoAssign()` when no explicit assignee was supplied, reusing the exact direct-service-call pattern already established for scoring; explicit assignment always wins (verified deterministically, not by chance), `Manual` mode (today's default for every real tenant) continues to no-op via `autoAssign()`'s own pre-existing guard, and Client User self-assignment is unaffected since the new branch is structurally unreachable on that path; no new notification mechanism was added — `LeadAssignedNotification` is simply reachable a second way through the same pre-existing `assign()` → `DB::afterCommit()` chain — and `LeadReceived` remains intentionally undispatched, so the general new-lead notification and real-time board-refresh broadcast remain unreachable exactly as before; CSV auto-assignment/notification, the `lead_distribution_mode`/`auto_distribution_method` admin UI, and any `LeadAssignmentService` redesign were explicitly evaluated and declined for this closure, not silently deferred; `LeadAssignmentService.php`, CSV (`LeadCsvImport.php`/`CreateLeadFromImport.php`), and webhook/email (`LeadWebhookController.php`/`ProcessEmailLeadSource.php`) all confirmed zero diff; focused tests 82/82 passed, broader Leads regression 1033/1036 passed with the same 2 pre-existing `LeadsIndexListenerTest` failures and 1 known IMAP skip re-confirmed via `git stash`; PHPStan on the one touched production file unchanged (0/0) versus the pre-existing baseline. L4 remains open, unaddressed by this closure. L4 (enum-at-migration-time CHECK fragility, from the original forensic audit) — **CLOSED / CONFIRMED ARCHITECTURAL RISK, LOW PRIORITY, MITIGATED**; an already-provisioned PostgreSQL CHECK constraint can drift behind its PHP enum when a case changes without a companion migration — proven real once (`LeadSourceType::CsvImport`, caught only by a manual smoke test, per that migration's own docblock) and structurally invisible to the SQLite-only automated suite by construction, since a freshly-migrated test database always re-derives its constraint from the live enum; current state directly verified as 20/20 enum-backed constraints matching with zero drift; mitigated with a read-only, additive guardrail — `App\Services\EnumConstraintVerifier` (discovers mappings from a fixed 10-model Lead-Management set via `getCasts()`, inspects `pg_constraint`/`pg_get_constraintdef()` directly, matches by word-boundary column search rather than Laravel's default naming convention, compares values as unordered sets) plus `php artisan schema:verify-enum-constraints` (deliberate three-state exit code: 0 clean, 1 drift/error, 2 not-applicable — never a false pass) plus a focused Pest suite (17/17 passed); confirmed read-only by direct grep for every write/DDL verb (zero matches) and by re-confirming the one test-side schema mutation was inside an uncommitted, rolled-back transaction; no migration, enum, model, config, or CI file touched — current CI remains SQLite-only and the existing companion-migration convention is unchanged; native PostgreSQL ENUM and catalog-table conversion were evaluated and explicitly rejected as disproportionate; the guardrail is explicitly scoped to the one defect class (enum-vs-CHECK-constraint drift), not a general schema-drift detector. This was the last of the four (`M6, M9, L2–L4`) originally carried-forward findings — none remain open. SEC-1 (cross-tenant `LeadSource`/`SourceLeads` data exposure, discovered during a separate, fresh, full forensic audit of the module conducted after the above four were closed) — **CLOSED / FIXED, CRITICAL**; `SourceLeads::mount()`'s class-level `viewAny` check replaced with instance-level `Gate::authorize('view', $leadSource)` reusing the already-existing, previously-unused `LeadSourcePolicy::view()`, plus an explicit `tenant_id` predicate added to `baseQuery()` as an independent defense-in-depth guarantee covering `leads()`/`fieldColumns()`/`downloadXlsx()` alike, plus a narrow `orderBy('id')` follow-up in `fieldColumns()` to resolve a patch-caused, `git stash`-confirmed intermittent ordering flake; real end-to-end cross-tenant HTTP reproduction confirmed the exploit before the fix and confirmed HTTP 403 with zero Tenant B data after it; a narrowly-scoped related-pattern search within the Lead Management module found no other candidate; Option D (a deeper, framework-level tenant-aware route-binding fix) was evaluated and explicitly deferred as a separate future consideration, not implemented; `LeadSourcePolicy`, `LeadPolicy`, `BelongsToTenant`, routes, and middleware all confirmed untouched. **This closes only SEC-1 — every other finding from that same fresh forensic audit remains open and unaddressed.** REL-1 (chat webhook per-item exception isolation, from the same fresh forensic audit that surfaced SEC-1) — **CLOSED / FIXED, MEDIUM**; `facebookMessenger()`, `instagram()`, and `whatsapp()` each gained a per-item `try`/`catch (Throwable $e) { Log::error(...) }` (WhatsApp logging to its dedicated `whatsapp` channel with structured context) around that item's own processing, mirroring the already-established per-item isolation convention in `handleFacebookLeadAd()`/`ProcessEmailLeadSource::handle()`; a genuine `LockTimeoutException` was reproduced both as a real two-process wait and, for the committed suite, via a deterministic Mockery facade mock, confirming a middle-item or first-item failure no longer aborts subsequent items and that redelivery of a failed item recovers with no duplicate Lead/Message for already-succeeded items; the separate `changes[]`/leadgen loop and the WhatsApp `statuses[]` reconciliation loop were intentionally left unchanged, and `withDedupeLock()`/`alreadyRecordedMessage()` were not modified. **This closes only REL-1 — every other finding from that same fresh forensic audit (REL-2, DB-1, DB-2, UX-1, PROD-1, SEC-2, QA-1, QA-2, ARCH-1, ARCH-2, INC-1, REL-3, REL-4, OPS-1, OPS-2) remains open and unaddressed.** REL-2 (website-form submission create-time idempotency gap, from the same fresh forensic audit) — **CLOSED / FIXED, MEDIUM**; both `websiteForm()` (legacy) and `websiteFormSubmit()` (builder) gained a `withWebsiteFormIdempotency()` wrapper around the entire logical submission (Lead creation and, for the builder path, consent recording together), keyed by a deterministic canonicalized SHA-256 content fingerprint scoped to tenant + source/form, protected by a short-lived `Cache::lock()` plus a 20-second "recently processed" marker written only after success; a staggered (real, non-simultaneous) duplicate submission was proven to silently create two Lead rows before the fix and exactly one after; a genuinely simultaneous pair was separately found to trigger a distinct, pre-existing PostgreSQL tenant-row lock-upgrade deadlock (`createLead()`'s `sharedLock()` vs. `Lead::nextIdentifier()`'s `lockForUpdate()`) — **that deadlock is NOT part of this closure, remains open and unresolved, and was not touched**, since it is a separate, cross-cutting defect also present in `CreateManualLead`, `CreateLeadFromImport`, and `ProcessEmailLeadSource`; `createLead()` itself, `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`, and the merge workflow are all confirmed unmodified, and no blanket email/phone uniqueness was introduced. **This closes only REL-2 — every other finding from that same fresh forensic audit (DB-1, DB-2, UX-1, PROD-1, SEC-2, QA-1, QA-2, ARCH-1, ARCH-2, INC-1, REL-3, REL-4, OPS-1, OPS-2), and the separate tenant-row deadlock discovered during REL-2's own inspection, remain open and unaddressed.** DEADLOCK-1 (tenant-row shared-lock → exclusive-lock upgrade deadlock, discovered during REL-2's own inspection) — **CLOSED / FIXED, HIGH-PRIORITY RELIABILITY/CONCURRENCY**; `Tenant::sharedLock()` replaced with `Tenant::lockForUpdate()` at all four Lead-creation call sites (`LeadWebhookController::createLead()`, `CreateManualLead::handle()`, `CreateLeadFromImport::handle()`, `ProcessEmailLeadSource::createLeadFromEmail()`) so every concurrent creator requests the same, strongest lock up front instead of two independently-correct coordination mechanisms (TOCTOU-safe pipeline-status reads vs. `Lead::nextIdentifier()`'s own identifier-counter lock) racing to upgrade the same row; `Lead::nextIdentifier()` intentionally left unchanged, its own `lockForUpdate()` now a harmless same-transaction re-lock; real PostgreSQL concurrency re-run repeatedly post-fix (2-way and 5-way webhook across 4 rounds, 5-way manual, mixed-path, different-tenant control) produced **zero** `SQLSTATE 40P01` deadlocks versus the pre-fix baseline's 3-of-5 and 4-of-5 failure rates under identical bursts, with identifier uniqueness and counter integrity independently confirmed; `StatusPipelineLockCoordinationTest.php` (12/12) confirms the readers/writer protocol's statement ordering is unchanged; a CSV-import characterization correction was discovered (`CreateLeadFromImport::handle()` already catches a deadlock per-row via its own pre-existing try/catch) but resulted in **no code change**; the email `\Seen`-before-loss recovery gap and CSV chunk-retry duplicate-safety question remain explicitly open, deferred to their own future investigations; `toggleLeadStatus()` writer-side logic, `LeadAssignmentService`, `LeadMergeService`, SEC-1, REL-1, and REL-2 all confirmed untouched. **This closes only DEADLOCK-1 — every other finding from the fresh forensic audit (DB-1, DB-2, UX-1, PROD-1, SEC-2, QA-1, QA-2, ARCH-1, ARCH-2, INC-1, REL-3, REL-4, OPS-1, OPS-2), plus the email `\Seen` recovery gap and CSV chunk-retry duplicate-safety question, remain open and unaddressed.** EMAIL-LOSS-1 (email `\Seen`-before-durable-failure recovery gap, discovered during DEADLOCK-1's own inspection) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; a new `FailedEmailMessage` model durably records a per-message failure — identified by IMAP UID + UIDVALIDITY as the primary safe replay key (RFC 3501; never UID alone) plus Message-ID as a secondary defense-in-depth check, never sender email alone — **before** `\Seen` is applied, never the reverse, so a transient failure can no longer become permanent silent lead loss; a new `leads:retry-failed-emails` command/scheduler fully reuses `ProcessEmailLeadSource`'s own existing `WithoutOverlapping`/`$tries` machinery (no new locking invented), with a uniform 5-attempt ceiling and 15-minute-per-attempt backoff preserving the pre-existing poison-message protection; a mid-closure Final Verification correctly caught that the first implementation pass conflated an event-dispatch failure with an attachment-only failure under one marker, risking a false `Recovered` state that permanently skipped auto-assignment/scoring/notification for an otherwise-correct Lead — fixed via a new `EmailPartialFailureStage` (Event/Attachments) persisted alongside whether the original operation was a create or an append, so a retry re-fires the event (as the correct `LeadReceived`/`LeadUpdated` class) only when it never dispatched, and never re-fires one that already succeeded; `AutoAssignLead`, `LeadAssignmentService`, `RecomputeLeadScoreOnReceipt`, `SendNewLeadNotification`, and every other event listener confirmed completely untouched, since the fix guarantees exactly-once dispatch rather than making any listener idempotent; a dedicated adversarial falsification pass found no branch where an event could misfire, double-fire, or falsely reach `Recovered`; no real end-to-end IMAP integration test was performed or claimed (`IMAP\Connection` is `final`/internal, constructible only via a genuine `imap_open()`, and no real mailbox was touched) — verified instead by deterministic DB/state-level tests plus repeated direct code trace; the multi-attachment mid-loop duplicate-`LeadAttachment` risk, the retry record-write-failure edge case, the IMAP UIDVALIDITY-reconfiguration edge case, CSV import's chunk-retry duplicate-safety question, and the Messenger/Instagram/WhatsApp/Facebook-Lead-Ads always-200 same-pattern risk all remain explicitly open, deferred, untouched. **This closes only EMAIL-LOSS-1 — every other finding from the fresh forensic audit (DB-1, DB-2, UX-1, PROD-1, SEC-2, QA-1, QA-2, ARCH-1, ARCH-2, INC-1, REL-3, REL-4, OPS-1, OPS-2), plus CSV import's chunk-retry duplicate-safety question and the always-200 same-pattern risk named above, remain open and unaddressed.** DB-1 (Lead hard deletion had no durable deletion audit, from the fresh forensic audit that also surfaced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; hard-delete/no-restore remains the unchanged product decision; a new `LeadDeletionAudit` model, written by the single shared `App\Actions\Tenant\RecordLeadDeletionAudit` primitive inside the SAME transaction as, and strictly before, `Lead::delete()` across all three delete call sites (`Index::delete()`, `Index::performBulkDelete()`, `Show::delete()`), is the sole durable record of a deleted Lead's identity, fail-closed (no try/catch — any audit-write or consent-snapshot failure rolls back the whole transaction, Lead survives); `lead_consents`/`lead_consent_events` moved from `cascadeOnDelete()` to nullable `lead_id` + `nullOnDelete()` with new `lead_identifier`/`lead_name` snapshot columns populated only at deletion time, so consent history survives a Lead's deletion, detached and independently identifiable; a concurrency blocker found during this finding's own first Final Verification (two concurrent delete requests for the same Lead could both durably write an audit row for one physical deletion) was closed via its own full Architecture Brief → Approval → Implementation → Testing → Final Verification → Approval cycle — `RecordLeadDeletionAudit::handle()` now re-fetches the Lead with `lockForUpdate()` inside the transaction and no-ops (no audit, no side effect) if it is already gone, verified via a real two-independent-PostgreSQL-session `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` exclusivity test plus 8/8 real two-process concurrent-deletion races producing exactly one audit row, one physical deletion, and zero deadlocks each round; `LeadObserver`, `LeadPolicy`, and `LeadMergeService` confirmed completely untouched; historical (pre-closure) deletions are explicitly not reconstructed, and no legal/regulatory/retention compliance claim is made beyond the approved product behavior; `lead_duplicate_links` cascade loss remains accepted with no tombstone; bulk delete's `$succeeded` counter and a narrow, pre-existing `LeadMergeService` concurrent-delete blind spot remain explicitly unfixed, out-of-scope residual risks. **This closes only DB-1 — every other finding from the fresh forensic audit (DB-2, UX-1, PROD-1, SEC-2, QA-1, QA-2, ARCH-1, ARCH-2, INC-1, REL-3, REL-4, OPS-1, OPS-2) remains open and unaddressed.** DE1 (LeadSource with inbound Conversation history permanently undeletable, first surfaced during C1's own inspection) — **CLOSED / WORKING AS INTENDED**; re-verified fresh at DB-1's own closing HEAD that the `conversations.lead_source_id` `restrictOnDelete()` constraint and C1's application-level guard still safely and permanently block deletion with no data loss, and that `LeadSource.is_active`/`toggleActive()` is a functionally real retirement mechanism (confirmed via the webhook resolver's `is_active` gate, not assumed) that leaves historical Conversation/Message data fully accessible; approved product decision: this is intentional behavior, not a defect — deactivation, not deletion, is the correct retirement path for such a source, applying to existing and future sources alike, with no retroactive migration; no archive entity, detach mechanism, purge workflow, or Conversation/Message/LeadSource schema or code change was made — this is a documentation-only closure; `PlatformLeadSourceDeletionTest.php` re-run unmodified, 11/11 passed; C1 and DB-1 both confirmed untouched and not reopened. SEC-2 (legacy website-form webhook had no rate limiting, from the fresh forensic audit that also surfaced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; two new named limiters (`legacy-website-form-per-source-ip`, 20/min; `legacy-website-form-per-source`, 60/min), both keyed on `LeadSource.id` — never the API key, never a form token, since none exists on this route — attached to the legacy route only via a new, narrowly-scoped `App\Http\Middleware\ThrottleRequestsFailOpen` rather than Laravel's stock `throttle:` middleware, which has no fail-open capability at all (confirmed by direct source read); a limiter-backend failure fails open with a warning log, an unregistered-limiter configuration mistake fails open with an error log, and every other exception on the request (authentication, REL-2, Lead creation, event dispatch) remains completely outside the fail-open try/catch and is never swallowed; rate limiting runs as route middleware, before the controller, before REL-2; REL-2 itself and the builder route's own limiters are both confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged; 13 new focused tests plus 125/125 on the direct-neighbor suite (including every pre-existing builder-throttle test, unmodified) passed, broader Leads regression 1085/1086 with the same 1 known pre-existing skip, Broadcasting's 2 known pre-existing failures unchanged; 0 net-new PHPStan diagnostics confirmed via `git stash`; no migration, no `LeadSource` schema change, no trusted-source tier/exemption/admin UI introduced, per the approved product decision. DB-2 (no DB-level backstop for concurrent duplicate-email CSV creation, from the fresh forensic audit that also surfaced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2, previously flagged but not reproduced by the original audit) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; the confirmed defect was a TOCTOU race in `CreateLeadFromImport::processRow()`'s own pre-lock duplicate check, reproduced 6/6 via a real two-process PostgreSQL race; fixed by re-running the exact same, unmodified `checkExistingLeadDuplicate()` helper a second time inside the method's existing `DB::transaction()`, immediately after DEADLOCK-1's own `Tenant::lockForUpdate()` — no new lock, no unique constraint, no schema change; real PostgreSQL concurrency re-verified 12/12 rounds across same-email N-way races (N=4 and N=8), case/format-varied races, and concurrent distinct-email imports, with zero false positives, exceptions, or deadlocks; a query-budget regression this fix necessarily causes (+1 query per email-only CSV row) was reconciled by updating `LeadCsvImportEngineTest.php`'s pre-existing M6 query-count guard from a measured 299/threshold-310 to a measured 319/threshold-330, preserving the guard's original margins and discriminating power exactly; `LeadDuplicateDetectionService`, `LeadMergeService`, `CreateManualLead`, `LeadWebhookController`, and `ProcessEmailLeadSource` all confirmed untouched; INC-1 remains separate and unresolved. REL-3 (unbounded in-PHP firstResponseSummary aggregation "bounded only by the UI 92-day cap," from the fresh forensic audit that also surfaced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; confirmed the audit's literal framing was inaccurate (the 92-day cap was already server-enforced by the Livewire caller, not merely UI-side) but the underlying concern was real: `LeadReportingService::firstResponseSummary()` itself had no independent range enforcement and executed an unbounded query when called directly, proven empirically with a real 10-year range; fixed by adding a canonical `MAX_RANGE_DAYS = 92` constant on the service (now the sole source of truth, referenced by `Index.php` instead of a duplicate private copy) and a guard at the top of `firstResponseSummary()` throwing a new, minimal `LeadReportingRangeException` before any query runs; a mid-implementation correctness catch found and fixed a formula error (the service receives an exclusive `$to`, so its span check intentionally omits the `+1` `rangeError()`'s own raw-date formula uses — using `+1` here would have wrongly rejected the currently-valid exact-92-day selection, caught and corrected before Final Verification); real direct-service and boundary verification confirmed exactly-92-days accepted, 93 days rejected, `from > to` rejected, and zero database queries execute for a rejected direct call; existing Livewire behavior, messages, query, and aggregation all confirmed unchanged; no row-count cap, no SQL aggregation redesign, no change to any other `LeadReportingService` method, no schema change; REL-4 untouched; >92-day reporting remains unsupported and undecided, not newly enabled by this closure. REL-4 (Builder-level each()/OFFSET pagination silently skipping a qualifying row after a concurrent delete or WHERE-disqualifying mutation between batches, from the fresh forensic audit that also surfaced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3) — **CLOSED / FIXED**; confirmed via direct Laravel v13.29.0 source inspection (each() delegates to chunk(), a pure page-number-derived OFFSET with no row-cursor) and a real reproduction against this repository's own Lead model; the three confirmed-realistic sites — `SendLeadAlertNotifications::sweep()`, `NurtureInactiveLeads::sweepTenant()`, `SendTaskAlertNotifications::sweep()` — had their Lead/Task-level `each()` replaced with `chunkById(200, ...)`, the same ID-cursor pattern already proven safe elsewhere in this codebase (`RecalculateLeadScores.php`, `DetectDuplicateLeads.php`, `Leads\Index::export()`); every existing filter, tenant scope, eager-load, and callback side effect preserved unchanged, only the pagination mechanism itself changed; the three `Tenant::query()->each()` iterations in the same files (no `Tenant::delete()` feature exists) and `SendNewLeadNotification` (realistically never reaches a second `each()` page) were deliberately left unconverted per approved Option A scope, not overlooked; observability additions were explicitly kept out of scope; 9 new regression tests directly reproduce and disprove the skip under both deletion and disqualifying-update scenarios; 0 net-new PHPStan diagnostics (one pre-existing, unrelated diagnostic incidentally resolved by no longer calling each() at all). QA-1 (missing assignment-denial authorization test coverage, from the fresh forensic audit that also surfaced SEC-1/REL-1/REL-2/DEADLOCK-1/EMAIL-LOSS-1/DB-1/DE1/SEC-2/DB-2/REL-3/REL-4) — **CLOSED / TEST-ONLY**; confirmed production authorization was already correctly enforced at write time in all three assignment-mutating entry points (`Show::assign()`, `Index::applyBulkAssign()`, `Index::bulkUnassign()`, all independently re-checking `LeadPolicy::assign()` rather than trusting earlier UI-hiding guards) — this was a regression-detection gap, not a live defect; added direct write-time denial tests for all three entry points plus one direct Gate-level cross-tenant `LeadPolicy::assign()` regression test (a `Show::assign()`-based cross-tenant Livewire test was attempted and empirically failed, since `Show::mount()`'s own `view` authorization shares and is reached before `assign()`'s identical tenant condition — reported and replaced with the Gate-level alternative, not bypassed); renamed the misleading `'bulk assign is Client-Admin-only'` test (which only exercised the modal-open UI guard, never the actual write-time check) to accurately describe what it verifies, preserving its assertions unchanged; zero production-code change; `LeadPolicy.php`, `Leads/Show.php`, `Leads/Index.php`, `LeadAssignmentService.php` confirmed untouched; 0 net-new PHPStan diagnostics. QA-2 (`Index::applyBulkAssign()` permission-denial branch lacked direct denied-actor test coverage — a distinct finding from QA-1, scoped specifically to `applyBulkAssign()`) — **CLOSED / ALREADY FIXED, ABSORBED BY QA-1**; QA-1's own approved remediation had, at implementation time, already added the exact required test (`'a non-admin is denied applyBulkAssign() at the write-time Gate check, not just the closed modal'`, `tests/Feature/Leads/BulkActionsTest.php`, introduced by commit `63dd8bf`) — a dedicated reconciliation pass independently verified every literal element of QA-2's requirement against that test's actual body (denied actor, direct invocation with no reliance on `openBulkAssignModal()`, the write-time `Gate::denies()` branch, unchanged `assigned_to`, absent side effects) rather than assuming coverage from the test's existence; no new code or test was added, since a duplicate would have provided no additional coverage; `LeadPolicy.php`/`Leads/Show.php`/`Leads/Index.php`/`LeadAssignmentService.php` and QA-1's own test files all confirmed untouched by this closure.
